# Rendering & themes

Reference for `render_quote.py`: layout, typography, and the per-theme design
notes, plus the bundled-font table. `CLAUDE.md` keeps the theme checklist and
conventions and links here; other sections named below ("Baked Quote
Database", "Runtime Loop", …) are in [`pipeline.md`](pipeline.md) and
[`runtime.md`](runtime.md).

**Adding a theme?** Read the checklist in `CLAUDE.md` ("Rendering") first.

## Bundled fonts

Every face the renderer sets ships under `idle_hours/fonts/` as package data, one directory per family with its licence beside it (`OFL.txt`, or `LICENSE.txt` for the Apache-licensed Special Elite and Permanent Marker). The table below is generated by `scripts/generate_font_table.py` from `THEME_FONTS` and from each theme module's source. **Sets it** means the face comes first in one of the theme's candidate chains, so it is what the panel shows. **Fallback only** means the theme lists it after its own face and loads it only if that face is missing. The top-level Playfair files are the last bundled fallback of nearly every chain, so they are listed only where a theme sets them. `tests/test_font_table.py` fails when the table is stale or when no theme loads a bundled directory. Why each theme chose its face is in that theme's paragraph further down.

## Rendering (`render_quote.py`)

Imports `pick_quote` in-process (`pick_quote_module.select_quote`) and lays out an 800×480 RGB PNG — no subprocess, no stdout-JSON contract. Key details:

- **Palette.** Colors are drawn from the 6-color Spectra 6 palette (white/black/red/yellow/blue/green) and the final image is re-snapped to that palette via `snap_image_to_palette` so the Inky dithering stays predictable.
- **Themes.** The `THEMES` dict defines one color set per theme, cycle-ordered by the `THEME_ORDER` tuple. Every theme colour must stay on the Spectra 6 palette (enforced by a test).

  **Paragraph order.** The order below is close to but not identical to `THEME_ORDER` — `fillmore` clusters at the end with the other "fill gaps in the rotation's visual spread" additions rather than being interleaved at its cycle position.

  **Custom frames.** Many themes bypass the literary layout entirely and own their whole composition, dispatching from `render` into a `render_<theme>_frame`; `CUSTOM_FRAME_THEMES` in `tests/test_theme_decoration.py` is the canonical list and `TestThemeDecorationRegistry` cross-checks it against `render`'s dispatch ladder. Per-theme paragraphs deliberately carry no ordinal ("the fourteenth theme to bypass the literary layout"): that is a fact about the whole set, and hand-maintained in a dozen places it went stale and produced a duplicate. For the same reason no doc states how many themes there are: read `len(THEME_ORDER)` or `len(CUSTOM_FRAME_THEMES)` instead.

  **Selection.** `--theme` selects one (plus `auto`, which picks day/night by wall-clock hour, and `random`, which rerolls the theme each time the displayed quote changes); `run_clock.py` forwards it via `--theme`. Button B advances through `THEME_ORDER` one step per press; the web UI dropdown jumps directly.

  **Adding a new theme.** Extend `render_quote.THEMES`, add to `THEME_ORDER`, add a `render_quote.THEME_FONTS` entry (see "Fonts" below — missing themes silently fall back to the Playfair chain, defeating the point of per-theme typography), and add a `display_inky.THEME_SATURATION` entry. `THEME_ORDER` lives in the Pillow-free `theme_names.py`, and `run_clock.py`'s `--theme` choices read it, so there is no second list to update.
  - `default` (white/black/red, Playfair Display).
  - `dark` (black/white/yellow, Playfair Display).
  - `newsprint` (white/black/no-accent, Old Standard TT) — bold-weight differentiation only; broadsheet typography lives entirely in ink weight, not chromatic contrast.

    **Frame.** `draw_newsprint_border` draws a Scotch-rule frame: thick outer rule + hairline inner rule, no corner accents.

    **Paper.** The Layer 0 ground pairs the original 12.5% black halftone with a faint 12.5% sepia-foxing layer — 1 red + 1 green pixel per 4×4 Bayer tile at cell values 2 and 3, diagonally ~2.8 px apart — which blends at panel distance into the pale rust-brown tint real archival newspaper paper develops as the lignin oxidises under light. The matched-phrase / body / accent THEMES slots stay black-on-black so `test_newsprint_theme_has_no_colour_accent` still passes — the rust-brown lives entirely on the *paper*, not the typography.

    **Broadsheet furniture.** Inside the Scotch frame, all in ink (black) only so the no-chromatic-accent identity holds: a **masthead nameplate rule** (a thick+thin Scotch pair spanning the inner frame at y≈38) with two short **column-rule ticks** descending from it (the vertical hairlines that separate broadsheet columns, stopped above the text at y≤68), a short centred **folio rule** at the foot (clear of the bottom-left attribution column), and a small filled-black **printer's-diamond dingbat** centred on both the masthead and folio rules. All positions clear the text block (the quote never starts before y=72).
  - `nightvision` (black/green/yellow, Space Mono) — retro-terminal / weapons-HUD feel.

    **Text inks.** The body / attribution / oversized-quote-mark glyphs are stippled with white in a 50/50 Bayer pattern via `_draw_text_body` + `draw_text_dithered`, so Spectra-6 saturated green lifts to a perceived mint that reads at panel distance instead of the dimmer pure-green ink the panel produces solid. The matched-phrase yellow accent is rerouted in `_draw_text_body` to a 5/8:3/8 Y+G LIME stipple (yellow-biased green via the same Bayer threshold deco's tangerine uses), so the phrase reads as the brighter neon "tactical readout" glow of a real HUD warning rather than flat alert-flag yellow.

    **HUD frame.** `draw_nightvision_border` draws four L-shaped green corner brackets with NO continuous outer frame between them — the bracket-only composition is the signature camera-viewfinder / weapons-HUD motif — plus a bottom-margin **bearing-scale ruler** (graduated green ticks with a taller mark every fourth division and a yellow centre-index caret, the heading/distance tape of a real readout) and three-rung **rangefinder ladder notches** stepping inward along each bottom corner bracket's vertical arm. The brackets stay solid green (decorative silhouettes would fragment under stippling), but the faint scanlines get a sage post-pass — bbox-flipping ~25% of the painted green to white per Bayer threshold 4 — so they read as ambient W+G 1:3 ground glow rather than crisp bright-green CRT lines.

    **Debug band.** All the added HUD furniture is bottom-weighted so the y=14-29 debug-banner band stays clear, which is why `nightvision` stays absent from `_DEBUG_LABEL_RIGHT_INSET`.
  - `gothic` (black-ground/white-body/red-accent, UnifrakturMaguntia) — the blackletter sets *both* the matched-phrase bold and the oversized quote marks.

    **Matched phrase.** Short matched phrases like "half past two" render in dramatic blackletter, sitting in the body like a chapter heading. The red is rerouted in `_draw_text_body` to a 50/50 R+Y **amber** stipple — the recipe the `diags` synth band labels "amber" — so the phrase reads as warm candle-flame against the black cathedral ground and lifts clear of the red border ornaments instead of sharing their ink. On the plate the phrase runs 54% red / 46% yellow, the checkerboard against glyph shapes.

    **Border.** `draw_gothic_border` draws a double rule (red outer + white inner) with red-and-white quatrefoils — four small red lobes around a tiny white centre dot — at the four outer corners (the four-lobed Gothic-tracery motif of cathedral rose windows and printed-book ornaments), plus small cream (Y+W) diamond ornaments centred on each mid-edge as chapter-divider accents.

    **Finials.** Red **trefoil finials** (a three-lobed Gothic-tracery clover, each lobe a red disc with a white centre dot) are centred in the top and bottom margins, painted in solid rubric red rather than the corners' maroon recipe so they read on the open black field, tying the head/foot of the page back to the corner quatrefoils. Both are centred horizontally, so the top one clears the right-aligned `DEBUG MODE` banner and the foot one clears the bottom-left attribution.
  - `bauhaus` (white/black/blue-accent + red-ornaments, Jost geometric sans) — three primaries simultaneously.

    **Corners.** `draw_bauhaus_border` draws a thin black outer rectangle with red-circle (TL) / blue-square (TR) / yellow-triangle (BL) / red-circle (BR) corner accents. The BL triangle paints in yellow rather than a second blue so all three Bauhaus primaries (red + blue + yellow) appear on the page alongside the black outer frame.

    **Poster composition.** Concentric outline rings (in the frame colour) sweep around the two corner discs as the quarter-arc "target" geometry of a Kandinsky concentric-circle study. Two off-centre primary semicircles bulge inward from the top (blue) and bottom (yellow) frames as the playful edge punctuation of a Bauhaus poster — deliberately set at x=0.68w / 0.32w (never the frame midpoints) so the composition stays asymmetric and the sampled top/bottom frame-midpoint pixels keep their frame colour. Two floating primary elements (a small red square on the left margin, a small blue disc on the right) drift in the side margins at y=0.34h / 0.66h, echoing the corner shapes while staying off the side-frame midpoints.

    **Accent.** The matched-phrase blue accent is rerouted in `_draw_text_body` to a 50/50 B+K navy stipple, giving the phrase tighter contrast against the yellow triangle than the body-vs-accent palette would otherwise produce.

    **Border dispatch (all border themes).** The themes with borders — atomic / bauhaus / comic / dispatch / gothic / newsprint / nightvision (and the other later-added border painters) — are dispatched through their `BorderSpec` (`registry.BORDER_SPECS`): `render` lays the quote out, then paints the border once in the knockout pass (a spec with `paints_twice` is also painted on the bare page first, because those painters do not reproduce their own output and the panel shows the composite — issue #361); `render_source_card` paints only on the bare page. Several paint in the top-right corner (bauhaus / gothic and others), so the debug-mode "DEBUG MODE" banner shifts inward for those by the spec's `debug_label_inset` — set it when adding another theme-specific TR graphic. Dispatch's TR rubber-stamp graphic sits below the label band and atomic's atom symbol is centred horizontally, so both deliberately set none.
  - `comic` (yellow-ground/black-body/red-accent, Bangers comic-book display) — `draw_comic_corner_stripes` lays a full comic-page composition over the empty yellow ground.

    **Ben-Day dots.** An evenly-gridded triangle of small red dots fills the top-left corner (the Lichtenstein / four-colour-print texture), inset ≥26 px from both edges so the panel-gutter corner — and the `(15, 15)` / `(12, 12)` / `(20, 20)` / `(36, 56)` pixels the cross-theme gating tests sample — stays clean yellow page_bg, diagonally counterweighting the bottom-right stripes.

    **Racing stripes.** 45° stripes cycling through blue / green / red / black, masked to a right-triangle pinned to the bottom-right corner of the canvas — the hypotenuse spans the lower-right quadrant from `(width/2, height)` up to `(width, height/2)`, so bands fan out from the corner without obscuring the quote body.

    **Panel border.** A heavy black border at inset 2 / width 4 hugging the canvas edge — the gutter line every comic panel is framed by — painted last so it sits over both the dots and the stripes, with the stroke kept at x/y 2–5 so it clears all the frame-edge gating sample points.

    **Palette source.** The stripe palette is hardcoded at module scope (`_COMIC_STRIPE_PALETTE`) since the cool blue/green half of the chevron isn't reachable from the comic theme dict's two non-bg accents — an exception to the "borders pull from `colors`" pattern that would otherwise force a THEMES-schema extension and re-pin every cross-theme invariant test. The Ben-Day dots and panel border DO pull from `colors` (accent / text), defaulting to the comic red/black via `.get()` so the direct `draw_comic_corner_stripes(img, {"page_bg": ...})` unit-test calls — which pass only the page_bg — still render the full panel.
  - `dispatch` (white/black/red, Special Elite slab-mono typewriter face) — vintage office / field-report / dossier register. Same palette as `default`, but the typewriter face plus the dossier graphics give it a completely different silhouette.

    **Paper.** `draw_dispatch_border` paints a sparse 1-in-8 yellow-on-white Bayer cream wash as Layer 0 across the page ground (per `BAYER_4x4[y%4][x%4] < 2` — the same Bayer threshold `newsprint`'s halftone uses but flipped for `page_bg→yellow`, so the page reads as a faint cream/vellum tone, the documented Y+W cream recipe), plus a thin black outer frame.

    **Perforations.** Two columns of small tractor-feed perforation circles run down the side margins (continuous-feed dot-matrix sprocket holes). Every other pair flips from solid black to a sepia stipple via the documented R+G sentinel-paint-then-bbox-post-pass pattern, reading as the rust-brown "carbon-paper bleed" real continuous-feed forms accumulate where the carbon backing oxidises through the sprocket holes.

    **Rubber stamp.** A maroon imprint in the upper right inside the frame — two concentric ellipse outlines plus four short diagonal hatch lines, painted in red as a sentinel and then bbox-post-passed to flip half to black per `(x+y)&1` parity (the documented R+K maroon recipe) — so the stamp reads as the aged-ink oxblood of a real archival stamp rather than fire-engine red.

    **Dossier furniture.** Two **binder filing-punch holes** (thin black ring outlines centred in the top margin at y≈40) and a typed **"— FILE COPY —" footer** (the theme's own Special Elite face, centred in the bottom margin), both in black ink so the dossier identity stays monochrome-plus-stamp.

    **Debug band.** The stamp sits at y≈40–70; the punch-holes and footer are centred horizontally (clear of the side perforation columns and the bottom-left attribution), all below or centred relative to the `DEBUG MODE` banner band, so dispatch is intentionally absent from `_DEBUG_LABEL_RIGHT_INSET`.
  - `atomic` (green-ground/black-body/red-accent, the chunky 1950s Atomic Age display face) — the only theme to claim Spectra 6's flat green as a *page background*; reads as a vintage atomic-age advertisement at a glance.

    **Frame and atom.** `draw_atomic_border` draws a rounded-corner red outer frame (Googie streamlined-modern curves) and a centred atom symbol at the top of the page: three rotated red ellipse "orbits" at 0° / 60° / 120° plus a small filled red nucleus. PIL's `ellipse` doesn't accept rotation, so each orbit is drawn as a 64-point polygon-line approximation rotated through the standard 2×2 cosine/sine matrix.

    **Starbursts.** Twin tangerine starbursts at the mid-edges — eight rays radiating from a small filled dot, the atomic-energy spark of mid-century diner / motel signage. Each ray is painted in red as a sentinel ink, then a per-starburst bbox post-pass Bayer-flips ~3/8 of the red pixels to yellow at threshold 6/16 — the documented R+Y 5/8:3/8 tangerine recipe `deco` uses for its matched phrase. The atom stays solid red, so the contrast reads as solid orbits against warm-stippled rays.

    **Boomerang.** An atomic **boomerang** centred in the bottom margin — the Googie / Formica kidney-boomerang the era stamped on diner tables and motel signs, a chevron-shaped filled polygon with a small orbiting-electron dot off one tip — painted in red then bbox-post-passed through the same R+Y 5/8:3/8 tangerine recipe as the starburst rays.

    **Debug band.** The atom is centred horizontally so it doesn't conflict with the right-aligned `DEBUG MODE` banner, and the boomerang sits at the bottom centre clear of the bottom-left attribution; atomic is intentionally absent from `_DEBUG_LABEL_RIGHT_INSET`.
  - `marker` (white/black/blue-accent, Permanent Marker hand-drawn) — a "use the full capabilities of the display" brief: `draw_marker_border` is a fridge-doodle frame that lights up every non-white spot colour the Spectra 6 panel can render.

    **Perimeter.** A thick 3 px marker-dash perimeter cycles through the five-colour palette so each edge picks up roughly one full rotation of the cycle. Corner asterisks are coloured red TL / blue TR / green BL / yellow BR for a "marker pot" register rather than a single-Sharpie doodle.

    **Mid-edge dots and twinkles.** A left mid-edge mint highlighter dot (G+W 1:1 bbox post-pass via the documented two-ink recipe `nightvision`'s body uses) and a right mid-edge violet overlap dot (R+B 1:1) balance the otherwise-stranded yellow + green pixels from the perimeter cycle. Two doodle **"twinkle" sparkles** (a tapered four-point star with a tiny companion mark, the little stars a kid scatters around a fridge drawing) sit in the top-centre (red) and bottom-centre (blue) margins, pulling two more palette inks into the head/foot space.

    **Debug band.** The TR corner asterisk overlaps the y=14-29 banner band, so `marker` carries an entry in `_DEBUG_LABEL_RIGHT_INSET`; the top twinkle sits at x=width//2-70, left of the banner, so it needs no further inset adjustment.
  - `saloon` (white/black/red, Rye wood-engraved slab serif) — 19th-century saloon poster / "WANTED" broadside. Same palette as `default` / `dispatch` / `roman`, but a `draw_saloon_border` decoration heavy enough to read as hand-printed Western broadside at a glance.

    **Foxing.** Sparse red foxing speckles across the full page: a deterministic seeded `_SALOON_FOXING` stipple of ~360 single-pixel + 3×3 spots, painted in red then `(px+py)&1` post-passed to flip half to green per parity — the documented R+G 1:1 brown/sepia recipe — for aged-paper rust-brown foxing.

    **Frame and ornaments.** A doubled wanted-poster frame whose outer 3 px red rule similarly post-passes to read as rusted iron; top + bottom decorative banner bands with mirrored ornaments; corner fleurons; mid-edge red diamonds; and side-margin drop-pendant diamond chains — two diminishing red diamonds joined by a thin tick, hung inward from each left/right mid-edge diamond into the otherwise-empty tall side margins, the "drop pendant" terminal a broadside printer ran down from a margin ornament.
  - `roman` (white/black/red, Cinzel Decorative chiselled serif) — Roman lapidary inscription.

    **Register.** The red accent is *rubrum*, the red lead pigment Roman carvers painted into engraved grooves to make inscriptions legible from a distance; Cinzel Decorative is a digital revival of Trajan's Column (113 AD), the canonical reference for capitalis monumentalis.

    **Stone-slab border.** `draw_roman_border` lays the rest of the vocabulary on top of the page: scattered limestone-grain speckles, a `tabula ansata` (Roman votive tablet) silhouette with two trapezoidal "dovetail" handles protruding from the left and right mid-edges, an SPQR cartouche at the top centre with interpunct dot separators between the letters, mirrored mid-edge interpunct dots, and a centred laurel sprig at the bottom — leaf colour painted in yellow then bbox-post-passed to olive per the documented Y+G 1:1 recipe, the canonical Mediterranean laurel.

    **Corner stops.** Small red **carved corner stops** — a right-triangle tucked into each inner-channel corner pointing inward along the diagonal, the lapidary corner terminal a stonemason cut where two channel rules meet (the rubrum-ink equivalent of a printer's corner bracket), sitting in the corner gutters clear of the body text.
  - `alchemy` (yellow/black/red+blue, IM Fell English body + MedievalSharp matched phrase + ornament) — parchment grimoire register, the aged-yellow ground a real alchemical manuscript takes on after four centuries of candlelight and iron-gall ink.

    **Type.** IM Fell English is Igino Marini's revival of John Fell's 17th-century Oxford-press types, the same Oxford types that printed actual alchemical treatises (Ashmole's *Theatrum Chemicum Britannicum*, Newton's manuscript translations of Flamel). The matched phrase is set in MedievalSharp with a red rubricated accent, the way medieval scribes flagged the operative phrase of a spell; blue Hermetic ornaments form the oversized quote marks.

    **Matched phrase.** It paints **solid red** — a rubric, the way a scribe actually flagged the operative words.

    **Transmutation circle.** `draw_alchemy_border` draws a circle whose inner figure (rings, incantation ticks, inscribed pentagram and pentagon) paints through a sentinel and is gated to a 50% (x+y) parity stipple of blue on the parchment, so each hairline reads as a faint construction line behind the black serif instead of a solid blue rule slicing through every line of the quote. The red outer rule and corner sigils stay solid, since they frame the text rather than crossing it.

    **Elements.** The circle's four classical-element triangles each paint a different documented two-ink elemental recipe: 🜂 Fire (R+Y 5/8:3/8 tangerine, BL), 🜁 Air (R+B 1:1 purple, TR), 🜃 Earth (Y+G 1:1 olive, TL), 🜄 Water (B+W 1:1 sky blue, alternate TR) — the four classical elements rendered as their canonical synthesised colours.

    **Tried and rejected.** Rerouting the matched phrase to the 50/50 R+B purple stipple: it shredded MedievalSharp's thin strokes into a dotted smear at body size.
  - `deco` (white/black/red-stippled-to-yellow → perceived orange, Righteous geometric art-deco display sans) — the warm sunburst-and-chevron palette of the 1930s, synthesised because Spectra 6 has no orange ink.

    **Face and accent.** Righteous (Astigmatic, OFL) is single-weight, so the matched phrase reuses Regular and earns differentiation from the synthesised-orange accent alone — a richer variant of the bichrome-ribbon trick comic / dispatch / atomic / marker / saloon use. Both `_draw_text_body` (matched-phrase glyphs) and `draw_deco_border`'s final pass (stepped L-shapes + rising-sun fan) flip half of the painted red pixels to yellow on the same `(x+y) & 1` checkerboard, so body text and decoration land on one shared tangerine tone at panel distance rather than fire-engine red.

    **Frame.** `draw_deco_border` draws a doubled hairline rule at insets 14 and 22 px; three concentric stepped-corner L-shapes per corner — the canonical 1930s skyscraper-steps motif of every cinema marquee and travel poster; and three nested inward-pointing chevrons at each of the left/right mid-edges (echoing the corner steps and filling the side margins; painted in accent so the tangerine pass picks them up).

    **Rising sun.** A centred fan at the top inner edge: a small filled accent dot with five short radial rays fanning upward through the band between the two rules. After the global tangerine pass converts the rays from red to R+Y tangerine, a second cream-gradient post-pass on just the inner ~5 px of the fan band flips remaining red pixels to white per `(x+y)&1` parity, so the inner rays read as bright Y+W cream fading back into the R+Y tangerine at the tips — a true sunburst's central glow rather than a uniform fan.

    **Debug band.** Centred horizontally, and the stepped corners reach x ≤ width-14, so `deco` is deliberately absent from `_DEBUG_LABEL_RIGHT_INSET` for the same reason as `atomic` / `dispatch`.
  - `chalkboard` (black-ground/white-body/yellow-accent, Playwrite GB J Guides — TypeTogether, OFL) — the UK primary-school joined cursive handwriting model *with* the dotted-outline guide letters schoolchildren trace over.

    **Face.** Single-weight (Regular only); the dotted/hollow letterforms themselves are the point, so the matched-phrase role reuses Regular and gains differentiation from the yellow chalk-stick accent. Falls back through DejaVu Sans Italic / Liberation Sans Italic before degrading to the Playfair chain, so a missing install lands on at least a slanted silhouette rather than dropping a cursive theme onto an upright serif.

    **Frame and chalk dust.** `draw_chalkboard_border` draws a doubled white wooden frame (outer 3 px rule at inset 8 + inner 1 px rule at inset 18, with the ~7 px gap between them left unfilled so the black ground reads through as dark wood grain) and a deterministic chalk-dust stipple of single-pixel white dots tucked into the bottom-left corner — pinned to the BL because that's where the chalk tray actually sits on a classroom board.

    **Teacher's marks.** A small green-chalk teacher's `✓` check-mark at the upper-right inner margin (solid Spectra 6 green at y≈50, two short line segments forming the canonical "marked correct" annotation; below the y=14-29 `DEBUG MODE` banner band by construction), with a yellow-chalk **gold-star "well done" sticker** beside it so the graded gesture reads as tick-plus-star.

    **Eraser smudges and practice rule.** Five small coral eraser-smudge dots spaced along the bottom inner edge — each a 3 px filled red circle post-passed with white at 50/50 `(x+y)&1` parity inside its bbox, the documented R+W 1:1 coral-pink recipe — read as the faint pink eraser-stub residue at the bottom of a real classroom board. A top-left **handwriting practice-guide rule** (solid top line + dashed midline + solid baseline in white chalk) is the ruling schoolchildren write between, the defining gesture of the Playwrite GB J Guides face.

    **Debug band.** The TR check-mark + star stay inside x ≤ width-22 and y ≥ 38, and the practice-guide rule is left-anchored in the top margin, so `chalkboard` is intentionally absent from `_DEBUG_LABEL_RIGHT_INSET` — same exemption as `dispatch` / `atomic`.
  - `placard` (white/black/red, Patrick Hand SC — Patrick Wagesreiter, OFL) — a friendly hand-printed face whose small caps for lowercase do almost all the visual work, giving the text the silhouette of hand-lettered shop signage / sandwich-board menus.

    **Face.** Single-weight (Regular only); the matched-phrase role reuses Regular and gains differentiation from the red accent alone, the same trick comic / dispatch / atomic / marker / saloon / deco / chalkboard use. Falls back through DejaVu / Liberation / Noto Sans Bold before degrading to the Playfair chain, so a missing install lands on a chunky display silhouette.

    **Frame.** `draw_placard_border` draws a doubled sign-painter's frame at insets 14 and 18. The outer rule is a 1 px red stroke perimeter-post-passed to flip half its pixels to green on the 50/50 `(x+y)&1` checkerboard — the documented R+G 1:1 rust-brown sepia recipe `saloon`'s foxing uses — reading as the weathered wood of a sun-faded A-frame menu rather than freshly typeset printer-ink black. The inner rule stays solid black, so the colour shift between the two parallel rules reads as "core inked, weathered at edges."

    **Thumbtacks.** Four red filled circles (radius 4) at the inner corners suggest pins holding the sign up. Each tack's red pixels are post-pass-flipped to white on a 50/50 `(x+y)&1` checkerboard inside its own bbox (the documented coral-pink two-ink recipe), so they read as weathered hand-painted red rather than fire-engine vermilion — the exposed corners of a sandwich-board sign are the first thing to fade in the rain. The tacks sit at y ≈ 38 / y ≈ height-38, well below the default debug-banner band (y=14-29), so `placard` is intentionally absent from `_DEBUG_LABEL_RIGHT_INSET` — same exemption as `dispatch`.

    **Dividers and price tags.** **Sign-painter header + footer dividers** — a short centred black rule carrying a small coral diamond (the same R+W recipe), the hand-lettered ornament that tops and tails an A-frame menu board — sit at y≈34 (clear of the text block at y≥72) and centred at the foot (clear of the bottom-left attribution column). **Side-margin hanging price-tag ornaments** drop a short vertical black rule inward from each left/right mid-edge of the inner frame, terminating in a small weathered-coral diamond "ticket" (the same R+W recipe again), the tag a sandwich-board menu hangs in its otherwise-empty side margins.
  - `chanbara` (black-ground/white-body/red-accent, Shojumaru — Astigmatic / Brian J. Bonislawsky, OFL) — a dramatic brush-painted display face by the designer of Righteous and Atomic Age, evoking samurai cinema posters and Japanese woodblock prints.

    **Face.** Single-weight (Regular only); the matched-phrase role reuses Regular and gains differentiation from the red sun-disc accent alone, the same bichrome trick the other display-face themes use. Falls back through heavy DejaVu / Liberation / Noto Sans Bold before degrading to the Playfair chain, so a missing install lands on a heavy display silhouette.

    **Rising sun and seal.** `draw_chanbara_border` anchors a large red filled "rising-sun" disc off-canvas in the bottom-right corner (centre at width+30 / height+30, radius 220 — only the upper-left arc visible, sweeping through the lower-right quadrant; the white quote text on top reads cleanly thanks to the high white-on-red contrast and the palette-snap pass). It is balanced diagonally by a small red artist's-chop seal in the top-left corner: a 28×36 px filled red rectangle with one thin white horizontal "ichi" stroke through its centre, suggesting a Japanese hanko ink seal without committing to specific kanji.

    **Signature column.** A vertical **brush-tick signature column** in the empty left margin — five short uneven sumi strokes + a few ink-spatter flecks, painted red then maroon-post-passed to share the chop's aged-ink register, the hanging signature / date column a samurai-cinema poster runs beside the artist's seal. It is pinned to x≈14-40, y≈196-280, the tall clear band below the oversized opening quote-mark and above the rising-sun arc.

    **Debug band.** The dominant graphic sits in the BL/BR quadrants by design, so the top-right stays clear of the debug banner — `chanbara` is intentionally absent from `_DEBUG_LABEL_RIGHT_INSET`, same exemption as `dispatch` / `atomic` / `placard` / `chalkboard`.
  - `lcars` (black-ground / white-body / yellow-accent, Antonio condensed sans — Vernon Adams, OFL) — a Michael Okuda LCARS interface.

    **Face.** Antonio is the de-facto free LCARS substitute used by Star Trek fan projects: a tall narrow condensed sans whose silhouette echoes Helvetica Compressed / Swiss 911, the original LCARS typeface family. A variable font, with Regular / Bold variation instances pinned explicitly. The yellow-on-black matched-phrase accent is the iconic LCARS button colour and renders solid (no `_draw_text_body` reroute needed).

    **Bars and elbows.** `draw_lcars_border` draws thin tangerine top + bottom horizontal bars wrapping in from the left via quarter-circle elbows, painted in red as a sentinel ink then bbox-post-passed to flip ~3/8 of the red pixels to yellow per Bayer threshold 6/16 (the documented R+Y 5/8:3/8 tangerine recipe shared with `deco` / `atomic`).

    **Rail blocks.** Six stacked blocks flush with the canvas left edge in the canonical Okudagram lavender → tangerine → coral → yellow → red → lavender palette — lavender via the 3-ink R+B+W per-block Bayer partition, coral via the 2-ink R+W 50/50 checkerboard (the recipe of `placard`'s thumbtacks and `chalkboard`'s eraser smudges) — carrying deterministic 4–7-character black alphanumeric labels (the meaningless instrument codes the show used as button labels). A right-aligned "LCARS" wordmark in Antonio Bold is anchored in the top bar.

    **Debug band.** Both bars sit below / above the y=14-29 `DEBUG MODE` banner band by construction, so `lcars` is intentionally absent from `_DEBUG_LABEL_RIGHT_INSET`, same exemption as `dispatch` / `atomic` / `chalkboard` / `chanbara`.
  - `fillmore` (sun-faded-yellow ground / maroon-stippled body / blue matched phrase, Bungee Shade) — a 1960s Fillmore concert poster (Wes Wilson / Victor Moscoso / Stanley Mouse), the visual maximalist of the rotation.

    **Every ink at once.** It deliberately surfaces every Spectra-6 native ink simultaneously across the page: yellow + white in the Layer-0 wash, the blue matched phrase, green / blue / red / yellow corner blob inks, and black in the body's R+K stipple plus Bungee Shade's drop-shadow strokes.

    **Body.** The `text` THEMES slot is the red sentinel that `_draw_text_body` routes through a 50/50 R+K stipple → maroon, subduing the loud pure-red-on-saturated-yellow combination without losing the psychedelic identity. Real Fillmore posters' red ink printed darker onto yellow stock anyway, so the perceived hue is period-authentic.

    **Ground.** `draw_fillmore_border` softens the ground with a sparse 1-in-8 white-on-yellow Layer-0 Bayer wash (`BAYER_4x4[y%4][x%4] < 2`, ~12.5% of yellow pixels flipped to white) — the density of the cream wash `dispatch` uses on its *white* ground, flipped here to desaturate a yellow ground rather than warm a white one. It reads as the partly-sun-faded poster stock a real Fillmore audience saw on a venue door rather than fire-bright Spectra-6 yellow.

    **Corner blobs.** Two free-form 18-point polygon "blob" panels at diagonal corners — a green blob in the top-left at (38, 38) with scale 0.4 and a small red 5-point star inside, mirrored by a blue blob in the bottom-right with a yellow filled inner circle. Each blob is seeded deterministically, so the same seed always produces the same polygon. They are sized to fit inside the ~72 px top / bottom margins that `render` leaves free of body text (`block_top = max(72, ...)`), so they never intrude into the quote block.

    **No frame.** The composition is grounded by the corner blobs rather than a containing rectangle, the way real Fillmore posters compose; fillmore is intentionally absent from `_DEBUG_LABEL_RIGHT_INSET` since the TR is empty by design.
  - `firmament` (navy synthesised ground / white Cardo humanist serif body / yellow→cream matched phrase) — a 17th-century celestial atlas in the lineage of Bayer's *Uranometria* and Cellarius's *Harmonia Macrocosmica*.

    **Navy ground.** The first theme to claim **navy (B+K 1:1) as a page ground**. `page_bg` is stored as solid black; `draw_firmament_border`'s Layer 0 flips half of those black pixels to blue per `(x+y)&1` parity so B+K averages at panel distance into deep navy — the same idempotent shape `fillmore` / `atomic` use for their Layer 0 ground washes. Saturation tier `0.7` in `display_inky.THEME_SATURATION` (dark non-white ground) keeps the matched-phrase cream and the synthesised ornament tones punchy against the navy at panel viewing distance.

    **Milky Way.** The first theme to use **3-ink mixes for decoration outside the diags panel**. Two diagonally-tilted 32-vertex blob silhouettes (rotated off-axis so the bands lie on a diagonal like the real galactic arm, not as axis-aligned blots) fill via a deterministic per-position hash into yellow pin-stars + red warm dust + blue cool dust, with a radial density falloff in each blob's own axis frame: a dense ~9%-painted core fading to a rim where only the brightest 1/40 pin-stars survive and everything else reverts to navy. The band reads as a concentrated star cloud feathering into the night rather than a uniform confetti rectangle. It is hand-rolled because `_fill_swatch_stipple_3way` unconditionally overwrites every rect pixel and would wipe the navy ground if called directly.

    **Matched phrase.** The yellow accent is rerouted in `_draw_text_body` to a 50/50 Y+W cream stipple — the documented two-ink recipe the diags synth band labels "cream" — reading as gilded constellation labels against the navy ground, the ink-on-vellum register of 17th-century celestial atlases.

    **Stars and constellations.** Stars scatter in four magnitude tiers: mag-4 single pixels, mag-3 2x2 clusters, mag-2 4-point compass crosses, mag-1 8-point sparkles with tapered cardinal rays + diagonal accents (~150 deterministic stars from `_FIRMAMENT_STAR_SEED = 0xF18`, restricted to the top and bottom margins so they never overlap the body region). Four named constellations are explicit polylines with Cardo Italic Latin labels — Cassiopeia (W shape, TL), Lyra (parallelogram with Vega as the bright vertex, TR), Crux Australis (the Southern Cross, BL), and Orion's Belt (three stars in a tilted line, BR).

    **Corner ornaments.** A Sol Invictus sun at TL with 16 alternating-length rays and an implied face (two navy-relief eye dots + a 5px smile arc carved into the lower disc via the existing Layer 0 navy stipple parity); a sky-blue (B+W 1:1) crescent moon at TR with two pixel-relief craters; a portolan-chart wind-rose compass at BL with four large filled triangular cardinal points, four shorter inter-cardinal wedges, a yellow pivot diamond, and a Cardo Bold "N" label; and a banded Saturn at BR — the first ornament to **combine two synthesised tones in a single figure** (an R+Y 5/8:3/8 tangerine disc with the `deco` / `atomic` recipe + a G+B 1:1 cyan double-ring system separated by a 1px Cassini-division gap).

    **Ecliptic and hours.** A shallow sky-blue ecliptic arc traces across the top margin from `(40, 70)` to `(width-40, 70)` peaking near `(width//2, 20)` — the path of the sun and planets through the zodiac. Roman-numeral hour markers XII / III / VI / IX sit at the four cardinal page positions in Cardo Italic, evoking an astrolabe rim, the period instrument that married astronomy to timekeeping.

    **Sentinel invariant.** The moon and ecliptic arc paint in off-palette sentinels (`(3, 3, 3)` and `(4, 4, 4)` respectively) so their bbox post-passes can't accidentally flip Layer 0's navy-stipple blue pixels in the surrounding margin to sky-blue — a correctness invariant any future tweak to those ornaments must preserve.

    **Debug band.** The crescent moon centre sits at y=54 (below the y=14-29 DEBUG MODE banner band) and the TR Milky Way blob sits centred near `(width/2 + 30, 52)` (well clear of the right banner edge), so `firmament` is intentionally absent from `_DEBUG_LABEL_RIGHT_INSET` for the same reason as `dispatch` / `atomic` / `placard` / `chalkboard`.

    **Type.** Body in **Cardo** (David Perry, OFL), a Renaissance humanist serif designed for classical scholarship, visually distinct from the EB Garamond of gothic; italic fills the ornament slot for the oversized opening quote mark, carrying the classical / mythological register of constellation names.
  - `astrarium` (cream-washed white / black body / tangerine matched phrase, Cormorant Garamond Regular) — a two-column dashboard inspired by mid-century astronomical instrument panels. A custom-render frame that bypasses the literary layout entirely, dispatching from `render` into its own `render_astrarium_frame` the same way `diags` dispatches into `render_diags_frame`.

    **Dial.** An outer minute-tick ring centred in the left half with four halftone ring quadrants painted via the documented two-ink stipples: tangerine R+Y 5/8:3/8 top-right via `_astrarium_paint_ring_quadrant`, sepia R+G 1:1 bottom-right, teal G+B 1:1 bottom-left, and solid-black top-left with a seeded `_astrarium_paint_constellation_field` speckle drawing 22 white pixels + occasional 4-pointed micro-stars from a `random.Random(2026)` seed for deterministic re-renders.

    **Dial centre.** A central white disc carries a small "LOCAL TIME" header, a tiny tangerine sun glyph (red sentinel + bbox post-pass at Bayer threshold 6/16 → R+Y tangerine), the HH:MM digits in 54-pt Cormorant Bold, and an AM/PM label beneath — all centred via PIL's `anchor="mm"` so digits with descender ink ("2:15") sit at the same baseline-derived y as digits without ("10:00").

    **Quote panel.** The quote sits in the right column (`panel_left = width/2 + 12`, `panel_top = 54`, `panel_bottom = height − 46`) with a small teal-stippled (G+B post-pass to cyan) four-pointed star ornament above, oversized opening/closing quote marks painted via `draw_text_dithered` in R+Y tangerine, and the body fit via `fit_quote(font_max=38, font_min=18, line_height_mult=1.14)`.

    **Byline.** A sans-faced attribution — `META_FONT_BOLD_CANDIDATES` for the author at 13 pt, `META_FONT_CANDIDATES` for the title at 12 pt. The sans face also matches the header date and datum-strip labels, for a consistent "metadata in chrome face, body in serif" editorial convention.

    **Header and datum strip.** `_astrarium_paint_header` paints the top strip (IDLE HOURS // ASTRARIUM brand on the left, SOL/year + day-label stack on the right, hairline dotted rule at y=50). `_astrarium_paint_datum_strip` paints a compact 36-px bottom band carrying two honest signals — solar elevation derived from `sin(π × minute_of_day / 1440) × 75` and lunar phase derived from `day_of_year % 30` — compressed under the left half of the strip with a vertical dotted separator between the two cells and a closing right-edge rule aligned to the dial/quote-panel divider above, so the two vertical guides read as continuous.

    **Dispatch.** Same convention as `diags`: the custom render path replaces the standard layout, so the `--mode debug` overlay does not apply. Unlike `diags`, astrarium IS included in `--theme random` since it still displays a quote — only `diags` (which surfaces swatches instead of a quote) is in `RANDOM_EXCLUDED_THEMES`.

    **Tried and rejected.**
    - **Cormorant Regular for the byline:** hairline serifs at byline sizes broke up after `snap_image_to_palette`.
    - **Cosmetic NEXT HIGH TIDE / TEMPERATURE / ATMOSPHERIC cells** with hardcoded values: removed entirely because the appliance has no thermometer/barometer/tide sensor.
  - `marquee` (black ground / white Cardo Italic body / red matched-phrase / yellow + red bulb-light border) — a 1930s movie-palace facade. A custom-render frame that bypasses the literary layout entirely, dispatching from `render` into its own `render_marquee_frame`.

    **Bulb border.** A solid black "theater facade at night" ground with an alternating yellow + red bulb-light border around the entire perimeter via `_marquee_paint_bulb_border`. Each 5-px-radius filled circle gets a 1-px white highlight pixel near its top-left so the bulb reads as lit glass; without the highlight the bulbs read as flat polka-dots.

    **Feature title.** A small "— NOW SHOWING —" Antonio Bold tagline sits above the **book title as the big chunky Bungee Shade feature-title chrome** at y=112 via `_marquee_paint_feature_title`: uppercased per marquee convention, auto-shrunk through 72→32pt to fit single-line, and wrapped onto two lines at the nearest space-character to the midpoint when even 32pt overflows the canvas width, with a fresh 54→24pt re-fit on the wrapped lines. A decorative double-rule divider at y=180 (thick yellow + thin red rules with a centred red diamond — a vintage proscenium / poster divider) follows.

    **Body and credit.** The literary quote body is white Cardo Italic with a solid red matched-phrase accent, fitted via `fit_quote(font_max=30, font_min=18, line_height_mult=1.22)`. A `WRITTEN BY [AUTHOR]` credit chrome (yellow Antonio Bold label + white Cardo Italic name) and a "— ONE NIGHT ONLY —" Antonio Bold tagline sit just above the bottom bulbs.

    **No digital time.** **The digital HH:MM is deliberately never surfaced** — the matched-phrase red highlight in the body carries the time signal, and a parallel digital clock would defeat the point of a quote-based fuzzy clock. `time_str` is retained in `render_marquee_frame`'s signature for dispatch uniformity with the other custom-render painters but is `del`-asserted at function entry and unused thereafter. The fallback chain when `quote_row['title']` is missing is author (uppercased) → the literal brand string `"IDLE HOURS"` — deliberately never the time.

    **Scale discipline.** Small-chrome labels (NOW SHOWING / ONE NIGHT ONLY / WRITTEN BY) use Antonio Bold at 12-14pt rather than Bungee Shade — Bungee Shade's 3D-blocked silhouette is gorgeous at the 72pt-and-down feature-title sizes but muddies into noise at small label sizes. Reserving Bungee Shade for the dominant feature title (the book title) is the same scale-discipline `astrarium` uses with Cormorant Bold for the dial digits versus the chrome sans labels in the datum strip.

    **Saturation.** Tier `0.7` (dark non-white ground), so the yellow bulb-lights, the white Cardo body, and the red matched-phrase accent all stay confidently chromatic against the panel's anchored black.

    **Tried and rejected.**
    - **A Solari split-flap "departures" board** in this rotation slot (yellow/black/red flap cells, Antonio condensed sans labels): the wayfinding chromatic + typographic register fought the literary content, so the slot was rebuilt as a movie-palace marquee that keeps the "chrome surrounds centerpiece" structure in a warmer, literary-friendly register.
    - **"STARRING" as the credit label:** the author wrote the book, didn't perform in it, so the literary register wins over the marquee metaphor for the credit line.

    **Its own sleep frame** (`render_marquee_sleep`, the theme's `spec.sleep`): the house closed for the night. The quote frame's black facade and alternating yellow + red bulb border stay lit, because a dark marquee reads as a dead panel rather than a closed one, and between a "— GOOD NIGHT —" and a "— THANK YOU FOR COMING —" Antonio Bold tagline sits a backlit changeable-letter board (`_marquee_paint_letter_board`: yellow trim, black reveal, white face, `_MARQUEE_SLEEP_BOARD`) reading **CLOSED** in red over **SEE YOU TOMORROW** in black. Each row (`_marquee_paint_board_line`) sets its capitals one letter at a time in Antonio Bold with wide tracking, as loose letters slotted onto a track are, between black track rails that run the width of the face; `_marquee_board_font` sizes the face by measured cap height, and a row shrinks in 2 px steps until it clears the face with a margin, so a longer notice cannot run off the board. Antonio rather than Bungee Shade: the board's letters are the plain block capitals of a real letter board, and Bungee Shade stays the quote frame's feature title. Composed at the canonical 800×480 and NEAREST-downsampled (the `metro` convention), solid inks only, no dither. Nothing on it tells the time; `TestMarqueeSleepFrame` pins the inks, the lit bulbs, the red-above-black rows and that the lettering clears the face.
  - `tarot` (cream-and-sepia foxed vellum ground / black EB Garamond body / Tyrian-purple matched phrase / red+blue rubricated chrome) — a single-card major-arcana tarot composition. A custom-render frame that bypasses the literary layout entirely.

    **Composition.** A **portrait card laid on the vellum cloth, the reading beside it**: `_TAROT_CARD_RECT` (260×432 — ratio 0.602, near-exact tarot proportions) at the left, a knocked-out cream reading cartouche filling the right — the canvas split `astrarium` uses. `_tarot_paint_card_stock` lifts the card off the cloth with a black shadow ledge (the `kanagawa` / `pride` idiom) and a *cleaner* stock than the cloth beneath it — the same two-ink recipe at three-quarters the cream density and no foxing, because without a tonal split the card's border reads as a rule drawn *on* the cloth.

    **Card furniture, head to foot.** A doubled rubricated border (3-px red outer rule + 2-px gap + 1-px black inner rule), a Roman numeral hour in Cinzel Decorative Black 36pt, a ruled illustration panel (`_tarot_paint_emblem_panel` — every historical trump sets its figure inside a keyline, and without one these compact line silhouettes read as clip art floating on the stock), and the **trump's own name in Tyrian purple (R+B 1:1 dither via `draw_text_dithered`)** in Cinzel Decorative all-caps **along the foot**, where a trump carries it. Numeral-above / figure-between / name-below is the Marseille and Rider-Waite layout. Small red rubricated lozenges (`_tarot_paint_corner_pips`) fill the four inner corners.

    **Trump name.** It comes from `_TAROT_TRUMP_NAMES`, and **`_tarot_paint_emblem` returns it** rather than the frame looking it up. The two emblem sources number the deck differently for three of the twelve hours (Marseille's Justice VIII / La Force XI against the painters' Waite ordering, plus the painters' World XII where the plates reach only Le Pendu), so a second lookup in the caller would title a Marseille plate with a Waite name the moment either dispatch changed, and would pass every visual check while doing it. `_TAROT_PAINTER_TRUMP_NAMES` is the fallback's table.

    **Name size is fitted, not fixed** (`_TAROT_NAME_SIZE_MAX` 22 down to a floor of 12). "THE WHEEL OF FORTUNE" measures 300 px at the top size against a 260 px card, so a single size would either clip the long names or set the short ones far smaller than the card can carry; nine of the twelve settle at 22, one at 20, one at 17, which is what a real deck does with its longest titles. Below the floor the name is drawn anyway rather than truncated, since every committed name clears it comfortably and a name spilling its rule is a louder signal than one cut to "THE WHEEL OF FORT…".

    **Card fences.** `TestTarotFrame` fences the wiring (the frame paints whatever the emblem call returned), the fit (blue ink only — the vellum's foxing scatters red across the whole cloth, so a red-inclusive sample reports the canvas width for every hour and passes whatever the name does), and the principle rather than the one field: the card region must be byte-identical across two unrelated rows at the same hour, so *nothing* from the quote row can reach it.

    **Woodcut plates.** The illustration panel carries the hour's trump as a **separated woodcut plate** (`TAROT_PLATES` — `idle_hours/assets/tarot_plates.png`, one 220×290 tile per hour on a 3×4 sheet, built by `scripts/ingest_tarot_plates.py` from the Jean Dodal *Tarot de Marseille* scans (Lyon, 1701–1715, public domain) committed under `idle_hours/assets/tarot/dodal/`). Unlike the dithered plates (`anna_atkins`, `letter`, `daguerreotype`, `control`, `autochrome`) this is **not** dithered at render time: a woodcut is line and flat colour rather than continuous tone, so there is nothing for an error diffusion to preserve. The ingest separates each card to white/black/red once at build time and the render crops a tile, which is also *cheaper* than running vector line-work. `_tarot_plate_tile` memoises the decoded sheet per process (the `_load_dithered_plate` shape; it matters for the 144-frame contact sheet) and `_tarot_stamp_tile` is the white-skip, clip-bounded stamp both emblem sources share.

    **Hour maps to trump number directly**, which puts the deck's own numbering on the card: Marseille numbers Justice **VIII** and La Force **XI**, where Waite — and the polygon painters below — swap them, so hours 8 and 11 changed figure when the plates landed. Hour 12 is **Le Pendu**, the Hanged Man — forced rather than chosen, since only trumps I–XII are committed; the earlier World substitution (trump XXI under a painted XII) would need `trump_21.jpg` added and hour 12 pointed at it. The ingest crops the card's own printed numeral and title bands, and that crop is load-bearing, not cosmetic: keep the bands and the card shows two numerals and a French title that argues with the quote.

    **Line work needs a LOCAL threshold, not a global one.** The committed scans are photographs of 300-year-old hand-coloured cards — foxed paper, uneven lighting, faded brown-black line — and the paper's own luminance varies more across a card than the line-vs-paper difference does in places. Across the twelve, a global cut produced **7.5%–35.6%** ink (dark blue robes swallowed whole as "line work", several cards reduced to solid blobs); a local threshold — ink is what is darker than its *own* neighbourhood, via a Gaussian local mean — holds **20.5%–25.8%**, and all twelve read at panel distance. A synthetic fixture with even lighting and flat colour cannot catch this, since that is precisely the case a global cut handles; `--line-mode global` keeps that behaviour for a clean source. Colour is still classified by saturation and hue, and *that* order matters the other way: the red flat is about as dark as the line ink, so testing darkness first fuses them. **3-ink beats 5-ink** on these scans for a reason beyond theme fidelity — aged cream paper is itself yellow enough to pass the saturation test, so keeping the yellow flat floods most of every card with the panel's saturated yellow.

    **Polygon-painter fallback.** The painters still draw all twelve when no sheet is on disk (the graceful-degradation shape `_load_dithered_plate` keeps), which is why ~740 lines of `_TAROT_EMBLEMS` survive: Magician I with vertical staff + infinity lemniscate / Priestess II with B/J pillars + crescent moon / Empress III with throne + 12-star crown arc / Emperor IV with ram-head finials + ankh scepter / Hierophant V with triple-tier triregnum + crossed keys / Lovers VI with sun + angel / Chariot VII with 4-spoke wheels / Strength VIII with lion silhouette / Hermit IX with hooded robe + raised lantern / Wheel X with concentric rims + T·A·R·O sigils / Justice XI with sword + scales / World XII with laurel wreath. That last stretch is Waite's numbering, so the fallback's hours 8, 11 and 12 disagree with the plates' by design.

    **Fallback rendering.** Each emblem is drawn at native size onto a white tile, **enlarged by `_TAROT_EMBLEM_SCALE` (1.5) and snapped back to its three inks**, then clipped to the panel. Enlarging happens in RGB rather than per-ink masks because three emblems knock white *out* of black (the Priestess's moon, the Hermit's beard), and a mask per ink loses the draw order that makes a knockout a knockout; clipping rather than lowering the scale keeps the stroke weight the enlargement bought.

    **Shared woodcut skeleton.** **Eight of the twelve are drawn to `_tarot_robed_figure`.** Rendered side by side, the emblems that read resolved into a *figure*; twelve hands drawing the same figure is why the set read as inconsistent even where individual cards worked. Three additions to the skeleton do most of the work, and they generalise past this theme:
    - **`_tarot_face`** is the highest-value mark available: an empty circle over a trapezoid is a ball on a wedge, and two eyes plus a mouth make the same two shapes read as a person — after which every attribute reads as *held* rather than floating beside a diagram. It draws brows as well as eyes because at a ~30 px head a lone dot reads as a blemish where a dot under a short bar reads as an eye.
    - **`_tarot_drapery`** puts fold lines inside the robe at one third the outline's weight, so the eye reads contour-then-detail; a uniform 3 px everywhere was most of why the first version looked like clip art. The folds carry fixed irrational-ish offsets rather than even spacing — even folds read as the pleats of an accordion skirt — and fixed rather than seeded because the frame must stay byte-deterministic.
    - **`_tarot_arm` / `_tarot_hand`** end a limb in something, so the attribute beyond it reads as gripped; one bend in the arm is the difference between a limb and a stick.

    **Golden coverage is one card in twelve.** `THEME_SWEEP_TIME` is `08:55`, so the generated `standard_tarot_production` fixture renders hour 8 and nothing else. `TestTarotEmblems` fences the rest structurally, because the failures worth catching are structural: an emblem that stops painting, two hours resolving to the same figure (a mis-indexed sprite-sheet crop, or a `_TAROT_EMBLEMS` typo — neither of which a smoke test notices, since both hours still render), and the panel clip, which six of the twelve *painters* depend on. Those three run against whichever source is live, so they fence the plates and the painters alike. `TestTarotPlateFallback` pins the painter path explicitly by pointing `TAROT_PLATES` at a nonexistent file — once the sheet is committed every other tarot test exercises it, and an unexercised fallback quietly stops working. The clip assertion samples the side gutters: the Wheel's rim overruns by ~1770 px, as far as the card's own border, and the overflow is overwhelmingly black and sideways, so a sample below the panel and only for red is vacuous. All three were verified failing against their own reintroduced fault.

    **Reading cartouche.** The literary quote body is EB Garamond Regular in a **clean cream cartouche knocked out of the cloth** via `_tarot_paint_body_panel`. The foxing-stipple from `_tarot_paint_vellum` is gorgeous as card-stock texture but breaks up EB Garamond's hairline serifs in small-glyph regions, so the body region is overpainted with a solid-white wipe followed by a fresh Y+W cream wash and framed with a thin red rule anchoring it as a deliberate "interpretation cartouche" — the same border family as the outer doubled rule. A centred author · title attribution in Cinzel Decorative Regular 12pt sits at the cartouche's foot.

    **The byline is truncated against the panel it sits in**, passed as `max_w` rather than hardcoded. A literal 470 px (the inner width of the old 520 px card) outlived the move into the 444 px panel and let nine shipped-corpus attributions ("Arthur Conan Doyle · The Adventures of Sherlock Holmes" among them) paint across the cartouche's red rule onto the cloth; anything past 470 was truncated *to* 470 and overflowed anyway, so the target was wrong and not merely the threshold.

    **Fixed geometry.** **Composed at the canonical 800×480 and NEAREST-downsampled** for a non-native request, the convention `metro` established. Every rectangle is an absolute panel coordinate, so a direct render at the curator grid's 320×192 put the whole reading panel (x=324..768) off the canvas and returned a cropped card corner with no quote on it. It is in `TestFixedGeometryFramesDownscale`.

    **Vellum ground.** `_tarot_paint_vellum` is a two-layer aged-paper recipe: a 12% Y+W cream base wash for warm parchment tone, then sparse R+G foxing at 3.5%, red or green by the low bit of the same draw. At panel viewing distance adjacent R + G dots blend into rust-brown sepia — the lignin-oxidation recipe `newsprint` uses for its foxing, but layered over cream rather than a darker halftone, so the card stock reads as older archival ritual-document than fresh manuscript vellum (distinct from the gold-cream Y+W register `astrarium` uses).

    **Both vellum layers are aperiodic, and neither is a Bayer read.** They are a **seeded `Random.randbytes` field** (`_white_noise`, the shared helper every aperiodic scatter uses) composited through `point` LUTs and `paste` masks. Seeded, so the frame stays byte-identical across processes where `hash()` would not. The two layers draw from **different seeds**, because sampling one stream twice correlates them — the failure `cardcatalog` hit when its foxing lattice shared a period with its cream wash and painted exactly nothing. Foxing is pasted first and cream over it, so a pixel both layers claim comes out cream; the painter covers the whole canvas unconditionally and must be the first thing laid on the frame.

    **Saturation.** Tier `0.5` (white ground despite the foxing) — the matched-phrase Tyrian purple and the rubricated red corner pips stay punchy at the panel's white-ground tier.

    **Tried and rejected.**
    - **A centred 520×440 card.** It read as a *border around the page* rather than as a card: no cloth was visible on any side, the whole vellum ground was hidden underneath it, and the emblem and the quote were forced to share one narrow column (the trump illustration got ~140 px of height, the quote a 92 px strip). The left-card / right-reading split fixes all three.
    - **The trump name under the numeral at the head.** That is not a card layout; the name belongs along the foot.
    - **`matched_text` in the name slot.** The card came out titled TWENTY MINUTES PAST ONE under a picture of the Magician: overrunning its own rule, naming the card after the hour (which no trump does), and restating the time the reading beside it already carries.
    - **Roman-numeral corner indices, bottom pair rotated 180°** (the playing-card convention). It does not survive contact with Roman numerals: a playing card's index is a glyph chosen to stay readable inverted, where a rotated `XI` reads as `IX` — the *wrong hour*, stated twice — and a rotated `VII` is not a numeral at all. Enlarging them from 18pt (where Cinzel Decorative's flares collapsed under the palette snap) only made the failure legible. Real trumps carry no corner indices anyway, so the glyphs were redundant with the head numeral even when they resolved; the lozenges replaced them.
    - **A global line threshold** in the plate ingest — see above; kept only as `--line-mode global` for clean sources.
    - **Per-emblem painters drawn independently.** Four didn't read — the Magician a rod with two beads, the Emperor an empty rectangle with a head on its rim, Justice a trident over two buckets, the Lovers two discs merging into one blot — and were rebuilt onto `_tarot_robed_figure`, with four more that had grown their own near-copies folded in.
    - **Too much solid black on a small head** (both skeleton additions' first cut). `hair` filled a chord from 190° to 350°, almost exactly the top half of the circle, and read as a motorcycle helmet; 215° to 325° leaves a forehead. The Hierophant's triregnum stacked three 15 px tiers flush and merged them into one tall solid trapezoid — a dunce cap, not a papal crown, since the *bands between* the crowns make it read as three. Both are outlined with gaps now rather than filled.
    - **A `BAYER_4x4` cell < 2 gate for the cream wash.** That tile's cell-0 positions lie on a diagonal, so the ground painted diagonal pinstripes — on the calibrated panel inks it read as hard corduroy rather than parchment, the "lemon grid" `kanagawa` documents. It is invisible in an RGB preview, where the saturated yellow specks swamp the structure.
    - **`position_noise` for the vellum.** Right character, but a Python call per pixel over a ground that covers the whole canvas twice (~770k calls, 60% of the frame's render time); the `randbytes` field took the frame from 402 ms to 128 ms.

    **Its own sleep frame** (`render_tarot_sleep`, the theme's `spec.sleep`): **XVIII, La Lune**, dealt in place of the hour's trump. The Moon is the arcana's card of night and of dreams, so the reading beside it is the bundled sleep quote, "To sleep, perchance to dream.", with **sleep** in the theme's Tyrian purple; and since no hour trump goes past XII, the numeral itself says the count has stopped. The plate is Dodal's XVIII (`assets/tarot/dodal/trump_18.jpg`) separated to black/white/red by the same ingest as the hour sheet, run in its `--single` mode into `assets/tarot_moon.png`; it lands at 24.4% black, inside the twelve hour plates' 20.5–25.8%, so it sits at the same weight on the card. Without the plate, `_tarot_emblem_moon` paints a crescent shedding drops over two towers, the polygon-painter fallback every hour has. The quote frame and the sleep frame share `_tarot_compose`; the reading is set from a 34 px ceiling rather than the quote frame's 26, the largest size that keeps the line whole (every size from 36 up wraps "dream." onto a line of its own). `SLEEP_QUOTE_ROW` moved from `core` down to `furniture` so a theme can quote it; `core` binds the same object.
  - `kanagawa` (white/black/red, Yuji Boku sumi-brush — Yuji Hamasaki, OFL via Google Fonts) — a stylised Japanese seascape inspired by Hokusai's "Thirty-six Views of Mount Fuji" series.

    **Seigaiha.** The centrepiece is the canonical seigaiha (青海波 / "blue ocean waves") textile pattern: overlapping fish-scale half-disks (`draw.pieslice` from 180° to 360°) in indigo with three concentric white arc stripes (radii 23 / 18 / 13) inside each scale, arranged in tight rows with each row offset by half a tile width from the row above so only the upper crescent of each scale shows — the woven-textile rhythm. Tile geometry is held on module-level constants (`_SEIGAIHA_TILE_RADIUS = 28`, `_SEIGAIHA_RING_RADII`), and the helper `_draw_seigaiha_band` fills an arbitrary horizontal band so future themes can reuse the primitive. The deepest row picks up a navy stipple post-pass (B+K 1:1, the documented `bauhaus` matched-phrase recipe) so the bottom of the "ocean" reads as deeper water.

    **Sky.** Layers above the seigaiha, deepest → shallowest: a vertically-graduated sky-blue Bayer wash (the first *gradient* Bayer wash in the codebase — threshold tapers linearly from 5/16 at y=0 to 0 at the horizon y≈264, reading as morning haze), five distant ink-stroke birds at module-level deterministic anchors (`_KANAGAWA_BIRD_ANCHORS`, with asymmetric per-bird left/right wing droops so the flock reads as five distinct soaring silhouettes rather than identical V stamps), and a thin sparse-stippled blue horizon line at y≈297 separating sky from seigaiha.

    **Hanko seal.** A red rounded-rectangle seal (32×38 px at the bottom-right corner, with a stylised `川` "kawa" / "river" kanji painted in 2 px white strokes — leftmost stroke kinked at the top to suggest brush motion) anchors the BR. Its base picks up a bbox `(x+y)&1` maroon post-pass (R+K 1:1, the recipe `dispatch` and `chanbara` both use) so it reads as the aged oxblood of a real archival ink stamp.

    **Paper panel.** The body text + attribution sit inside a cream-tinted rounded paper panel knocked out of the seigaiha (radius 12 via `draw.rounded_rectangle`), painted via the `clear_rect` dispatch in `render()`. A 1 px black frame follows the rounded corner arc, and a 2 px black drop-shadow ledge sits on the bottom-right edge (a black rounded rect offset 2 px right/down before the cream fill — the cream covers all but the 2 px ledge), reading as a hand-pressed paper card lifted slightly above the textile. The cream tint uses 4 off-grid anchor positions per 8×8 tile (at (1,3) + (5,6) + (2,7) + (6,2), ~6% yellow density on white) rather than the regular 4×4 Bayer matrix, which avoids the "lemon grid" lattice a Bayer pattern produces at desktop zoom against the saturated seigaiha indigo while still averaging to warm vellum at panel-viewing distance.

    **Type and accent.** The matched time phrase is solid Spectra red — the ukiyo-e signature-red register, tied tonally to the hanko seal's red base. Yuji Boku is the first proper Japanese sumi-brush face in the bundle (single Regular weight; the matched-phrase role re-uses Regular and gains differentiation from the red accent alone, the same trick comic / dispatch / atomic / marker / saloon / deco / chalkboard / placard / chanbara use).

    **Versus chanbara.** The rotation's other Japanese-flavoured theme is samurai *cinema* in a bold all-caps display brush (Shojumaru) with a rising sun; kanagawa is woodblock-print *textile* in a calligraphic body brush with seigaiha as the dominant motif.

    **No frame.** Authentic woodblock prints have no border (the composition discipline of `fillmore`); kanagawa is intentionally absent from `_DEBUG_LABEL_RIGHT_INSET`, since the seigaiha sits in the bottom half and no graphic touches the y=14-29 banner band.

    **Tried and rejected.** A literal Great-Wave silhouette via parametric Gaussian-peak polygons: PIL polygon fills + a 6-colour palette can't carry the ink-brush nuance the print needs, so the design pivoted to seigaiha.
  - `cartograph` (cream-washed white / black IM Fell English Italic body / red matched-phrase) — a hand-drawn antique cartographer's chart. A literary-layout theme with a heavy `draw_cartograph_border` decoration rather than a custom-render frame; it completes the navigation trio alongside `firmament` (celestial atlas) and `astrarium` (instrument dashboard).

    **Paper.** A white ground warmed by a cream Y+W Bayer Layer-0 wash plus a sparse R+G sepia foxing scatter — the two-pass aged-paper recipe `newsprint` / `tarot` use, here over cream.

    **Coastlines.** Two diagonal-corner coastline silhouettes in R+G sepia, each a 26-vertex two-octave ragged edge — a slow swell carving broad bays / headlands plus a fast jitter for the fractal nibble of a real engraved shoreline — so the land reads as a coastline with inlets and capes rather than a smooth filled triangle.

    **Topographic form-lines.** Each landmass carries real terrain relief traced as marching-squares iso-lines over a synthetic height field (3–4 deterministic Gaussian hills + basins summed across the land's bbox, contoured at six evenly-spaced elevation levels). Each marching-squares cell is skipped unless its centre falls inside the land silhouette (`_point_in_polygon`), so the contours stay on the landmass and read as the wavy multi-peak nested loops of a real topographic chart rather than concentric copies of the coastline. They are painted in black, so the red→green sepia post-pass leaves them as crisp engraved relief lines over the land fill.

    **Chart furniture.** A 32-px R+Y tangerine compass rose at the bottom-left, a small black sea-serpent margin doodle ("here be dragons"), and three hand-lettered italic Latin place labels in sepia.

    **Cartouche.** A `clear_rect` knockout turns the body region into a clean cream cartouche with a doubled red+black rubricated rule and registration-cross corner accents (the knockout `kanagawa` uses). The matched phrase picks IM Fell *Regular* (upright Roman) in solid red, so differentiation arrives via colour + roman/italic split rather than weight — the "place name upright, body legend italic" cartographic convention. Saturation tier `0.5`.
  - `vitrail` (jewel-glass field / black lead-came / clear white-glass quote cartouche) — a Gothic stained-glass cathedral window, and the "take full advantage of the hardware" theme. A custom-render frame that bypasses the literary layout entirely, dispatching from `render` into its own `render_vitrail_frame`.

    **Glass palette.** A tall lancet window whose black lead-came tracery (`_vitrail_paint_lead_came`) divides the canvas into an irregular hand-leaded mosaic of jewel-toned glass that deliberately exercises the FULL synthesised Spectra-6 palette: the four saturated native inks plus amber/gold (R+Y 5/8:3/8 tangerine), royal purple (R+B 1:1), teal (G+B 5/8:3/8), plum (R+B+K 3-ink), lavender (R+B+W 3-ink), rose/coral (R+W), sky-blue (B+W), olive (Y+G), mint (G+W), navy (B+K) and forest (G+K), drawn deterministically from the module-level `_VITRAIL_GLASS` spec list.

    **Panes.** `_vitrail_build_panes` starts from a 6×5 lattice, pins the border vertices to the frame but nudges every interior vertex off-grid by a seeded RNG (`_VITRAIL_SEED` / `_VITRAIL_JITTER`), then splits ≈half the cells (`_VITRAIL_SPLIT_PROB`) along one diagonal into two triangular shards with different tones, so the field reads as varied quadrilaterals and triangles rather than a regular grid. Each shape is filled via `_vitrail_fill_polygon` (a 1-bit polygon mask + the per-pixel `_vitrail_pane_ink`, whose solid / 2-ink / 3-ink branches mirror `_fill_swatch_stipple` / `_fill_swatch_stipple_3way` against absolute x/y, so the Bayer phase stays continuous across seams).

    **Beveled came.** `_vitrail_paint_lead_came` strokes every shape's closed outline as a *beveled raised bar* — three offset passes per seam (white specular highlight offset up-left = the lit top of the rounded lead, black drop shadow offset down-right = the shadow the raised bar casts onto the recessed glass, black core last) modelling light from the upper-left, so the came reads as a physical 3D frame standing proud of the glass. `_VITRAIL_CAME_BEVEL` sets the offset. The outer stone surround (`_vitrail_paint_outer_frame`) and the central cartouche frame carry the same lit-top-left / shadowed-bottom-right lip, so the clear glass reads as recessed behind raised lead.

    **Shimmer.** `_vitrail_paint_shimmer` sweeps a couple of diagonal specular sheen bands across the filled glass (white stippled in at a Bayer density peaking on each band's centreline and tapering to its edges, skipping black/white pixels), so the panes read as glossy glass catching light rather than flat colour fields. The field-wide pass runs before the came / cartouche so those paint cleanly over it; a second clipped pass re-applies the *same* streaks (band positions are always derived from the full window field, so they stay aligned) to the rose-window glass after the medallion is painted, so its petals catch the light continuously with the surrounding panes.

    **Arch and rose window.** A pointed lancet arch is carved into the top by filling the two top-corner spandrels black (`_vitrail_paint_arch_spandrels`). A rose-window medallion (`_vitrail_paint_rose_window`) at top-centre divides a stone-ringed glass disc into twelve jewel-tone petal wedges by radial came and carries the Roman-numeral hour (Uncial Antiqua, via the shared `_TAROT_ROMAN_NUMERALS` dict) at its clear-glass hub.

    **Quote cartouche.** The literary quote glows in a clear white-glass central cartouche (`_vitrail_paint_quote_cartouche`) knocked out of the coloured field with a solid-white wipe — not a wash — so the dark Liberation Serif body and the **violet-glass matched phrase (R+B 1:1 via `draw_text_dithered`)** stay legible over the busy panes. The author · title attribution sits at the cartouche foot in the same Liberation Serif body face. The digital HH:MM is never surfaced as plain digits — the matched phrase and the rose-window numeral carry the time.

    **Determinism and palette.** Deterministic (the pane jitter uses a seeded RNG, `_VITRAIL_SEED`), so re-renders of a given time are byte-identical. The palette in `THEMES` is a plain white/black ground with a blue (violet-glass) accent, consumed only by the palette-only paths (the button-C source card, the `--message` headline and the contact sheet's chrome — the quiet-hours sleep frame goes through the frame itself); the frame itself hardcodes Spectra-6 inks. Saturation tier `0.7` (the coloured glass dominates the canvas; the white cartouche has no chroma to scale).

    **Tried and rejected.** The Uncial Antiqua ornament chain for the attribution: the uncial's open letterforms shatter into ambiguous strokes at the 15px byline size after `snap_image_to_palette`, the hairline-at-small-size failure documented for astrarium / tarot.
  - `questline` (black night-sky ground / white body / yellow matched-phrase, Press Start 2P pixel face) — an 8/16-bit JRPG dialogue scene. A custom-render frame that bypasses the literary layout entirely, dispatching from `render` into its own `render_questline_frame`.

    **Scene.** The whole composition is built from chunky pixels: a sky-blue (B+W checkerboard) sky band over a solid-green hill band with a forest-green (G+K) lower lip, a yellow eight-ray sun and two white pixel clouds in the top margin (`_questline_paint_sky`), and a small caped hero sprite — an 8×10 pixmap (`_QUESTLINE_HERO`) scaled ×7 with a red cap / white face / blue tunic / black boots, standing on the hill line at the right (`_questline_paint_sprite`).

    **Dialogue box.** The quote is presented as **on-screen NPC dialogue** inside a bordered box owning the bottom ~50% of the canvas (`_questline_paint_box`): a navy (blue+black checkerboard) fill with a white double pixel frame (4-px outer rule + an inset inner rule, square corners — the pixel-authentic dialogue-box look, no smooth arcs). The **author becomes the speaker** on a small framed nameplate tab straddling the box's top-left edge (`_questline_paint_nameplate`, uppercased, truncated past 22 chars, yellow-on-navy; falls back to `NARRATOR` when the row has no author, so a bare-text quote still gets a speaker).

    **Body.** **Left-aligned** (reads as dialogue, not centred prose) via the shared `fit_quote` / `wrap_styled_text` pipeline at `font_max=40` / `font_min=10` / `line_height_mult=1.6`. The high `font_max` lets short quotes scale *up* to fill the box (fit_quote steps the size down only as far as the wrapped lines need to fit the box height), so a one-line quote renders large while a dense one shrinks toward the floor. Press Start 2P reports descent 0, so the generous multiplier supplies the inter-line gap the bitmap cell omits. The matched time-phrase is painted in the `accent` yellow — the RPG "highlighted keyword" tint; solid, no stipple reroute needed.

    **Box chrome.** A static white ▼ "press to continue" arrow sits in the box's bottom-right (`_questline_paint_arrow`, static — eInk), and a `— from {Title} —` footer runs along the box's bottom inner margin (`_questline_paint_footer`, centred, size 8, truncated to clear the arrow; skipped when the row has no title).

    **No digital time.** Like `marquee`, the digital HH:MM is **never surfaced** — the matched phrase in the dialogue carries the time, which is the whole point of a quote-based fuzzy clock; `time_str` is `del`-asserted at the top of `render_questline_frame` and retained only for dispatch-signature uniformity.

    **Face.** Press Start 2P (CodeMan38, OFL — the chunkiest of the bundle's three pixel faces, beside Pixelify Sans and Silkscreen) carries the body, the matched-phrase accent, and the chrome alike; single weight, so the matched-phrase role reuses Regular and earns its differentiation from the yellow accent alone (comic / dispatch / atomic discipline).

    **Rotation.** Saturation tier `0.7` (dark / saturated ground). Since it still displays a quote it stays in the button-B / auto / random rotation — only `diags` (which surfaces swatches instead of a quote) is in `RANDOM_EXCLUDED_THEMES`. No `_DEBUG_LABEL_RIGHT_INSET` entry — the custom frame paints its own composition and ignores `mode`, like the other frame themes.

    **Its own sleep frame** (`render_questline_sleep`, the theme's `spec.sleep`): the hero has **turned in at the inn**. It is the same scene after dark. A navy (blue+black checkerboard) night sky replaces the day's blue+white, with a yellow crescent moon (a disc with an offset disc of sky bitten out) and a seeded field of white pixel-cross stars (`_questline_paint_night`, fixed `_QUESTLINE_SLEEP_SEED`). The hills carry a wider forest-green (G+K) lip. Where the hero stood, a 22×16 inn pixmap (`_QUESTLINE_INN`, ×8) rises over the hill line, with a red gabled roof, a chimney, white walls, a lit yellow window and door downstairs and a **dark upstairs window**. A yellow `INN` board hangs off its wall (`_questline_paint_inn`), and three white Press Start 2P **Z's** grow as they drift up out of the dark window (`_questline_paint_zzz`). The dialogue box, nameplate, ▼ arrow and footer are the quote frame's own painters, fed a fixed row (`_QUESTLINE_SLEEP_ROW`). The **INNKEEPER** speaks: "You rest at the inn. HP and MP are fully restored. Sleep well, traveller!", with *rest at the inn* in the accent yellow where a quote's time phrase would go, over `— from The Wayside Inn —`. It reads as the game's save-and-rest beat, never as a hang. Nothing on the frame tells the time (`time_str` is `del`-asserted). The frame is composed at 800×480 and NEAREST-downsampled for any other size, so a thumbnail shows the whole inn instead of the cropped corner the quote frame's fixed coordinates would give. All six inks appear. `TestQuestlineSleepFrame` pins the night sky (moon present, no sun), the inn and the highlighted line.
  - `chrono` (twilight-blue gradient sky / white body / yellow matched-phrase, Pixelify Sans) — a **16-bit SNES JRPG** dialogue scene (Final Fantasy VI / Chrono Trigger era).
