    **The hour is the dedication year**: `ERECTED · ANNO XI` at the foot, Roman numeral, hour-only (byte-identical across minutes, pinned by `TestPlaqueRelief`), the tarot/vitrail Roman-hour precedent made in-fiction by an object that genuinely bears its erection date. Saturation tier `0.7` (dark coloured ground).

    **Tried and rejected.**
    - *The catalogue's forest-teal ground* (G+B+Y 40/40/20), for two builds. The inscription measured **3.33:1** against it, where WCAG asks 4.5:1 for body text and 3:1 even for large, and *pure white* reaches only **3.85:1** on it — so no ink or stipple could have fixed it; the ground was the binding constraint. Forest-teal goes back to the catalogue's unused list with that finding recorded against it. With the dark ground and brass face the inscription went **3.33:1 to 6.57:1**.
    - *Moving only the ground's lower cut.* That pinned blue and spent the entire swing on *yellow* (0% to 48%, the brightest ink in the mix), turning an intended hue mottle into a luminance one.
    - *Y+R gold as the inscription.* At ~3.3:1 on any ground this panel can make, it is too dark to be text. The crease was originally introduced to stop that face's red pixels — darker than the mid-tone ground — reading as holes punched through a 2-3 px stem; that job went with the red.
    - *Engraved dedication and attribution* — a black recess, then a recess with a lit far lip — on the reasoning that a real tablet casts its display line and engraves its fine print. True of tablets, wrong here once the ground went dark: **an incised groove has no contrast left to give.** Black on the dark patina is 1.3:1 — invisible — and the lit-lip variant reads as a 1 px bright outline around each letter, the "letters must read as lit metal, not outlined" failure applied to the text least able to survive it.
  - `daguerreotype` (black leather case / red velvet lid / brass Y+R mat / pewter K+W preserver / Atkinson-dithered silver plate / R+G tarnish, Libre Caslon Text) — **a cased 1850s photograph, lying open**, and the theme that claims both forward references the recipes doc had held open since the catalogue was written.

    **Atkinson dithering** is `dither_image_to_palette`'s third method: only 6/8 of the quantisation error is diffused, so highlights blow to clean silver and shadows crush to solid ink — the daguerreotype's tonal signature, and measurably distinct from Floyd–Steinberg on the same plate (pinned by `TestDaguerreotypePlate`). The **K+W 50/50 gray** ("engineering monochrome") carries the pewter preserver round the mat and the hinge knuckles. A custom-render frame (`render_daguerreotype_frame`).

    **The plate** is a committed continuous-tone landscape (`scripts/generate_daguerreotype_plate.py` — an original work in the idiom, the anna_atkins reasoning; a still river valley under a bright sky with one great canopied tree), cover-cropped to a portrait oval (`_load_dithered_plate`'s `focus`) and dithered at render time against white+black ONLY so error diffusion cannot scatter chroma into the silver — the panel's first actual monochrome photograph, the gray counterpart to `anna_atkins`'s blue one. A missing plate asset degrades to a synthesised horizon per the graceful-fallback convention.

    **All colour belongs to the case.** The **tarnish ring** creeps in from the oval rim as R+G parity sepia, hash-gated at a density rising toward the edge so it doubles as the vignette — the ageing that sells the object. The mat's pressed double ring is `paint_relief_mask`'s second consumer, embossing brass under the same upper-left light `plaque` introduced.

    **The case lies open, and the quote is stamped on the lid.** A cased daguerreotype opened like a book: the plate on the right behind its mat, and on the left the lid's pressed-velvet pad, where a studio stamped its name in gold. The quote goes there — solid yellow Libre Caslon on the red pad, the matched phrase in white, the byline under a short gold rule — with a debossed double border pressed into the nap (`paint_relief_mask` with highlight and shadow swapped). The velvet is solid red with its nap crushed to black in soft `_smooth_noise` drifts and a "pillow" falloff toward the padded edges, kept nearly clean in the middle so the stamping reads. Solid ink on solid ink at caption size, no stipple, so it stays crisp; on the calibrated inks it reads as gold leaf on a maroon pad. Black leather everywhere else, with pewter hinge knuckles on the spine.

    *Earlier version, replaced:* the plate on the left in a landscape oval and the quote on a cream caption slip (the kanagawa card idiom) to its right. It worked, but `autochrome` and `photo` used the same picture-plus-cream-card composition, and in the rotation the three read as one theme. The open case, `autochrome`'s lantern slide and `photo`'s floating card now give each its own composition.

    **A photograph carries no clock**: `time_str` is del-asserted and the matched phrase carries the time alone. Saturation tier `0.7` (velvet and leather: a coloured, dark ground).
  - `autochrome` (six-ink-dithered colour photograph / black lantern-slide mask / caption lettered on the mask, Libre Caslon Text) — **an Autochrome Lumière plate, 1907, as a lantern slide**, and the theme that dithers a plate against the **full six-ink palette** for the first time. A custom-render frame (`render_autochrome_frame`).

    **The mechanism and the medium are the same object, which is the whole argument for the theme.** An autochrome worked by carrying a mosaic of potato-starch grains dyed orange-red, green and blue-violet over the emulsion, so the image a viewer sees is not continuous colour at all — it is a *stochastic mosaic of three coloured grains* the eye integrates at viewing distance. A dither to six inks is that object. Every other synthesised tone in this codebase approximates a colour the hardware lacks; here the panel is not approximating the medium, it is doing what the medium did.

    **Every other plate restricts its palette; this one must not.** `anna_atkins` (W+K+B), `letter` (W+Y+R+G) and `daguerreotype` (W+K) each narrow the candidate set so error diffusion cannot scatter chroma into a ground that should not carry it. That is right for all three and wrong here, because the chroma *is* the subject. `_AUTOCHROME_PALETTE` is `SPECTRA6_PALETTE` and exists as a named constant so the choice reads as a decision rather than an omission; `TestAutochromePlate::test_the_palette_is_not_restricted` fences it, because a narrowed palette would fail nothing else — the frame would still render and still snap on-palette, it would just quietly stop being the thing the theme exists to be.

    **Muted and high-key is a measurement, not a mood.** A saturated source quantises to a chunky blue/red/green mosaic that reads as colour bars at 800×480, while a soft desaturated one breaks into a fine grain with white carrying the tone (measured on the shipped plate: white 48%, blue 19%, yellow 15%, black 12%, green 10%, red 6%). Autochrome's own character — pastel, high-key, soft-focus, warm-biased, because the grain layer scatters light and costs 2-3 stops — is the one photographic register that dithers *well* on these inks, so the period style and the hardware constraint point the same way. `test_white_carries_the_tone_and_no_chroma_dominates` pins both halves.

    **Every mass in the plate goes down as a blurred mask, not as a filled shape** (`scripts/generate_autochrome_plate.py` — an original work in the idiom, the anna_atkins reasoning). That is the difference between a photograph and a poster once the dither runs: a hard-edged ellipse of near-white quantises to a solid blob with a visible rim, where the same ellipse as a soft mask modulates the sky underneath it and error diffusion has a gradient to break up. The walk and lawn carry broad sun-and-cloud-shadow modulation so the dither has tone to work with, and the grass green is warmed and darkened so error diffusion does not reach for blue. `test_the_plate_is_a_photograph_not_a_texture` fences the composition by measuring how far the sky band's ink mix sits from the beds'.

    **Judge it on panel inks, never on a screenshot** — the `pride`-brown warning, and this theme is the sharpest case of it in the rotation. In sRGB the plate reads as harsh confetti with a visible blue speckle through the grass; remapped to the calibrated inks (red ≈ `#62201E`, green ≈ `#35563A`, blue ≈ `#233F8E`) and box-averaged for viewing distance, the speckle resolves into tonal grain and the frame reads as a soft summer meadow. The bloom drifts are biased into the left ~60% of the plate, a leftover from when a caption card covered the right third; the slide's window shows the full width, so they now read as a drift across the meadow.

    **The mount is a lantern slide.** An autochrome was a glass transparency, seen against light in a diascope or thrown on a screen, so the frame is the slide: a black paper mask with a wide, round-cornered window across the top, a hairline of light where the cut edge meets the glass, the process name printed in the top margin, and the projectionist's white **thumb-spot** in the lower-left corner (the orientation mark that told the operator which way up to load it). The garden is cover-cropped into the window (`_load_dithered_plate`'s `focus`, set high enough to keep the clouds, hills and poppy drift). The caption is lettered on the mask beneath: Libre Caslon in white, balanced against widows (`fit_quote_balanced`), sized up to 30 px so a short quote fills the strip. Picture above and words below, with no card. That composition is what keeps the theme apart from `daguerreotype`'s open case and `photo`'s floating card.

    *Earlier version, replaced:* the plate full bleed under black binding tape, with the quote on a cream card on the right. It was the same composition as `photo`, which falls back to this same garden when no picture is configured, so in the rotation the two were indistinguishable.

    **This is the one theme whose colour work is entirely in the plate.** The matched phrase is solid yellow rather than a synthesised recipe: on the black mask, yellow is the one chromatic ink that holds up as type (red barely clears black on the panel), and a two-ink stipple at caption size shreds a serif (the hairline failure `astrarium` / `vitrail` / `bakelite` all document). **A photograph carries no clock**, so `time_str` is del-asserted — `daguerreotype`'s rule for the same class of object. Composed at the canonical 800×480 and NEAREST-downsampled (the `metro` convention). Saturation tier `0.7` (the black mask).

    **Tried and rejected.**
    - *Compositing hard shapes and blurring once at the end* read as confetti over stripes.
    - *A flat gravel walk* floated as a beige wedge.
    - *A light sage green* sat far enough from every ink for error diffusion to reach for blue and speckle the grass cold.
    - *A composition test counting adjacent same-ink pixels* was vacuous: Floyd–Steinberg disperses by construction, so it passed for any source at all, flat fields included.
  - `photo` (the operator's own picture, or a bundled coast when none is set) — the open-ended sibling of `autochrome`, and the only theme whose art the operator chooses. A custom-render frame (`render_photo_frame`).

    **Source.** `--photo-path` (config key `photo_path`) names an image **or a directory**; a directory rotates deterministically as the displayed quote changes, driven by `_row_digest` rather than the clock, which is both the cadence a picture-frame appliance wants and what keeps run_clock's "quote unchanged, skip the redraw" dedup working.

    **The path reaches the renderer through the environment, not through argv.** `run_clock._corpus_render_args` documents why the render subprocess's argv may only carry flags an operator's own `--render-script` already recognises: an unrecognised flag exits argparse with status 2, which fails every tick and slides the appliance into render backoff. `IDLE_HOURS_PHOTO_PATH` is therefore the same channel `IDLE_HOURS_SUPPRESS_CORPUS_WARNINGS` uses, for the same reason. `run_clock.main` exports it into its **own** environment, which buys both consumers at once: children inherit it, and the in-process callers — the curator UI's `/api/preview`, `contact_sheet` — read the same value without a second mechanism. The name lives in `path_resolution` rather than `render_quote` so `run_clock` can reach it without importing Pillow (the reason `theme_names` exists).

    **An arbitrary photograph does not dither well, and the theme fixes that rather than hoping.** A saturated source quantises to a chunky blue/red/green mosaic that reads as colour bars at 800×480; `autochrome` dodges this by controlling its art, and here the input is whatever was on the operator's phone. `_photo_condition` pulls the picture into the band that survives, **adaptively** — the factors are computed from the source's own measurements and clamped so they can only reduce, so a photograph already in the band comes through untouched. Targets are read off the shipped `autochrome` plate (chroma 0.163, mean 0.671) rather than chosen, since that plate is the one measured to dither to a fine grain.

    **Brightness is asymmetric, and a dark photograph is lifted by gamma, not an offset.** The autochrome mean is a ceiling for bright photographs, not a target for every one: a source above it is pulled down to it, a dark source is lifted only to `_PHOTO_LIFT_TARGET` (0.50), and anything between keeps its own exposure. The target is decided from the source before the chroma correction runs, because blending toward grey makes a saturated photo measure darker than it is. The lift was once a flat offset, which raised the black point with the mid-tones: a forest scene at mean 0.26 came out with its deepest shadow at 36% grey, nothing on the panel stayed dark, and the frame read as fog. `_photo_lift` solves a gamma exponent on the lightest-channel histogram instead, which pins 0 and 255. Gamma widens the channel spread in the shadows, so chroma is re-capped after each lift, in a short loop because each re-cap costs a little luminance back. `test_a_dark_photograph_keeps_its_blacks` pins the black point and `test_a_moderately_dark_photograph_is_not_forced_high_key` pins the middle band.

    **Measure in the space the knob works in, and run levels last.** Chroma is measured as `(max − min) / 255`, *not* as HSV saturation, because `ImageEnhance.Color` blends toward greyscale and so scales that quantity linearly — a factor of `target / measured` lands in one pass. The two corrections are not independent: blending toward grey lowers the lightest channel of a saturated pixel, so a chroma correction drags luminance down with it. Levels must therefore run **last** (it only darkens or compresses; the lift is the gamma step above), and that ordering is stable: levels is `v * scale + offset` with `scale ≤ 1`, an offset leaves `max − min` untouched and a scale multiplies chroma by the same factor, so the levels pass can only hold chroma where it is or push it further below target — never back above, and no iteration is needed.

    **The quote cannot sit in a fixed place, because the fixed place might be the face.** `_photo_cost_map` scores candidate positions on two terms and the card takes the cheapest. *Detail* (mean edge energy) makes flat sky, wall and shadow cheap to cover and foliage or a horizon expensive. *Salience* (distance of the cell's luminance from the frame mean) exists because a smooth bright subject — a sun, a lit face, a window — has almost no edge energy **inside** it, so detail alone rates it as quiet and lands the card squarely on it. The score is `mean + 0.5 × max` over the covered cells rather than the mean alone: the card is 312×368, large enough to dilute one small face on an empty lawn to nothing at any weight, and the peak term asks the separate question "does this position clip anything important".

    **The map is computed on the continuous-tone image, and that is not a refinement but an inversion.** A dither turns every smooth region into a stipple where each pixel differs from its neighbour, so measured on the plate a flat pale wall scores *higher* edge energy than dark foliage and the card lands on precisely the part of the picture worth keeping. `test_placement_is_measured_before_dithering` pins it by asserting the plate still inverts the answer, so the test cannot quietly stop proving anything.

    **Composed at the canonical 800×480 and NEAREST-downsampled** (the `metro` convention). A preview's job is to show what the panel will show, so re-deciding the layout for a thumbnail would preview something never displayed.

    **Decoding is bounded before a pixel is materialised, and the order is the fix.** `Image.open` is lazy, so `_photo_open` drafts *then* caps *then* loads. `draft` asks the JPEG decoder to scale down during the DCT pass — a 108 MP phone panorama declares 12000×9000 and leaves the draft call at 3000×2250 — and is a no-op for PNG and every other format, which is why `_PHOTO_MAX_PIXELS` (40 MP) still has to backstop it. Pillow's own decompression-bomb guard does **not** cover this range: it raises only above `MAX_IMAGE_PIXELS * 2` (179 MP) and merely *warns* between there and 89.5 MP, so an image well inside its limits decodes eagerly — a 56 MP PNG measures 212 MiB of RSS, enough to OOM the render child on a 512 MB Pi Zero 2 W. An OOM kill is not the graceful fallback below; it is the render dying. Drafting *before* capping is what keeps the cap aimed at genuinely undecodable material rather than at ordinary photographs: a 48 MP phone JPEG is over any Pi-safe limit as declared and comfortably under it once drafted.

    **`photo_source_stamp` is public because the curator UI caches encoded PNGs.** `web_server._api_preview`'s key is theme, mode, dimensions, time, quote identity and corpus stamps — *none* of which move when an operator swaps the file at `--photo-path`. That cache answers before `_photo_frame_for` runs, so the mtime-aware key below never gets consulted and the preview would show the old picture until the entry is evicted. The stamp resolves the row's own photograph, so a directory whose membership changed is covered too: adding or removing a file can move which entry the row digest lands on, and the resolved path is what is stamped. Computed only for `theme == "photo"` — it costs a directory listing plus a stat, and every other theme's preview must stay free of both.

    **The default picture is its own: a coast with a lighthouse** (`PHOTO_PLATE`, `idle_hours/assets/photo_coast.png`, from `scripts/generate_photo_plate.py`). The theme used to fall back to `autochrome`'s garden, so with nothing configured the two showed the same meadow and read as one theme in the rotation. The coast is a different picture in kind as well as subject: a bright midday holiday snapshot rather than a soft pastel 1907 plate, carried by blue and yellow where the garden is green and red (`test_the_default_picture_is_not_autochromes` pins the ink mix). It is held to the same tonal band as the garden (chroma ~0.19, mean ~0.76) because it is not conditioned at render time, and it keeps the right-hand sky and sea quiet so the card has somewhere to land, with the lighthouse and headland on the left. If the asset is missing, `_photo_paint_coast_fallback` synthesises a coast from Bayer density ramps.

    **Every failure degrades to the bundled coast plate with a latched warning**, never raising into the per-tick render path: a missing path, a text file named `.jpg`, a truncated or zero-byte file, a CMYK scan, a palette or alpha PNG, an EXIF-rotated phone photo, a 13×9 thumbnail, a 4000×60 panorama, an empty directory. That fallback is also what makes the theme byte-deterministic with the variable unset, which is what lets it carry a golden fixture at all — and `tests/conftest.py` unsets the variable for every test, because a developer who exported it for their own appliance would otherwise have `UPDATE_RENDER_GOLDEN=1` bake their holiday photos into a committed fixture. The decoded-frame cache is a bounded LRU (the `betweenus` paper-cache lesson — a directory can hold thousands of files at ~1.1 MB a frame) keyed on path, mtime and size, so replacing the file at a configured path shows the new picture. **A photograph carries no clock**: `time_str` is del-asserted, `daguerreotype`'s rule. Saturation tier `0.5`.

    **Tried and rejected.**
    - *A blanket "make everything pastel" pass* damages gentle material in order to fix lurid material.
    - *Measuring chroma as HSV saturation.* HSV saturation is a ratio, so the same factor undershoots badly on dark colours: a factor of 0.35 for a lurid source moved it from 0.86 to 0.67 against a 0.30 target.
    - *Levels before chroma.* The chroma correction then drags luminance down after levels has set it — measured at 0.19, three times the tolerance.
    - *Computing the cost map on the dithered plate* (the first cut) lands the card on the part of the picture worth keeping; see above.
    - *Composing directly at the requested size*, on the theory that a content-derived layout ought to be re-measured per aspect. The card has a minimum sensible size that exceeds the canvas at curator-thumbnail widths, and the first cut raised `IndexError` for every render below ~250 px wide.
  - `letter` (cream/white aged-paper ground / black Dancing Script body / sealing-wax-red matched phrase) — **a wax-sealed handwritten letter**: a quote presented as intimate antique correspondence.

    **A literary-layout theme**, with a `draw_letter_border` painter rather than a custom-render frame, so the standard fit/wrap/justify pipeline lays out the flowing-script body and the border decorates the margins underneath it. Same white/black/red palette shape as `default` / `dispatch` / `saloon`, but the script body, fold creases and wax seal give it a wholly different silhouette. Fully deterministic so re-renders of a given time are byte-identical. Saturation tier `0.5`.

    **Layer 0: aged paper.** A committed continuous-tone aged-paper PNG (`idle_hours/assets/letter_aged_paper.png`, produced by `scripts/generate_letter_plate.py`: a warm cream base + edge-weighted tan vignette + low-frequency fibrous mottle + seeded reddish-brown foxing blobs) is Floyd–Steinberg-dithered at render time to the **white/yellow/red/green** sub-palette via `_load_dithered_plate`. The cream body breaks into a W+Y stipple and the browned foxing regions mix R+G into the documented sepia recipe — the photographic-tone reuse of the dithering capability `anna_atkins` introduced. Black and blue are excluded from the sub-palette so error diffusion can't scatter hard flecks into clean cream or drift the foxing cool; the crumple creases and wax seal supply their own black as primitives on top.

    **Synthesised fallback.** If the asset is missing, `_letter_paint_aged_paper` synthesises the equivalent: an *uneven cream tan* (the documented Y+W yellow cream wash, density modulated by a gentle edge vignette + low-amplitude sine mottle + a fine per-pixel grain jitter, ≈9% yellow at centre rising to ≈14% at the margins) plus *foxing* (a deterministic seeded scatter of small R+G sepia spots, each a 2×2/3×3 red/green checkerboard block so it averages to rust-brown, biased toward the edges). Either path yields pure Spectra-6 so palette-snap is a no-op.

    **Writing-area knockout.** When `render` threads `clear_rect` through (the standard literary path — `letter`'s spec sets `clear_rect_pad=(26, 18, 18)`), the body-text rect is reset to a clean pale-cream bed (≈6% yellow Bayer matching the plate's centre, foxing removed, feathered edges so it melts into the foxed margins with no drawn frame), so the thin Dancing Script body reads crisply instead of blurring against the plate's foxing speckle. It is the same `clear_rect` knockout `kanagawa` / `cartograph` / `anna_atkins` use, here resetting to cream rather than a panel ground.

    **Crumple creases.** Two horizontal fold creases at the page thirds (faint 1 px black stipple, the envelope folds) plus, **in the synthesised-fallback path only**, a few soft random-angle wrinkles rendered *tonally* (a thin cream ridge + a white highlight relief on the lit side, so they read as light catching a wrinkle rather than as black scratches). The wrinkles are skipped when the committed plate is in use because the plate already carries continuous-tone surface texture and a solid cream ridge over its W+Y stipple reads as a hard streak. All are painted only on bare paper/cream and overpainted by the body text the way ink sits over a fold.

    **The wax seal** (bottom-right, the hero graphic) is a pressed bead of dark **oxblood / maroon** wax rendered as a glossy dome lit from the upper-left. It sits on a **clean cream bed** — a soft feathered disc knocked out behind the seal that wipes the plate's R+G foxing, leaving a light W+Y cream stipple the same tone as the surrounding plate so the disc edge stays invisible, its rim thinning the foxing out gradually rather than cutting a hard circle — so the bead and its shadow sit on calm paper the way a pressed seal clears the fibre beneath it, rather than on busy foxing speckle.

    **Shading the seal.** An irregular scalloped disc (two summed sinusoids vary the rim radius so it reads as hand-pressed wax, not a clean circle), shaded per-pixel via a Bayer ramp anchored on **maroon** (R+K): pure red survives only on the lit shoulder, white dithers in for a coral gloss at the highlight, and black density climbs past 50% toward the rim so the core shadow deepens to near-black. A soft cast shadow is stippled onto the paper (offset down-right, fading at its edge; painted on all four paper inks so it stays a coherent soft disc over foxed plate paper instead of leaving foxing specks poking through), plus a solid-white specular hotspot, so the bead reads as a raised 3-D blob standing proud of the page.

    **Seal detail.** A ring of small beads just inside the rim (each shaded by its dome position — dark dot + a coral highlight on its lit side), a recessed **hourglass emblem** carved into the centre — the time motif of the clock, each groove a maroon stroke with a coral bevel on its far inner wall so it reads as engraved into the wax — and a couple of tiny maroon spatter flecks beside the seal for drip character. The seal sits at y≈height-78 / x≈width-72, clear of the centred attribution and the y=14-29 debug-banner band, so `letter` is intentionally absent from `_DEBUG_LABEL_RIGHT_INSET`.

    **The matched time phrase** is solid Spectra-6 red (Dancing Script Bold), echoing the seal's lit-red highlight — the one passage the correspondent pressed harder on. The oversized Pinyon Script quote marks go down on the black/white 50/50 faux-gray path (`ornament_dark` / `ornament_light`), so they read as a pale pen stroke rather than a blot.
  - `sampler` (cream Aida-cloth ground / black-floss body / red-floss matched phrase) — **a counted cross-stitch embroidery sampler**, the most mechanically novel theme in the rotation and a custom-render frame (`render_sampler_frame`) that bypasses the literary layout like `questline` / `outrun` / `vitrail`.

    **Every glyph is rendered as cross-stitch "X" marks** — the defining trick, new to the codebase. Text is wrapped and drawn to a small offscreen `"L"` mask in the **Silkscreen** pixel font (5px cap height at size 8, a clean fixed grid), then every set mask pixel is stamped via `_sampler_stitch_x` as a scaled-up `size`×`size` cell — two short diagonals — onto the cloth. It is the same "draw-to-mask → iterate mask pixels → write `px[x,y]`" pattern `draw_text_dithered` uses, so cost stays bbox/area-proportional, and every write is bounds-clipped for the `/api/preview` thumbnail path.

    **Fit in mask-pixel space.** The body fit loop (`_sampler_paint_quote`) walks `_SAMPLER_STITCH_SIZES` (6, 5, 4, 3) largest-first and picks the largest whose wrapped block fits the body rect — **layout happens in mask-pixel space (`body_w // size` virtual columns) and is scaled up at stitch time**, so short quotes render in chunky size-6 stitches and dense quotes auto-shrink.

    **Floss colours.** The matched time-phrase chunks are stitched in **red floss** (the time signal — no digital HH:MM is surfaced; `time_str` is `del`-asserted at frame entry); the body is black floss, and the author·title credits (`_sampler_paint_credits`) are stitched smaller in blue/green floss as the sampler's signature line.

    **Furniture, all built from the same stitch primitive.** A clean **white Aida ground** with a faint regular yellow weave-grid (`_sampler_paint_aida` — a sparse 1-fleck-per-`_SAMPLER_WEAVE_PITCH` grid reads as woven cloth without over-tinting the ground); a **stitched vine border** (`_sampler_paint_border` — alternating green-leaf / red-bud stitches around the frame with blue + yellow flower motifs at the corners and mid-edges, surfacing all four chroma inks); and classic **sampler motifs** (`_sampler_paint_motifs` — a red/yellow house, a heart, a bird, two trees in the lower margin) parsed from hardcoded character-grid templates via `_sampler_stamp_motif`. Fully deterministic (no RNG) so re-renders are byte-identical.

    **Type.** Body / matched-phrase / ornament all in **Silkscreen** (Jason Kottke, OFL — Regular + Bold), the first stitch-mapped pixel face; distinct from the other two bundled pixel faces (Press Start 2P → questline, Pixelify Sans → chrono). Stays in the button-B / auto / random rotation (it shows a quote — only `diags` is in `RANDOM_EXCLUDED_THEMES`). Saturation tier `0.5` (cream/white ground).

    **Tried and rejected.**
    - *A 1-in-8 Bayer cream wash for the Aida ground* over-yellowed it and crushed the black-floss contrast.

    **Its own sleep frame** (`render_sampler_sleep`, the theme's `spec.sleep`): the bedtime prayer stitched as a sampler, which is what real samplers often carried. "Now I lay me down to sleep, / I pray the Lord my soul to keep." (`_SAMPLER_PRAYER`, the *New England Primer*, 18th century, public domain) is cross-stitched through the same Silkscreen-mask-to-stitch path as the quote (`_sampler_stitch_chunk`), at the chunky size-6 stitch. Each verse line is split into two rows (`_SAMPLER_PRAYER_BREAKS`) so it can take that size. The body is black floss and "sleep" is red floss, the colour the quote frame gives the matched phrase, so the red word still says what the hour is for. Only the first couplet is stitched: the second, "If I should die before I wake", is the wrong thing for a bedroom clock to say all night. A sampler's other parts are stitched around it in the theme's own floss: an alphabet row in blue (`_SAMPLER_ALPHABET`) above the prayer, the maker's line "Wrought in the Idle Hours" in green below it (no year, since the quote frame stitches none), the same Aida ground and vine border, a night band of a blue-outlined yellow crescent moon between four stars (yellow alone barely shows on white panel ink, so both get a blue outline), and the quote frame's motif band with the house's windows dark (`_SAMPLER_HOUSE_DARK`: lights out). Composed at 800×480 and NEAREST-downsampled. Nothing on the frame tells the time (`time_str` is `del`-ed). **Tried and rejected:** the moon and stars as loose motifs flanking the prayer. The moon read as a stray "C" and left the lower third empty, so they moved into a band of their own above the motif band.
  - `circuit` (forest-green PCB soldermask / white silkscreen body / gold (yellow) copper matched-phrase, Space Mono) — **a printed-circuit-board composition**: the literary clock rendered as if etched onto the very board that drives the eInk panel.

    **A literary-layout theme** (not a custom frame) with a heavy `draw_circuit_border` decoration threaded through the `clear_rect`-knockout dispatch the same way `kanagawa` / `cartograph` are.

    **Layer 0: forest soldermask.** The flat Spectra-6 green `page_bg` is darkened by half the green pixels flipped to black on the `(x+y)&1` checkerboard → G+K 1:1 deep bottle-green FR-4 (the documented forest-green recipe `vitrail` uses), deliberately distinct from `atomic`, whose 1-in-4 white-on-green Layer 0 lightens the same flat green toward mint. The wash only flips *green* pixels, so it's idempotent across `render`'s two border-painter calls.

    **Over the board.** **Gold copper traces** (Spectra-6 yellow) routed from the deterministic `_CIRCUIT_TRACES` polylines with rounded `joint="curve"` teardrop corners, hugging the perimeter margins; **plated pads** (gold annular ring + dark drilled centre) at every trace endpoint; **four corner mounting holes** (white silkscreen keep-out ring + dark drill, inset 28 px — the TR hole overlaps the y=14-29 banner band so `circuit` carries a `_DEBUG_LABEL_RIGHT_INSET` entry of 46); a **Y1 quartz-crystal component** (white outline + two pads + label) in the bottom-left margin — the literal timekeeping element on a real board, a quiet nod to what the appliance is; **silkscreen reference designators** (white, `R1`/`R2`/`C1`/`C4`/`U1`/`D2`) beside the pads; and an `IDLE HOURS · REV 2.0` board legend bottom-right.

    **The body.** The `clear_rect` knockout resets the body region to clean forest soldermask — wiping any trace / pad / silk that routed across — and frames it with a thin white silkscreen "component outline" rounded rectangle + a pin-1 corner square, so the quote reads as a populated, labelled board section. The matched phrase renders in solid gold (yellow) — the highlighted copper net — and the oversized quote marks collapse onto solid gold (both ornament keys = yellow, the `atomic` / `gothic` trick) so they read as copper against the dark board rather than dithering pale. Space Mono carries the technical-silkscreen register (shared with `nightvision` — a mono is exactly the face a PCB's designators are set in). Saturation tier `0.7` (dark forest-green ground).
  - `betweenus` / `betweenus_dark` (the **Between Us** app's card UI) — [gkoch02/BetweenUs](https://github.com/gkoch02/BetweenUs), a two-person checklist for iPhone whose visual language is a warm paper ground, one rounded card at a time, a serif wordmark whose second word is italic, and a serif headline whose operative phrase is set in *italic terracotta*: "A quieter way to say *what you want.*"

    **Why it fits.** That last gesture is what this clock does with a quote, so the theme is not a skin over the layout — body in the serif, matched time phrase in italic accent — and the rest of the frame is the app's furniture. Both variants share `draw_betweenus_border`, a literary-layout theme threaded through the `clear_rect` knockout dispatch (`clear_rect_pad` 28/18/24) like `kanagawa` / `cartograph` / `letter`. Saturation tiers `0.5` (light) / `0.7` (dark).

    **Paper.** The app's top-to-bottom `paper → paper2` gradient (light #F0E9DF → #E7DDD0; dark #231E18 → #1A1611) becomes a *gradient* wash thresholded on a per-pixel positional hash rather than a Bayer rank — at the 3-9% densities involved an ordered tile lays the visible "lemon grid" `kanagawa` documents, where a hash scatter reads as paper fibre (bakelite's "hash alone is sandpaper" warning is about a mid-density field). Light: yellow on white, ~3% at the head to ~9% at the foot; dark: red on black at ~6% fading to ~2.5%, plus a trace of white, so the ground reads as the app's warm charcoal rather than the panel's cool black.

    **The paper cache is bounded.** The wash is quote-independent, so it is built once per `(width, height, variant)` and held in a **bounded LRU** (`_BETWEENUS_PAPER_CACHE_MAX`, four entries) — `render` calls every border painter twice. The bound matters because the geometry is caller-controlled: `/api/preview` is ungated and takes any size up to 800×480, so an unbounded dict keyed on it would let an unauthenticated client retain ~1.1 MB per distinct size. Four entries cover every geometry the appliance cycles through — the native panel plus the curator UI's fixed thumbnail sizes.

    **The card.** The body knockout is a rounded card (radius 18, the app's 22 pt corner scaled) with a 1 px `line` edge — R+G parity sepia on the light paper; on black the red half of that vanishes and the line reads green, so dark uses a 3/8 white hairline, the app's dark `line` #453C31. Light floats it on a **soft drop shadow** — the app's `cardShadow` is a blurred warm 14% — painted as a falling-density black stipple read off a Gaussian-blurred offset silhouette (the `paint_neon_mask` idea with the sign flipped: a bloom of dark). Dark cannot cast black on near-black, so the same blurred silhouette instead *clears the warm specks* around the card, and the clean black halo reads as the shadow. The card interior is flat white / flat black — the app's card is a shade lighter than its paper, but on eInk a speckled ground under 24 px body text is noise, so the card is the one flat surface on the page.

    **Brand row.** "Between *Us*" top-left (Fraunces SemiBold + Fraunces Italic, the app's `BrandMark`) and a capsule pill top-right carrying the **daypart** (Morning / Afternoon / Dusk …) in Inter Medium — in the app that pill names whose sheet this is; here it is the category label the app prints in italic beside its progress count, and the vocabulary is the corpus's own `daypart_for_hour` buckets. The pill sits in the y=14-38 band, so both variants carry a `_DEBUG_LABEL_RIGHT_INSET` of 144.

    **Progress bar.** The app's 5 px track under the brand row, at y=44-48 (the dense layout's body never starts above y=72 and the card pads it by 18, so the card top is never above y=54 — the count row the app prints beneath its bar is folded into the pill rather than spent on a line there is no room for). The fill length is the **fraction of the day elapsed**, the one thing on the page that moves with `time_str`, painted as a horizontal density ramp from Y-major gold to R-major tangerine on the 8×8 tile — the app's `curious → want` gradient.

    **No digits anywhere.** The matched phrase carries the readable time and the bar + pill carry the shape of the day, the `questline` / `outrun` posture but with `time_str` *used* rather than `del`-asserted. `time_str` reaches the painter through the by-name dispatch in `render`, exactly as `synoptic`'s stamp does; the registry path (button-C source card, `--message` headline) draws an empty track and no pill, which is correct for a page that is not a reading. The quiet-hours sleep frame is not on that path — it goes through `render` with the entry time, so it shows a real track and pill.

    **Legend.** The app's five answer tiers along the foot — ● Love it ● Like it ● Neutral ● Curious ● Hard No — in Inter SemiBold with each dot in its tier colour, mapped onto documented recipes without inventing one: light = solid red (deep terracotta), R+Y 5/8:3/8 tangerine (warm terracotta), K+W gray (stone), Y+R 5/8:3/8 gold (ochre), and **solid black** for `limit` (issue #257). Dark lightens every tint the way `Theme.swift` does (R+W coral, R+Y amber, K+W gray, Y+W cream) and carries `limit` all the way to **solid white** — the same lightening taken to its end, and the direction the app itself moves (its dark `limit` #9FB6AD is a light tint of the same near-neutral). The row sits at `height - 24`, in the bottom margin the dense layout leaves free (card foot ≤ ~436); in debug mode it overlaps the footer strip, the same trade `dispatch`'s "FILE COPY" footer makes.

    **`limit` is achromatic because it measures achromatic.** It is the only tier whose token does not survive a literal hue read: `Theme.swift` calls it "slate green" and it looks green written down, but it measures #5D6B66 — max minus min is 14/255, chroma 0.055 — a dark *neutral* with a cool cast, where the panel's green (#35563A) is a fully saturated ink. Trusting the name over the numbers is the `pride`-brown warning run in the other direction. The tier therefore drops onto the achromatic axis at its *extreme* and lets stone keep the middle.

    **The matched phrase is italic, not bold.** Light paints it solid red: the panel's red (~#62201E) is the app's `love` terracotta unmixed, and a solid ink keeps an italic's thin strokes whole where a stipple would shred them. Dark's `accent` is a yellow *sentinel* that `_draw_text_body` reroutes to the R+Y 1:1 amber `gothic` uses — the apricot the app's dark `want` (#DFA07C) becomes on these inks; on a black ground that brightness is the point (the recipes doc's washed-out caveat is about white grounds), and the sentinel surfaces literally only in the debug banner, the `anna_atkins` / `firmament` pattern.

    **No quote marks.** The shared layout's oversized quote marks are **skipped outright** for both variants via `_THEMES_WITHOUT_ORNAMENT_MARKS` in `_paint_ornament_mark` — the app has none, and the marks paint outside the body rect, on the stippled paper rather than the clean card, where even a `page_bg`-coloured glyph would punch a ghost hole in the wash; both ornament slots still take the page ground so `TestThemes.INTENTIONALLY_ORNAMENTLESS` stays honest.

    **Tests.** `tests/test_render_quote_themes.py::TestBetweenUs` pins the day-fraction carrier, the daypart vocabulary, the clean card, the shadow on each ground, the legend inks (including that no tier reaches for the green ink, asserted on the recipes and on the canvas), the amber reroute, and the Fraunces instance pin.

    **Tried and rejected.**
    - *Painting `limit` by its name* put an unmistakably green chip on the plate.
    - *Mixing for `limit`.* Solid green is 63 away from the token in RGB and G+K forest 88, while K+W gray is nearest at 24 but is exactly what `Neutral` (stone, #8A8076) already paints, so the two chips would collapse into one.
  - `carcosa` (black night / white Almendra body / solid-yellow matched phrase) — **The King in Yellow**, Robert W. Chambers, 1895, staged as the play itself, after *Cassilda's Song* ("Along the shore the cloud waves break, / The twin suns sink behind the lake, / The shadows lengthen / In Carcosa. / Strange is the night where black stars rise, / And strange moons circle through the skies").

    **A literary-layout theme** with a heavy `draw_carcosa_border` painter, no `clear_rect` knockout: the decoration keeps to the margins the shared layout leaves free.

    **Curtains.** A tattered yellow drape down each side (≤ 34 px wide at the rail, so clear of a body column that never starts before x≈60), tied back to a blood-red cord and tassel, its inner edge torn by two incommensurate sines plus a periodic rent, its hem frayed into strands, with moth-holes and black stars eaten through the cloth. Fold creases are a 3 px red shadow under a 1 px black line, the red then Bayer-flipped to the R+Y 5/8:3/8 tangerine so the folds read as shadowed, decaying cloth. The right curtain hangs through the y=14-29 banner band, hence a `_DEBUG_LABEL_RIGHT_INSET` of 46.

    **Sky.** A sparse yellow haze across the top margin (a `position_noise` threshold, not a Bayer rank — at ~9% an ordered tile lattices) with six **black stars** rising in it ("Strange is the night where black stars rise"), each a black four-pointed star with a yellow rim, because a black shape has to be outlined to be seen against black.

    **The Yellow Sign** is centred over the proscenium, clear of the right-aligned `DEBUG MODE` banner, and it is an **original glyph**: Chambers never describes the Sign, and the familiar triskelion is a later artist's design, so it is drawn here as three hooked Archimedean arms off a ring, the third arm longer, with a stray dot beneath — almost symmetric, which is the unsettling part.

    **Lake Hali** along the foot: the twin suns sink half-set on the horizon under a second sparse yellow haze (their glow over the far shore), the spired towers of Carcosa stand black against them (kept low enough that both suns' crowns stay clear — the city stands against the suns, it does not hide them), a lit slit in the tallest towers, cloud-waves breaking along the shore to the left as rows of white crests, and the suns' reflections breaking into dashes that narrow with depth.

    **The King's colour is never softened.** The matched phrase is **solid** yellow, so the inks are the same as `dark`'s by design; the painter and the face are what separate them. Both ornament slots pin to yellow so the Almendra Display quote marks paint solid.

    **Construction.** Every element is `ImageDraw` primitives plus bounds-checked per-pixel passes, with geometry scaled by `min(1, width/800, height/480)` so it clips cleanly at `/api/preview` sizes. No time surface of its own — the matched phrase carries the time. Saturation tier `0.7`.
  - `control` (white void / black Jost Bold body / Hiss-red matched phrase) — **Remedy's *Control* (2019): the Astral Plane as a title card**. A custom-render frame (`render_control_frame`).

    **Three of the game's registers stacked on one page:** the **Astral Plane** (a flat white void with isometric stone blocks floating in the margins and the Board's inverted black pyramid hanging from the top edge), the **Oldest House** (a brutalist concrete plinth across the foot carrying a black wayfinding sign in white condensed caps — the Bureau's ring-and-triangle device, FEDERAL BUREAU OF CONTROL over THE OLDEST HOUSE), and the **Hiss** (the matched phrase in solid red, bleeding a stippled coral halo into the white).

    **The blocks are drawn, not dithered plates.** Each is three polygons under one upper-left light — a white top, a lit face at 25% black and a shadow face at 56% on the plain `BAYER_8x8` tile, outlined in black — so the flat machined geometry of the Plane comes out exactly; unjittered on purpose, because a jitter would put grain on a surface whose whole character is smoothness. The Board's pyramid is the same construction with its lit face at 68% and its shadow face solid, so it reads as the darkest solid on the page.

    **The plinth is the opposite surface, and is a dithered plate.** `idle_hours/assets/control_concrete.png` (800×88, produced by `scripts/generate_control_plate.py`) is a continuous-tone sheet of board-formed concrete — the timber grain of the shuttering, a tonal step per formwork panel with a soft recess at each joint, two horizontal lift joints, aggregate pits and lime specks, darkening toward the foot as a wall does below eye level — Floyd–Steinberg-dithered to white+black at render time via `_load_dithered_plate`, the `letter` plate capability. A hash-jittered ordered stipple (the `bakelite` moulding recipe) stays as `_control_paint_concrete_stipple`, the fallback for a missing asset: jittered because an ordered tile alone reads as a screen door and a hash alone as sandpaper. Formwork seams and a row of tie-holes go on top in either case, without which a grey band is just a grey band.

    **The Hiss is the first bloom on a white ground.** `paint_neon_mask` had only ever lit dark grounds, where a falling density of a bright ink reads as light; a falling density of red on white reads instead as a stain spreading from the phrase, which is what the Hiss does to the House. `ground` is pinned to white so the halo cannot eat the black prose beside it, and the parameters (radius 6, cap 0.62, gamma 1.6) are deliberately heavier than they look correct at 1:1 — the `izakaya` lesson: a bloom tuned at pixel scale vanishes once the panel is box-averaged by viewing distance, and `tests/test_render_quote_themes.py::TestControlFrame` pins that the halo carries at least a fifth of the core's red. The prose is pasted from a *thresholded* mask rather than drawn antialiased: a mid-grey edge pixel is nearer red than black in RGB distance, so the palette snap would speckle black text with red.

    **Resonance is an echo, never a tear.** Three thin bands of the phrase are repeated a few pixels sideways as a half-density red stipple (`_CONTROL_RESONANCE`), the horizontal signal-tearing the game lays over Hiss-corrupted text — written only onto white, so it can neither cut a glyph of the phrase (the `vhs` lesson: the time phrase is the clock's job) nor overwrite the prose or the bloom.

    **The Board speaks in pairs**, and that is the attribution: the sign's right half reads `AUTHOR/ORIGIN:` and `WORK/VESSEL:` over the row's own author and title — labels in the Board's diction, values never invented. The column's budget is measured off the painted name run, and a title that still will not fit is shrunk to a floor and then ellipsised through the shared `fit_text_to_width`, never chopped mid-word.

    **No time surface beyond the matched phrase**: the Plane has no clock and a `SECTOR 07` would be a number bolted on, so `time_str` is `del`-asserted (the `pride` / `outrun` posture) and pinned byte-identical across every time. Composed at the canonical 800×480 and NEAREST-downsampled (the `metro` convention). Saturation tier `0.5` (white ground; the coral halo should stay a stain, not a fire-engine smear).
  - `observation` (black space / white IBM Plex Mono body / yellow matched phrase in a tangerine halo) — **No Code's *Observation* (2019)**, the station AI's camera feed. A custom-render frame (`render_observation_frame`).

    **The premise.** The player of the game *is* S.A.M. (Systems Administration and Maintenance), the AI of a space station orbiting Saturn in 2026, seeing only through the station's cameras — so the page is one camera feed with the AI's interface laid over it.

    **The feed.** A seeded star field (`_OBSERVATION_STAR_SEED`), Titan as a small tangerine disc, and Saturn — a disc whose latitude bands are computed on the sphere (the pole leans toward the viewer by `asin(_OBSERVATION_RING_K)`, so each band curves the way a real belt does), shaded by a terminator plus limb darkening from an upper-left sun, its **blue-grey north polar cap** (the Cassini-era observation) carrying the **hexagonal jet stream** at its true 78°N, projected onto the visible pole.

    **The rings** are a tilted annulus computed in the ring plane: C ring faint, B ring bright, the Cassini division and Encke gap as real gaps, an F-ring thread. Far-side ring pixels over the disc are skipped (the planet occludes them); near-side ones cross in front **translucently** — an unlit tile cell or a gap over the disc leaves the planet showing rather than punching black.

    **Sensor damage.** Faint blue sensor noise haunts the black, and three **tracking tears** shear bands of the feed sideways — applied *before* the HUD, so a tear can never cut the quote (the `vhs` lesson), pinned by `TestObservationFrame::test_tears_never_cut_the_transcript`.

    **Saturn is where the vibrancy lives, and it is one read per pixel.** Every band is a documented two-ink mix — cream (Y+W), gold (Y+R), tangerine (R+Y), sky blue at the pole (B+W) — and the terminator is black; `_observation_mix` resolves all three with a **single** `BAYER_8x8` rank partitioned three ways (lowest ranks black, the rest split between the band's two inks in its own ratio), which is `pride`'s rule: a second read of the tile is perfectly correlated with the first and would slide the band's hue across the terminator instead of darkening it. The bands are mostly cream with thin gold and tangerine belts, because a planet striped in saturated orange reads as a beach ball.

    **The anomaly.** The game's alien presence is a hexagon, so below the rings float nested glowing hexagons — white core, blue bloom through `paint_neon_mask` with `ground` pinned to black — boxed by a yellow tracking reticle labelled `UNIDENTIFIED OBJECT / TRACKING — NO DATA`.

    **The HUD.** Camera-frame corner brackets, the `S.A.M. SYSTEMS ADMINISTRATION & MAINTENANCE` identifier, a red REC tally, the camera designation in yellow, an `UPLINK / EARTH` meter (three of five green bars, `DEGRADED` in red) and a station schematic along the foot.

    **The quote is an audio log:** an opaque terminal panel with an inverted header naming the author as the log's source (`AUDIO LOG — UNATTRIBUTED` for a bare row), the quote set **ragged-right** — `wrap_quote_into_masks` grew an `align="left"` for it, since a transcript does not centre — white prose pasted from a thresholded mask, the matched phrase a yellow core in the `bakelite` split-band **tangerine halo** (`glow=red`, `glow_minor=yellow` at 3/8, 8×8 tile, `ground` black so it can never eat the prose), a green playback waveform with a yellow playhead, and the title as the recording's `FILE`.

    **The hour is the camera number** — `CAM 07 · ENGINEERING` — the 12-hour clock hour (`_clock_hour12`, the shared parser every hour-only surface uses), with two cameras per module so the hour also lights one module of the schematic. That is the `cardcatalog` due-hour / `bakelite` setting-index posture: a designation a reader parses as a feed, not a clock; the minute stays with the matched phrase, pinned byte-identical across every minute of an hour. The log number and waveform are seeded from `_row_digest`, never the clock, so a different quote is a different recording.

    **Housekeeping.** Surfaces all six inks at once (pinned). Composed at the canonical 800×480 and NEAREST-downsampled (the `metro` convention). Saturation tier `0.7`.
  - `trisolaris` (black space / white Titillium Web body / sunlit yellow matched phrase) — **Liu Cixin's *The Three-Body Problem* (三体, 2008)**: a custom-render frame (`render_trisolaris_frame`) whose sky is **computed, not drawn**.

    **The clock advances an integration.** Three suns and a massless planet are integrated under softened Newtonian gravity from committed initial conditions, so the frame is a *solution* of the three-body problem rather than a picture of one. Each minute of the twelve-hour dial is a fixed slice of simulated time (`_TRISOLARIS_SAMPLES_PER_MINUTE` quarter-minute samples, each `_TRISOLARIS_STEPS_PER_SAMPLE` leapfrog steps); noon and midnight both start the dial, and a 90-minute preroll means 12:00 already has a trail. `_trisolaris_ephemeris` integrates the whole day once per process (~0.1 s on a desktop; memoised because a contact sheet renders 144 frames in one process).

    **The novel's mechanics fall out of the physics.** In the book's VR game a *stable era* is a stretch in which the planet orbits one sun while the other two are far away; here it is read off the integration — stable when the strongest sun's pull on the planet exceeds the next by `_TRISOLARIS_STABLE_DOMINANCE` — and the header prints `STABLE ERA · REHYDRATE` or `CHAOTIC ERA · DEHYDRATE` (the king's two orders) beside a three-disc glyph, one filled or all three. When the planet falls into a sun or is flung out of the system, that civilization is destroyed: the planet is reborn in a circular orbit about the most isolated sun and `CIVILIZATION NO.` advances (the counter is how the game numbers its civilizations).

    **Burns, losses and rebirths.** The burn and loss checks run on **every leapfrog step**, not every sample — a fast pass can dip inside the burn radius and back out between two samples. The rebirth speed is the circular speed under the *softened* force law the planet actually feels, `v² = m r² / (r² + ε²)^{3/2}`, because the Keplerian `√(m/r)` is ~5% too fast at the rebirth radius and starts every civilization on an eccentric orbit. The planet's trail is broken at every rebirth so the teleport never draws as a streak (`TestTrisolarisFrame::test_the_planet_trail_breaks_at_a_rebirth`).

    **Byte-identical everywhere, by construction.** The integrator uses only `+ - * /` and `math.sqrt`, all correctly rounded under IEEE 754; `**` is deliberately kept out of the force law (`r2 * sqrt(r2)`, not `r2 ** 1.5`), because `pow` goes through the platform libm, which is not required to round correctly. Kick-drift-kick leapfrog at a fixed step with Plummer softening — an unsoftened close pass needs an adaptive step, and a step controller's float comparisons would make the frame platform-dependent. A test re-integrates in a fresh interpreter under a different `PYTHONHASHSEED` and compares the last sample's `repr`.

    **The initial conditions were searched, not guessed.** A generic bound three-body system ejects a member within a few dozen crossing times, which would leave the dial showing one sun receding off the panel. The constants come from an offline search over random zero-momentum starts for one whose suns stay bound for the whole window *and* whose planet lives through both eras with a handful of civilizations lost per day; `TestTrisolarisFrame` fences all three against the committed constants (plus the barycentre staying at the origin to 1e-9, which is what zero total momentum under pairwise-symmetric forces guarantees — and the point the dish's transmission aims at). The projection is fitted to the suns' whole-day paths, not the planet's: a lost planet flies far outside the system, and fitting it would shrink the suns to a speck.

    **The orrery** owns the left of the canvas: each sun a gold bloom (yellow core with a white share, the bakelite split-band tangerine halo), its last hour of path trailing behind as a stipple that cools from yellow to tangerine and thins with age — *tangerine*, not solid red, because the panel's red sits within a few luminance points of its black. `_trisolaris_stipple_line` carries every warm line on the frame for that reason, and is the frame's one line rasterizer — the wake yellow share is `hot + (1 − hot) · 3/8`, so a wake cools smoothly to the tangerine floor rather than stepping onto it. The sun halos are confined to empty sky (`ground={black, blue}`) so a sun passing the lower-left of the sky box dims into the dish's wavefronts rather than stippling over them. The planet is a blue disc with a white limb and a sparse white trail.

    **The dish.** At the foot, the Red Coast Base dish stands on Radar Peak as white **line-work** — face, back, feed struts, pedestal — over a tangerine crest line with two fainter contours, and a fan of wavefront arcs leaves its feed: Ye Wenjie's transmission. The dish axis is *derived* — aimed from its pivot at the projected barycentre — rather than hardcoded.

    **The quote** sits in the dark sky on the right under a `三体` masthead in the bundled Yuji Boku, the prose solid white and the matched phrase lit as sunlight. Along the foot runs the pacifist's reply that closes the first act, `DO NOT ANSWER!`, repeated as the column allows, in solid yellow — red barely clears the black at 11px and a two-ink tangerine shreds a letterform that small. Stars are kept out of the entire text column, not just the quote block (excluding only the block leaves 26 stars inside the masthead, the header chrome and the warning on the committed seed).

    **No digit of the time is printed**: the clock drives the simulation and therefore the era; the civilization number counts deaths, not hours. Composed at the canonical 800×480 and NEAREST-downsampled (the `metro` convention). Saturation tier `0.7` (black ground; every light is a falling-density bloom or a sparse stipple).

    **Tried and rejected.**
    - *Burn/loss checks at sample boundaries* let one such planet per day survive.
    - *A hardcoded −52° dish axis* pointed 18° above the system while the docs claimed it aimed at it.
    - *A black dish silhouette against a dusk glow* failed both ways: the glow read as a red dune field sitting on the ridge, and the black dish vanished above it.
    - *A straight beam from dish to suns* read as one more orbit trail.
    - *A cold blue halo on the prose*: at radius 2 it read as a blue outline rather than as starlight.

    **Its own sleep frame** (`render_trisolaris_sleep`, the theme's `spec.sleep`): **"Chaotic era. Dehydrate."** In the novel's game a chaotic era is survived by dehydrating, drying out and being stored until a stable era allows rehydration, which is the Trisolaran way to sleep. The frame reuses the quote frame's scene (`_trisolaris_scene`: stars, the last hour of orbits, Radar Peak and the dish, suns and planet) frozen at `_TRISOLARIS_SLEEP_INDEX`, the first ephemeris sample of the day's longest chaotic era, so the masthead prints `CHAOTIC ERA · DEHYDRATE` with all three discs of the era glyph filled and that sample's civilization number (a count of deaths, not a time). In the quote's place the column reads "Dehydrate, and rest. You will be rehydrated when the stable era returns.", the last four words lit as sunlight in the matched phrase's bloom, over the dry store: three dotted shelves of rolled, dried citizens in the dish's white line-work, each roll's open end an elliptical spiral (`_trisolaris_paint_spiral`) and two thin yellow cords tying it. A single central cord band was tried first and made the rolls read as batteries at panel distance. The foot's `DO NOT ANSWER!` gives way to the store's notice `STORED DRY · AWAITING THE STABLE ERA` in the same 11px solid yellow, so the frame reads as rest until morning rather than catastrophe. `time_str` is unused (`del time_str`), and no digit of the time appears. Composed at 800×480 and NEAREST-downsampled, like the quote frame; at 320×192 the rolls' hairlines break up, as the quote frame's do.
  - `biomech` (K+W airbrushed wall / blood-red dithered dusk / bone-white Spectral body / ember matched phrase) — **H. R. Giger's biomechanical wall round a Zdzisław Beksiński dusk**. A custom-render frame (`render_biomech_frame`).

    **The portal.** The two painters supply what the other lacks — Giger is *surface* (airbrushed flesh-machinery: vertebrae stacked like pistons, corrugated hoses, elongated skulls, lit as wet bone and chrome), Beksiński is *distance* (burning evenings over desolate plains, cathedrals of something once alive, shrouded figures, leaning crosses, haze) — so the page is a pointed arch cut through a Giger wall, and through it a Beksiński dusk.

    **The new technique is `shade_height_field` — the eighth tone axis in `docs/spectra6_color_recipes.md`, and the first that is a 3-D render rather than a stipple rule.** A whole surface is given a normal at every pixel and shaded Blinn-Phong under the house upper-left light, and the result is left as *continuous tone* for Floyd–Steinberg to carry onto the inks — which is what an airbrush is, and what no constant-density recipe, falling-density bloom or rank partition can fake. It differs from `paint_relief_mask` in kind: that primitive lights the blurred *edge* of a flat mask and writes ink per pixel in Python (right for a raised letter); this one takes an arbitrary height field and returns tone, and everything per-pixel is C-speed — two 3×3 Sobel `ImageFilter.Kernel` passes for the slopes and one lookup into a memoised 65536-entry `(gx << 8 | gy) → tone` table (~30 ms for the full canvas).

    **The Sobel weights were measured, not assumed:** `ImageFilter.Kernel` applies its rows in reverse order but its columns as written, so `_SOBEL_Y` looks upside down and `_SOBEL_X` does not. A flipped sign still renders a plausible relief, just from the wrong corner, which is why `TestShadeHeightField` pins both against ramps. `scale=2` (four grey levels per unit slope) is what stops a broad soft dome terracing into contour bands.

    **The wall is built as heights, not drawn as ink.** Every form is a solid shape blurred into a rounded profile (a line blurred to about a quarter of its width is a tube, an ellipse is a dome) and unioned with `ImageChops.lighter`; carving is `ImageChops.subtract` — the skull's sockets, nasal cavity and a row of teeth, and the grooves across every corrugated hose (`_biomech_hose`, grooves laid along the polyline's normal every `pitch` px by `_biomech_resample`). Piers of vertebrae (bodies, spinous knobs, swept transverse processes) crowned by skulls, a ribbed archivolt and a second hose following the arch outward, spandrel tendrils sweeping out of the top edge, a hose along the sill, all over a ground of two octaves of seeded value noise.

    **Shading the wall.** The field is shaded, **darkened by its own height** (recesses fall to black, Giger's depth cue) and by a cavity term (blurred minus sharp), then dithered to **white + black only**: a neutral grey sits almost equidistant from black, white *and* red in RGB, so a four-ink dither scatters red specks through the bone (the `daguerreotype` reasoning).

    **The red on the wall is rim light, not pigment.** A second shading pass lights each pier from the portal side, low; where it rakes a surface red is stippled in at a density following it, via one C-speed compare against a tiled `BAYER_8x8` field (`_bayer_threshold_field`), and only within ~30 px of the opening. Confined, the burning sky is *reflected* on the machinery, which is what makes the arch and the view one space rather than a frame pasted round a picture. The rim test asserts red stays under 12% of the pier and concentrates on its portal side.

    **The dusk is painted, then dithered to K/R/Y/W.** A per-row gradient (a truly black zenith, a blood-red middle sky, a molten horizon), cloud streaks from bicubic-stretched seeded noise (`_smooth_noise`, the `_white_noise` C-speed white noise upsampled) brightening and darkening the middle sky only, a half-set sun clipped to the horizon, then silhouettes in atmospheric perspective — each coloured as black pushed toward the sky behind it, the further the more (`_biomech_haze`): a far ridge, a shrouded giant, a cathedral of thorned organ-pipe spires whose tallest stands in front of the sun with lancets lit from behind, and leaning crosses stood on the horizon's brightest band. A streaked fog veils every base. Blue and green are left out of the palette so error diffusion cannot cool the fire; the theme's test pins the frame to exactly those four inks.

    **Both plates are procedural and quote-independent**, so the composed background is built once per process — the first theme to dither a render-time painting rather than a committed PNG. The cache (`_BIOMECH_BACKGROUND`) holds one entry keyed on the painter functions themselves, looked up at call time: a plain "built yet?" flag would hand a test that neuters `_biomech_paint_wall` the frame painted before the patch, and the structural decoration fences would measure the cache, not the painter. `test_background_cache_respects_a_patched_painter` fences it.

    **The quote** sits in the black upper sky inside the arch (`_BIOMECH_QUOTE_RECT` is checked against `_biomech_arch_halfwidth`): bone-white with a 2 px black halo (the `anna_atkins` answer — a halo, not a panel, so the arch keeps its view), the matched phrase an **ember** — solid yellow core, red bloom via `paint_neon_mask` with `ground` pinned to black so it can only spill onto night. Byline in Spectral Medium Italic over the dark plain.

    **The hour is the sill plate's title**, `Biomechanoid · XI`: Giger numbered whole series in Roman numerals, so a number on the plate is the work's own furniture (the `tarot` / `plaque` posture). Hour only, pinned byte-identical across the minutes of an hour; the numeral is ember yellow because red on black nearly vanishes on the calibrated inks. Composed at the canonical 800×480 and NEAREST-downsampled (the `metro` convention). Saturation tier `0.7`.

    **Tried and rejected.**
    - *Treating both Sobel kernels as a true convolution* lit every form from the lower left.
    - *A gentler height-to-tone mapping* made the whole wall read as a mid-grey noise field.
    - *Rim light across the whole wall* made the piers read as red noise.
    - *A near-black `(4,0,0)` zenith* dithered to a regular lattice of red specks behind the quote.
    - *An unclipped sun* left its lower half lying on the plain as a white disc.
    - *Crosses on the ground* were black on near-black and vanished.
  - `codex` (cream page / black Fondamento body / red italic matched phrase / full-palette plant plate) — **a page of Luigi Serafini's *Codex Seraphinianus* (1981)**, the imaginary encyclopedia of a world that does not exist, written in a script nobody can read.

    **The page.** A custom-render frame (`render_codex_frame`), laid out as one of the book's botanical entries: a chimerical specimen plate on the left, columns of script on the right, and the quote as the page's **one deciphered passage** — set in a pen hand between lines of untranslated script, as though a reader had pencilled the gloss in.

    **The script is generated, not typeset.** No font can carry it, because the point of Serafini's writing is that it is not an alphabet. `_codex_script` drives a pen along a prolate trochoid, `x = x0 + w·t + r·sin 2πt`, which is the mathematics of a cursive hand: the stroke rises, swings back and crosses itself (a loop) whenever `r > w/4`. Each letter draws its swing, height and direction (ordinary loop, hump, tall ascender, descender) from a `random.Random` seeded by `_row_digest` — never `hash()` — so every quote gets its own page of script, byte-identical across processes. Words are one continuous stroke with the pen lifted between them, plus the occasional diacritic dot or hook.

    **The word budget is the worst case, not the average** (`_CODEX_WORD_OVERHEAD`: lead-in, exit tail and stroke, on top of the widest letter), so the ink provably stops at the line's end rather than running into the rainbow vignette. `TestCodexFrame::test_script_never_overruns_its_line` samples 1,500 line ends because an overrun is a worst-case event (60 long lines pass with the overhead zeroed).

    **Lines are also bounded vertically:** every letter and diacritic stays within `_CODEX_REACH_ABOVE` (2.1) / `_CODEX_REACH_BELOW` (1.5) x-heights of its baseline — diacritics go over short letters only — and every stacked pair of lines is pitched at least `below·xh_upper + above·xh_lower` apart, so an ascender can never cross the descender above it. Collisions are quote-dependent, which is why a single golden render cannot show them. The plate caption sits below the roots (`_CODEX_CAPTION_BASE`) for the same reason.

    **The time is the page number.** Serafini numbered his pages in an invented numeral system that readers later worked out to be base 21. `codex_page_digits` writes the **minute of the day** in base 21 — at most three digits, since 21² = 441 and 1439 = 3·441 + 5·21 + 11 — in the bottom outer corner using twenty-one invented digit glyphs (`_codex_numeral`). Each glyph is a curved stem whose features (top loop, foot hook, crossbar, dot, tail curl) are switched on by the five bits of its value, zero being a bare ring, so the twenty-one are pairwise distinct and a given digit is always drawn the same way — which is what makes it a numeral system rather than decoration. Undecipherable at a glance, exactly as in the book, but decodable by a determined reader; the readable time stays with the matched phrase. `time_str` is therefore *used*, the `lieder` / `izakaya` posture, and `test_time_reaches_only_the_folio` pins that the clock moves nothing but the folio corner.

    **Vibrancy is the plate's job, not the ground's.** The page stays a calm cream (a sparse ~5% yellow scatter from `_white_noise`, aperiodic for the lattice reason `position_noise` documents) so the dense 1 px script columns read. The specimen surfaces every native ink plus six documented recipes at once: a tapering stem banded through the spectrum (red, R+Y tangerine, yellow, green, blue, R+B violet), **fish-shaped leaves** growing nose-first out of the stem on tangerine tails — teal G+B, mint G+W or solid green, with blue scale arcs, a red dorsal fin and one eye each — a corolla of alternating rose R+W and violet petals round an **open blue-irised eye**, striped seeds drifting off on thread parachutes, and sepia R+G roots curling into spirals below a green-hatched ground line.

    **Fill discipline.** The roots are drawn into a private mask and filled through `_CODEX_SEPIA`, never as red then parity-flipped, because a "flip any red pixel in this box" post-pass would silently recolour anything red a later change painted there. Every shape is a documented recipe filled through `_vitrail_fill_polygon` and then inked with a black contour (`_codex_fill`) — coloured pencil inside a pen line, the way every plate in the book is drawn.

    **Apparatus.** Dotted leaders run from the right-hand fish to asemic labels, the apparatus of an encyclopedia plate captioned in a script nobody can read. A small **rainbow** beside the red rubric heading is tacked to the page by a red pin at one foot and drips its bands into a violet puddle at the other — the Serafinian move of a familiar object that turns out, on a second look, to be something else. The band order, leaf colours and petal count are seeded per row, so each entry in the encyclopedia is a different specimen. Composed at the canonical 800×480 and NEAREST-downsampled (the `metro` convention). Saturation tier `0.5`.

    **Tried and rejected.**
    - *Looser reach and pitch* (an ascender reaching 2.5 x-heights and a descender 1.9 at a 20 px pitch) collided lines whenever a tall loop met a low one.
    - *A caption above the roots* was written straight through the root spirals.
  - `culture` (black space / white Jura body / yellow matched phrase in a green drone-aura bloom / Share Tech Mono chrome) — **a Mind's signal beside the Orbital it concerns**, after Iain M. Banks's Culture novels. A custom-render frame (`render_culture_frame`), and the first of a pair with `orbital`: one Orbital seen from two places.

    **Both tell the time with the Orbital itself.** An Orbital is a ring millions of kilometres across that spins once a day, so each plate cycles through dawn, noon and midnight, and at any instant the ring as a whole carries *every* local time. `_culture_clock` reads the **full 24-hour clock to the minute** (on an Orbital noon and midnight are different pictures, so the 12-hour convention the other hour-carriers use would be wrong here); neither theme prints a digit of it.

    **The signal (left).** Set out the way the novels print ship-to-ship traffic — a bracketed transmission line (`[tight point, M16, tra. @n4.28.861.3644]`) then `x` (from) and `o` (to) ship names in Share Tech Mono — and the quote as its body in Jura, ragged-right via `wrap_quote_into_masks(align="left")`, white prose pasted from a thresholded mask and the matched phrase a yellow core in a green `paint_neon_mask` bloom (the drone's aura field; `ground` pinned to black so it can never eat the prose).

    **The Orbital (right)**, tilted in space, rendered **per pixel in closed form** by `_culture_paint_orbital`: for a screen pixel the ring's centreline is an ellipse of half-height `h = R k sin`, so a pixel is on the far arc when `v` lies in `[-h - W, -h]` and on the near arc when it lies in `[h - W, h]`; the near arc is tested first so the hull passes in front of the face where the two meet at the ends. The far arc shows the **inner face** (land, sea and cloud from `_culture_terrain`'s incommensurate sines, shaded toward the terminator by one `BAYER_8x8` read partitioned `pride`-style, its night side black but for yellow city lights), the near arc the **hull** (a K+W stipple where it faces the sun, with ribs). A small GSV and two escorts cross the dark above it as field-sheened hulls in a blue bloom.

    **The time is the marked plate.** `_culture_plate_theta` puts the plate whose local time is now at `_CULTURE_NOON + (clock − 0.5)·2π`: overhead on the lit face at noon, round on the hull at midnight, once round per day. `_CULTURE_NOON` is the *middle* of the far arc (−90°) so that arc holds exactly the 06:00–18:00 plates, pinned by `TestCultureFrame::test_marker_is_on_the_lit_face_by_day_and_the_hull_by_night`. The cost is that the face's night side has shrunk to its two tips, so its city lights show only at dusk's edge. Its leader runs radially away from the ring's centre so it never crosses the band — except near the left end, where radial would point into the quote, so it drops straight down instead; `TestCultureFrame::test_time_reaches_only_the_orbital` sweeps the clock to pin that nothing time-dependent ever reaches the signal column.

    **Marain.** The matched phrase is written out again under the ring in a generated **Marain-idiom** script on the Culture's 3×3 dot grid (`_MARAIN_TABLE`): each letter and digit gets a distinct, orthogonally connected 4–6-point pattern, walked by a fixed multiplicative step through the 512 codes so neighbouring letters don't get near-identical glyphs, and wrapped only at word gaps (hyphens count as gaps — the script has no hyphen glyph, and "five-and-twenty" fused into one word overruns a row). Banks published the grid; the table is ours and the comments say so — it transcribes deterministically, it does not claim to reproduce his script.

    **The Marain block shrinks rather than drops**: `_culture_marain_fit` steps the grid pitch down from 7 until the whole phrase fits, because the phrases that overflow are the long spelled-out ones whose last row is the hour itself. `TestCultureFrame::test_no_corpus_phrase_loses_a_glyph` sweeps both committed corpora.

    **Signal furniture.** **Ship names are invented** (a time-flavoured list — this is a clock) rather than lifted from the novels, and every piece of signal furniture (ships, channel, `M` level, the `tra.` date, the Orbital and plate names) comes from `_row_digest`, never the clock, so a different quote is a different signal. Chrome text is white or yellow, never blue: panel blue on black is the lowest-contrast pair after red and small mono type in it vanishes — blue is kept for rules and blooms. Composed at 800×480 and NEAREST-downsampled (the `metro` convention). Saturation tier `0.7`.

    **Tried and rejected.**
    - *`_CULTURE_NOON` at −40°* put the visible face at 01:20–13:20 and parked the afternoon marker on the hull.
    - *Slicing the Marain block to a row budget* removed exactly the word the block exists to carry — the hour (#268).
  - `orbital` (sky-toned ground / Jura SemiBold on a day-white or night-black card / blue matched phrase by day, yellow in a green aura by night) — **the Culture's Arch, seen from one of the Orbital's plates**. A custom-render frame (`render_orbital_frame`), `culture`'s companion.

    **The Arch is the clock.** A point `phi` round the ring keeps a local time `phi/2π` of a day ahead of ours (`_orbital_arch_day`), so the band is lit plate by plate by each plate's *own* hour: near the horizons it shares our time and the zenith, halfway round, is twelve hours away. At noon the Arch is lit at its feet and dark overhead; at midnight the zenith blazes in daylight across a black sky and the feet are dark — Banks's own image, and a 24-hour dial nobody needs teaching to read.

    **It narrows as it recedes**: a plate `phi` round is `2R sin(phi/2)` away, so the band width falls as `1/sin(phi/2)` (`_orbital_arch_width`). `_orbital_paint_arch` solves each row's two possible x-spans from the ellipse in closed form, so the pass costs the band's area rather than the sky's. The rim walls are a fixed ~1.5 px at every width, so the Arch keeps its outline where its surface is in night — which by day is overhead, where the far side's night reads as a band of navy a shade deeper than the sky.

    **The sky follows the hour** (`_orbital_period`: day / twilight / night on the sun's zenith cosine). Day is a blue-to-pale ramp; twilight a navy haze with a red-into-gold band **weighted toward the sun's side** of the sky (east at dawn, west at dusk, via a separable column weight); night black with a seeded star field.
