Skies are laid by `_orbital_ramp_mask` — a row-density gradient against a tiled `BAYER_8x8` threshold, jittered by `_white_noise`, compared with one saturating `ImageChops.subtract` — because a quarter-million-pixel sky is too many for a per-pixel Python loop.

    **The sun** travels a wide, steep path (`_orbital_sun_xy`) that keeps its disc clear of the quote card at every daylight minute — `TestOrbitalFrame::test_sun_is_never_behind_the_card` sweeps the day — and stands it below the Arch's apex at noon (an Orbital is tilted to its star precisely so the far side does not eclipse noon). The plate itself is a hazy far range over forested hills by day and silhouettes with a cold blue crest by night, lit only by a few settlements that gather along the far slopes — and stay dark at twilight, when nobody has needed a lamp yet.

    **The card flips with the sky**: white with black Jura SemiBold and a solid blue phrase by day (lifted on the `kanagawa` / `pride` shadow ledge), black with white type and `culture`'s yellow-in-green aura phrase by night (floated on a blue field bloom — a drone's field, not a sheet of paper). Its header names the plate and Orbital from the same `_culture_signal` digest and repeats the plate's name in Marain. Composed at 800×480 and NEAREST-downsampled. Saturation tier `0.5` (the white card and the day sky dominate most hours).

    **Tried and rejected.**
    - *A sun path shifted right of the apex* sat behind the card from about 06:30 to 09:00.
  - `furies` (black gallery wall / cadmium-orange R+Y ground / white Libre Franklin wall text / orange matched phrase with a red smear) — **Francis Bacon, *Three Studies for Figures at the Base of a Crucifixion* (1944)**, hung under glass in gilt. A custom-render frame (`render_furies_frame`).

    **The painting.** Named for the Eumenides, the Furies of Aeschylus' *Oresteia*, with which Bacon identified the three figures: left, a hunched draped figure on a table with dark hair hanging over its face; centre, a long-necked figure on a pedestal, eyes bound by a white bandage, the lower face opened into teeth; right, a body on stalk legs in a tuft of grass, neck stretched horizontal to a screaming mouth — all three on the flat cadmium orange Bacon laid on the rough side of Sundeala board, with a few thin R+K perspective lines to put them in a room. Every figure is original procedural drawing, not a reproduction.

    **The theme introduces per-layer separation of a procedurally painted image.** `biomech` already paints its dusk in memory and dithers it, but as *one* image against *one* palette, and the committed plates (`anna_atkins`, `letter`, `daguerreotype`, `autochrome`, `control`) likewise go through a single dither. `furies` paints each panel as layers — ground, perspective lines, pedestal, grass, flesh — each a continuous-tone RGB image with its own soft alpha, and does three things with them. This is printers' plate separation, done for paint.

    1. **Drags the wet paint** (`_furies_drag`): the figure's layer is pasted along a vector in fading steps, each step's alpha multiplied by *bristle striations* — noise held constant along the drag and stepping across it, like the hairs of a loaded brush — so the trail thins unevenly into rows and reads as a smear, not a motion blur. The figure itself goes down last at `keep` (0.9) strength, so the form is smeared rather than ghosted: Bacon's swiped-flesh gesture.
    2. **Separates each layer against its own inks** (`_furies_compose_panel`): Floyd–Steinberg via Pillow's C quantiser, but ground against red+yellow, lines against red+black, flesh and pedestal against white+black+red, grass against green+yellow+black. `TestFuriesFrame::test_each_layer_is_separated_against_its_own_inks` proves a single flattened pass fails (see below). The ground deliberately gets **no black at all** — with black available FS reaches for it in every deeper patch of the scumble, and a black speck beside a yellow one reads olive; the lines that need black are their own layer.
    3. **Composites through a dithered alpha** (`_furies_dithered_alpha`): each soft alpha is thresholded against the `BAYER_8x8` tile instead of cut at 50%, so a smeared edge becomes a stippled interpenetration of flesh and orange — paint dragged into paint — instead of a sticker edge (half alpha takes exactly 32 of 64 cells; pinned).

    **Ground grain.** The ground carries a seeded `randbytes` grain, because FS over a perfectly flat colour settles into vertical worms (`effect_noise` would do it too but is unseeded).

    **The figures are modelled, not flat.** `_shade_silhouette` lights each silhouette from the house upper-left: where the shape meets its own down-right shift it has an exposed upper-left edge (lit rim), where it meets its up-left shift the lower-right core shadow, both blurred so the modelling rolls round the form. `_furies_brushwork` then sweeps curved strokes in greys and a few pinks through the silhouette *before* the drag, so the marks smear with the flesh. Silhouettes are Catmull-Rom splines (`_catmull_rom`) and necks and legs tapering tubes along one (`_furies_tube`), because a figure built from `ImageDraw` ellipses reads as a snowman. The bandage and both mouths — maw, throat, two rows of teeth — go down *after* the drag, because they are the focal points Bacon kept sharp.

    **Under glass, in gilt** — Bacon insisted on both. Each panel sits in an 8 px bevelled moulding (`_furies_paint_frames`): a yellow-major gold flat (12/64 red), a one-pixel Y+W lit face top-left, an R+K shaded face bottom-right and a black rebate. A window reflection (`_furies_paint_glass`) crosses all three panes as a sparse white stipple along a shallow diagonal — one reflection on three sheets of glass, so it must be continuous across the gutters (the test requires every pane).

    **The quote is the wall text; the matched phrase is the scream.** White Libre Franklin Medium on the black wall via `wrap_quote_into_masks`, prose pasted from a thresholded mask. The time phrase is the painting's orange — R+Y at 24/64 red, the yellow-major split, because on black the even mix goes rust — and the *same* `_furies_drag` pulls a red smear off it down and to the right. The smear keeps a two-pixel berth round every glyph of the phrase (a `MaxFilter` dilation), so it trails rather than blots, and is written only onto black, so it can never cut prose; `test_smear_never_touches_the_prose_or_the_phrase` pins that neutering it only ever removes red-on-black.

    **A painting carries no clock**: `time_str` is `del`-asserted (the `daguerreotype` / `autochrome` rule), pinned byte-identical across every time. The triptych is quote-independent and deterministic, so it is composed once per process and cached; the paste lives inside `_furies_paint_triptych` so the decoration fence's neutering still removes it. No blue anywhere — the painting has none, and the per-layer separation gives error diffusion no route to it (pinned). Composed at the canonical 800×480 and NEAREST-downsampled (the `metro` convention). **Judge it on panel inks, not an RGB preview**: panel red is dark (~#62201E), so the cadmium ground reads as a deep ochre-orange on the device where the screenshot shows fire orange. Saturation tier `0.7`.

    **Tried and rejected.**
    - *A single FS pass over the flattened panel.* Grey flesh sits between R+G and W+K, so the naive pass leaks green into the figures, and it scatters black into the orange.
    - *A 3/8 red split for the gilt flat* read as a second orange next to the ground, not as gilt.
    - *A steeper glass reflection* touched only the left pane.
    - *A smear with no berth round the phrase* filled counters and the gaps between words until "half past two" read as one red smudge.
  - `bosch` (sky-blue / meadow-green / rose / black Hell / cream banderole, Grenze Gotisch) — **Hieronymus Bosch, *The Garden of Earthly Delights* (c. 1490–1510)**, the triptych standing open. A custom-render frame (`render_bosch_frame`).

    **A different triptych from `furies`.** `furies` is Bacon's three flat panels hung under glass in gilt on a gallery wall, a *secular* triptych with no hinges; this is an altarpiece opened on its hinges, which is why its wings carry the halved centre arch.

    **The panels.** Paradise on the left wing (the pink Gothic Fountain of Life rising from its pool, an owl watching from the dark orb at its foot, the dragon tree, a flowering rose bush), the Garden across the centre panel (the far lake with its rose spires and spiked blue globes, the pool of bathers ringed by the cavalcade of riders, a goldfinch and a kingfisher, and the giant fruit of the foreground — two strawberries, the lovers' glass bubble, cherries), Hell on the right wing (a city burning, the knife between two ears with its letter M, the Tree-Man on rotten trunk legs under his disc hat and bagpipe, the frozen lake with boats locked in the ice). Each wing is painted on its own tile (`_bosch_paint_paradise` / `_garden` / `_hell`) and set into a dark oak frame with a Y+R gilt fillet and hinge straps.

    **The quote is lettered on a phylactery banderole** (`_bosch_paint_scroll`) — the device Bosch wrote on ("Cave cave Deus videt" circles the pupil of the *Seven Deadly Sins* tabletop): a fluttering cream parchment with rolled ends and a cast shadow, sized to its text (the `pride` card rule) and centred on the centre panel, in black Grenze Gotisch with the matched phrase **rubricated** in solid red — which is what red ink was *for* in a fifteenth-century hand, marking the passage not to be missed.

    **Craquelure is the new technique** (`paint_craquelure`, a shared primitive). Every other texture in the renderer is a *ground*; a five-hundred-year-old oil on oak is instead *crazed*, and the net is what says "old painting" before any motif does. It is built as a **graph**, not a per-pixel field: a jittered lattice whose right and down edges are drawn as midpoint-bowed polylines, `drop` of them left out so islands merge into larger irregular blocks, `diagonal` of the cells gaining a corner-to-corner split for the Y-junctions that stop it reading as a grid — connected by construction, which a thresholded noise field is not (the `abyssal` caustics lesson). The cells are wider than tall because oak-panel craquelure runs across the grain of vertical planks. Along the net a positional hash keeps `continuity` of every 8 pixels so the fissures read as fine and broken.

    **A crack changes polarity with the paint it crosses:** on a light passage it has filled with grime and paints `dark`; on a dark one it has opened to the white chalk ground and paints `light`, but only `light_share` (0.15) of the time, because a chalk line is brighter than a grime line is dark and full strength lays a white net over Hell. **Cracks never cut a letter**: the frame draws the banderole's lettering a second time into an `"L"` mask, and `keep_out` dilates it so a fissure stops short of every stroke — the quote is the clock, and a crack through a glyph is a crack through the time. `TestBoschFrame` pins the lettering byte-identical with and without the crack pass, while requiring the banderole to be crazed away from it.

    **The wing tops are the centre arch halved and mirrored** (`_bosch_arch_tops`). A triptych closes: each wing covers half the centre panel, so its top is half the centre's segmental arch with the apex at the wing's free edge — and opened, that free edge swings outermost, so an open wing is high at the outside and low at the hinge. The wings here are narrower than half the centre (the quote needs the width), so the half-arch is compressed, but the direction is the physical one.

    **Colour** is a single `BAYER_8x8` read per pixel partitioned into as many inks as the passage needs (`_bosch_pick` — `pride`'s rule), with the rank jittered so a broad sky or meadow lays no lattice (the `bakelite` moulding recipe). The jittered rank field is built **once per process** from a seeded `Random.randbytes` (`_bosch_rank_field`) rather than a `position_noise` call per pixel — ~450k reads a render, and the call was a fifth of the render time (`tarot`'s vellum made the same move). It is **clamped to 0..63**, fenced by `test_parchment_carries_no_stray_red`, and it is built into a local and published whole, since preview threads may race to build it. The meadow, every panel's largest passage, is inlined with separable trig. Render ~0.55 s warm. Flesh (`_bosch_flesh`) is light orange (W+R+Y) run white-major, about 68 : 16 : 16, shading to black, because Bosch's nudes are a cool pallor, not a peach.

    **Hell burns yellow**: red over black is the lowest-contrast pair the panel's inks offer, so the blaze is yellow-hot at the root with only its tips red, blooming a tangerine glow (`paint_neon_mask`'s split glow) into the black above the city — fenced as yellow-dominant, because a red fire measures as a dark smudge on calibrated inks.

    **No clock surface beyond the phrase**: an altarpiece carries no hour, so `time_str` is `del`-asserted (the `pride` / `nocturne` posture), pinned byte-identical across every time. Surfaces all six inks. Composed at the canonical 800×480 and NEAREST-downsampled (the `metro` convention). Saturation tier `0.5`.

    **Tried and rejected.**
    - *Chalk cracks at full strength* laid a white net over Hell.
    - *An unclamped rank jitter* put ~4% of ranks either side of the tile, so a share of 0 still painted its ink and the parchment's edge-darkening test fired on the scroll's centreline, flecking red behind the rubricated phrase.
  - `semiotic` (black bulkhead / yellow+black hazard stripes / Cobb-red sign frames / white placard, Barlow Condensed) — **Ron Cobb's Semiotic Standard**, the signage he drew for the *Nostromo* in *Alien* (1979). A custom-render frame (`render_semiotic_frame`).

    **The signs are the real set, not redrawn.** `idle_hours/assets/semiotic_signs.png` is a 6-column sprite sheet (250×262 per sign, legend order 001–030 with 020A–C and 029A) built by `scripts/ingest_semiotic_signs.py` from [LouH's vector adaptation](https://github.com/louh/semiotic-standard) (CC BY 4.0, after Cobb's drawings and Brandon Gamm's recreations), fetched from a pinned commit; attribution and the changes made live in `idle_hours/assets/semiotic/README.md`, which ships with the sheet. The adaptation is flat colour in seven values, and every one has a home on the panel — Cobb red, black, white, amber, navy and dark green each snap to one native ink, and the mid grey of the "system" signs is the K+W 50/50 stipple — so the signs need a *classification*, not a dither.

    **Classify after resizing, never before** (the `ingest_tarot_plates` rule): the sheet stays antialiased, each tile is resized to the size it is painted at, composited onto the black ground it sits on, and only then snapped with `quantize` against a fixed palette. **Each sign is classified only against the source values it actually uses** (`_semiotic_sign_values`, anything covering ≥0.5% of the master, plus black for the ground), fenced by `TestSemioticFrame::test_sign_seams_stay_on_the_signs_own_inks`. The grey stipple is phased on absolute canvas coordinates so adjacent grey fields share one checkerboard.

    **Composition.** Hazard-stripe bands across the head and foot; a header with the Standard's name, four status lamps (blue / green / yellow / red — which is also what guarantees all six inks at every hour) and the section number; the featured sign large at the left with its legend number and name; three companion signs beneath it; and the quote on a crew-notice placard framed the way Cobb framed a sign — white edge, red band, white panel — with the band broken where the header and footer rules cross it. Black Barlow Condensed prose and a solid-red matched phrase are pasted from thresholded masks (`wrap_quote_into_masks`), the attribution fitted along the foot. The notice's reference number is `_row_digest`-derived.

    **The hour is the section**: the featured sign comes from `_SEMIOTIC_HOUR_SIGNS` (twelve distinct signs) and the header reads `SECTION 07` — a location on the ship, which is what the Standard is for. Hour only, the `cardcatalog` / `observation` posture, pinned byte-identical across every minute; the three companions come from `_row_digest`, so a different quote is a different stretch of corridor. A missing sheet degrades to blank red-framed panels. Composed at the canonical 800×480 and NEAREST-downsampled (the `metro` convention). Saturation tier `0.7`.

    **Tried and rejected.**
    - *Classifying every sign against all seven source values* let the bulkhead door's black/white seam average onto the dark green and speckle it.

    **Its own sleep frame** (`render_semiotic_sleep`, the theme's `spec.sleep`): **HYPERSLEEP**, the Nostromo's crew asleep, read as a ship at rest rather than an alarm. The same bulkhead, hazard bands and placard frame as the quote frame; the header reads STATUS STASIS with only the blue and green lamps lit. The featured sign is Cobb's own **004 CRYOGENIC VAULT**, painted from the CC BY sheet exactly as the 04:00 quote frame paints it (`TestSemioticSleepFrame` checks the two panels are pixel-identical): it is what the film's hypersleep vault carried. A hypersleep pod drawn in code stood there first and was replaced at review, since the Standard already has the sign and the real one keeps the provenance simple. The companions are calm signs from the sheet: 021 LIFE SUPPORT SYSTEM, 025 AUTODOC and 030 COMPUTER TERMINAL (MOTHER, flying the ship). The placard is text only, in black on white and centred between its rules: HYPERSLEEP / DO NOT DISTURB, then CREW IN STASIS, ALL SEVEN CREW ACCOUNTED FOR, LIFE SUPPORT NOMINAL · SHIP AT REST, and AUTOPILOT ENGAGED · WAKE ON ARRIVAL. A row of seven small drawn pods, one per crew member, sat above the status line at first. It was removed at review: the frame reads more like the Standard with every pictogram one of Cobb's. **Provenance:** every sign on the frame comes unedited from LouH's CC BY 4.0 sheet. Nothing on it tells the time; composed at 800×480 and NEAREST-downsampled.
  - `atropos` (black night / teal B+G fog dithered to the cold inks / white Saira body / tangerine matched phrase, Michroma HUD) — **Housemarque's *Returnal* (2021)**, night in the Overgrown Ruins of Atropos. A custom-render frame (`render_atropos_frame`).

    **The premise.** Selene Vassos crash-lands on a planet that kills her and starts her again, and every cycle she walks the same rain-soaked ruins of a civilisation whose statues are taller than her ship; the page is one of those nights, with the HUD laid over it and the quote as a **xenoglyph cipher** the scout has just translated.

    **Two passes, two palettes: the night.** The night is painted in continuous tone — a per-row gradient from a black zenith to a lit teal horizon, fog banked by stretched seeded noise, pools of lit fog behind the near Sentient and round the wreck, rain as hundreds of slanted streaks, a far skyline of monoliths, the ring gate half sunk with the fog brighter through it, layered stone pillars, the *Helios* on its nose with its cabin windows lit, two Sentient statues (the long crested skull swept back, sloped shoulders, arms hanging to elongated hands) in atmospheric perspective with a teal rim where the fog catches the near one, the cipher slab, and the wet plain reflecting the horizon in vertical streaks. It is then **Floyd–Steinberg-dithered to black / blue / green / white only**, the `biomech` recipe inverted: red and yellow are left out of the quantiser so error diffusion can never warm the night. The teal is the B+G mix at a density that *falls* with the fog rather than a fixed ratio, which is why it is dithered rather than stippled. The black tendrils (`_atropos_tendril_paths`, a seeded random walk per root pulled toward a target, tapering stems with a rim pass and side shoots) are painted into the same scene.

    **Two passes, two palettes: the lights.** Everything that glows is laid on afterwards through `paint_neon_mask` with `ground` pinned to the three cold inks: the ember nodules on the tendrils, the *Helios* beacon and the xenotech seams on the Sentient's crest and chest are a solid yellow core in a red + yellow halo; the volley of orbs — a different pattern per quote, seeded from `_row_digest` — are yellow with a white-hot centre in the same halo, or white in an R+B violet one, each with a fading trail; the matched phrase is the tangerine recipe proper (red with yellow at 3/8).

    **The scene lights carry yellow at 1/2 rather than 3/8**: on the calibrated panel red is nearly black, and a halo five-eighths red reads as a shadow round the core, not as light; the phrase keeps the 3/8 recipe because it sits on pure black where the hue is what matters.

    **The cipher is the quote in alien script.** `_atropos_glyph` builds a 26-letter alphabet once from a fixed seed — three to five strokes on a 3×4 lattice plus an optional dot, the angular carved register of the game's glyph walls — and the slab carries the matched phrase and then the quote letter for letter, so the same letter is always the same glyph; the strokes are 1 px white, one ink, which survives the snap where a two-ink stipple at that size would not.

    **The quote** sits in the black zenith as white Saira over a black halo (the `biomech` answer — a halo, not a panel, so the scene stays visible), inside corner brackets labelled `XENOGLYPH CIPHER` / `TRANSLATED`, with the attribution as the cipher's source line.

    **The HUD**: the biome title top-left, an integrity bar and adrenaline pips bottom-left whose readings are seeded from the quote (a different quote is a different run), and a `HELIOS UPLINK — NO SIGNAL` readout bottom-right.

    **The time is the cycle counter, hour only** — `CYCLE 07` top-right, the way the game's scout logs are numbered; a designation a reader parses as a run, not a clock (the `cardcatalog` due-hour / `observation` camera posture). The minute stays with the matched phrase, pinned byte-identical across the minutes of an hour by `TestAtroposFrame`. Nothing reads the wall clock.

    **Caching and housekeeping.** The scene is quote-independent and cached once per process (`_ATROPOS_BACKGROUND`, keyed on the painters themselves, the `biomech` pattern, so the decoration fence measures a painter and not a cache). Surfaces all six inks (pinned). Custom frames never draw the debug banner, so the HUD's top-right counter needs no `debug_label_inset`. Composed at the canonical 800×480 and NEAREST-downsampled (the `metro` convention). Saturation tier `0.7`.
  - `expedition` (black zenith / dusk dithered against the calibrated inks / white IM Fell Double Pica body / painted-yellow Fell-italic matched phrase) — **Sandfall Interactive's *Clair Obscur: Expedition 33* (2025)**, the Monolith seen from the Lumière promenade. A custom-render frame (`render_expedition_frame`).

    **The premise.** Once a year the Paintress wakes and paints a number on the Monolith across the water, and everyone of that age turns to petals; the page is the view every citizen of Lumière lives under — the promenade's balustrade and a Belle Époque gas lamp in the foreground, the dusk sea, and on the islet the Monolith with the Paintress seated beside it, brush raised, and the number she has just painted is the **hour**.

    **Clair-obscur, literally:** black silhouettes against one warm light. The scene is painted in continuous tone — a per-row dusk from a blue-black zenith through violet and rose to an amber horizon, a radial glow pooled behind the Monolith, a vignette keeping the journal's corner dark, the sea with shimmer streaks and the glow's broken reflection, every silhouette (the slab with a rim of the glow down each edge and the rocks of its islet, the Paintress with her hair down her back and a raised knee, the Continent's coast hazed on the far horizon, the balustrade's vase balusters lit toward the lamp, the lamp's post, bracket curls and lantern cage), and a brush-stroke facture multiplied over all of it — then Floyd–Steinberg-dithered.

    **Dithered against the calibrated inks** (`_dither_calibrated`): every tone is specified in the panel's measured colour space and the quantiser is handed those measured colours, so diffusion decides how much red a violet needs against what red actually looks like on the panel — nearly black — and the chosen indices are re-labelled with the nominal inks afterwards. The sky is quantised without green, the sea with it, so the lit water can be the B+G teal of the recipe table.

    **The number is paint** (`_expedition_numeral_mask`). Bebas Neue — the game's own numeral face, and a tall narrow stroke is what the Paintress's number looks like on the slab — is roughed into a brushed stroke with bristle gaps carved by a streaked noise, edges that swell and bite by a second noise's threshold, drips run from its lowest points and a flick beside it. It is painted through `paint_neon_mask` as a yellow core with white at 1/4 in a red + yellow halo at 1/2 (the `atropos` scene-light argument: a 5/8-red halo reads as shadow on the panel), with its reflection flipped over the horizon, broken by the shimmer and faded with depth as a two-ink density stipple (`_expedition_stipple_field`); the Paintress's brush tip reaches into the halo, so she is painting it.

    **The petals are the Gommage**: a gust rises from the promenade, crosses the water below the journal page and streams past the Paintress to the number — a cubic path, each petal a teardrop polygon filled with the R+W pink stipple (pink is the only way a petal reads against the night, since panel red is nearly black), a white rim toward the light and a red one away from it, sizes falling along the path so the near ones are the viewer's and the far ones motes, seeded from `_row_digest` (a different quote is a different gust) and kept out of the page, the byline, the wordmark, the credo and the number.

    **The chroma**: `paint_flow_strokes` through a field that is the tangent of circles about the number mixed with a wind — the swirl the Paintress's work leaves in the sky — blue and a few white strokes in the cold sky, red and yellow flecks near the glow.

    **The quote** is the journal page: white Fell roman over a black halo (a halo, not a panel, so the dusk stays visible) with the matched phrase in Fell italic and the number's own paint, so the readable time and the painted one are the same fact, and the byline in gold Bebas capitals beneath. `CLAIR OBSCUR` in Bebas over `EXPEDITION 33` in spaced Cinzel Decorative capitals top-left, over a gold hairline with a diamond — the ornament the game's whole interface is drawn in — and the expedition's credo at the foot; gold is solid yellow, since small text in a stipple shreds.

    **Time surfaces:** the number is hour-only, pinned byte-identical across the minutes of an hour by `TestExpeditionFrame`; the minute stays with the matched phrase; nothing prints digits beyond the hour and nothing reads the wall clock. The scene is quote- and hour-independent and cached once per process (`_EXPEDITION_BACKGROUND`, keyed on the painters themselves, the `atropos` / `biomech` pattern). Surfaces all six inks (pinned). Composed at the canonical 800×480 and NEAREST-downsampled (the `metro` convention). Saturation tier `0.7`.

    **Tried and rejected.**
    - *Designing the dusk in nominal RGB*: it looked right on a monitor and went to mud on the panel.
  - `witcher` (cream deckled parchment / dark binding / black Barlow Condensed body / tangerine matched phrase — **CD Projekt Red's *The Witcher 3: Wild Hunt* (2015)**, a bestiary page under the meditation dial). A custom-render frame (`render_witcher_frame`).

    **The object.** Geralt's journal is the game's reading surface: a worn page in a dark leather binding, an entry headed in condensed capitals over a thin red rule, an opening quotation attributed to whoever said it, and a line of what the creature is susceptible to at the foot. The page is one of those entries: the book is the entry, the quote its epigraph, the Gutenberg ID its entry number (real data, not an invented catalogue figure).

    **The page** is the `tarot` vellum recipe (Y+W cream under a sparse R+G foxing) inside a deckled edge: the page mask's outer band is eaten by a seeded noise so the binding shows through a torn margin rather than a ruled one. The binding is black with a sparse red fleck, which is how six inks spell dark brown. Rules with notched corners and a red header rule with diamond ends are the game's thin-line chrome.

    **The meditation dial is the time carrier, hour-only**: two engraved rings with a hatched band between them, forty-eight ticks with every fourth one major, a pointer on the tick train and one marker on the hour's radius — a sun by day, a crescent by night (which one is the 24-hour hour, still hour-only, pinned byte-identical across the minutes of an hour by `TestWitcherFrame`). The minute stays with the matched phrase and nothing prints a digit.

    **The hub carries three claw slashes** after the III of the game's logotype rather than the wolf's head. Each slash is a blade pointed at both ends and widest a third of the way down, with the spike that trails it below the cut; the outer two are the same blade scaled down and swung outward about the hub. Red outlined in black, which on the panel is dried blood on parchment — the right thing to find on a bestiary page.

    **The quote** is the entry's epigraph in Barlow Condensed Medium, ragged right, with the matched phrase Bold in the interface's red-orange (the R+Y tangerine stipple) and the author beneath it. The five signs — Aard, Igni, Yrden, Quen, Axii — are drawn as icons at the foot, and the ones this entry is "susceptible to" are filled in their in-game colours (Aard blue, Igni red, Yrden blue with red spokes, Quen gold, Axii red), chosen by `_row_digest`, which is also where the page's blue comes from.

    **Plumbing.** The page is quote- and hour-independent and cached once per process (`_WITCHER_PAGE`, keyed on the painters). Nothing reads the wall clock. Surfaces all six inks (pinned). Composed at the canonical 800×480 and NEAREST-downsampled (the `metro` convention). Saturation tier `0.5`.

    **Tried and rejected.** The Wolf School emblem in the hub: it is CD Projekt's mark, and a polygon redraw of it at 96 px read as rough. Plain Roman bars for the III read as a numeral and nothing else.

    **Its own sleep frame** (`render_witcher_sleep`, the theme's `spec.sleep`): the game's meditation screen, where Geralt kneels and the player drags the dial's hand round to the hour to wake. It uses the bestiary page's binding, deckled parchment and rules. The header reads `MEDITATION` over **MEDITATE UNTIL DAWN**, and the epigraph slot holds Geralt at rest, with "first light" in the matched phrase's tangerine. The dial grows to a 24-hour plate, as in the game: noon at the top with the sun, midnight at the foot with the crescent, a tick per half hour, and the engraved band hatched lighter by day and heavier by night. The hand, a red blade outlined in black, always stands on dawn. The hour the panel went quiet is a black pointer outside the ring, and the band between that hour and dawn is lit in R+Y tangerine at 3/8 yellow. That lit arc is the stretch the player chose to rest through, so the frame reads as asleep until morning and not as a stopped clock. The claw slashes sit at the hub, a little smaller than on the quote frame so the hand clears them. The hour comes from `_witcher_hour`, which is hour-only and falls back to noon on a malformed time, and every minute of an hour renders byte-identically (`TestWitcherSleepFrame`). The foot swaps the sign icons for what a night's meditation gives back (`VITALITY RESTORED ◆ POTIONS REFILLED`). Nothing prints a digit or reads the wall clock. The frame is composed at 800×480 and NEAREST-downsampled. **Tried and rejected:** a 12-hour dial reusing the quote frame's plate. It cannot put the sun and moon where the game does, and the arc to dawn would wrap ambiguously. Also rejected: a `·` separator in the foot, which Archivo Narrow drew as nothing at 12 px, so the separator is a red diamond.
  - `hades` (dithered night / black boon card with a blue fleck / gold meander frieze / white Spectral body / gold matched phrase, Caesar Dressing title — **Supergiant Games' *Hades II* (2025)**, a boon at the Crossroads under the moon). A custom-render frame (`render_hades_frame`).

    **The object.** Melinoë's war on Time is fought at night from the witches' camp Hecate keeps at the edge of the Underworld, and every offer the gods make is a boon card: a dark panel in a gold frame, the god's portrait in a medallion at the left, the god's name in textured capitals across the top, the description beneath with its key words lit in colour. The page is one of those cards — the author is the god, the quote the boon, the time the lit words.

    **The moon is the hour**: twelve phases for twelve hours, full at twelve, new at six, first quarter at nine, last quarter at three (`_hades_phase`). The terminator is the standard construction, `x = cos(2πp)·√(1−y²)` on the unit disc, and the unlit limb keeps a thin blue rim so a new moon is still a moon and not a hole in the sky. Hour-only, pinned byte-identical across the minutes of an hour by `TestHadesFrame`; the minute stays with the matched phrase.

    **The sky is painted in continuous tone and dithered** (the `expedition` posture): a per-row gradient from a black-violet zenith through the panel's blue to a teal horizon, the moon's halo and a green witch-glow under each brazier pooled on it with blurred masks, a stretched noise for the facture, then Floyd–Steinberg against the calibrated inks and re-labelled nominal.

    **Two quantisers**, neither with red or yellow. The upper sky is quantised to black, blue and white, without green — with green, the grain tipped the zenith's black-violet into green specks across the whole sky — and the horizon band with green added, composited through a feathered mask so the seam is a drift of green into the blue rather than a rule.

    **After the dither.** Stars, the ridge (black earth to the foot of the frame, cypresses, two broken columns) and Hecate's torches go on after the dither so they stay crisp. Each torch is a gold tripod and bowl with a flame that is a white core wrapped in green under `paint_neon_mask` — witchfire. The earth is black, with the same sparse blue fleck as the card, and the card's gold rule is what separates them.

    **The card** is black with a blue fleck (midnight in six inks) inside a double gold rule with a bossed diamond at each corner and a **Greek-key frieze** at the head and foot: `_hades_meander`, one continuous line on a 3 px grid, a baseline with a square spiral every twelve pixels. The medallion holds **Chronos's hourglass** — the Titan of Time the game is about killing is the one god a clock could take a boon from — in gold rings over a blue bloom, the sand a yellow with a one-in-four red lattice so it reads amber rather than lemon, `CHRONOS` beneath in the chrome face.

    **Rarity is a roll**, as a boon's is: `_hades_rarity` takes `_row_digest` mod 100 against the game's rough odds (Common / Rare / Epic / Heroic / Legendary at 50 / 28 / 14 / 6 / 2), so a different quote is a different draw and the same quote always the same. The pips are filled in the rarity's colour, with purple spelled as a blue pip in a red outline.

    **Plumbing.** The scene is quote- and hour-independent and cached once per process (`_HADES_SCENE`, keyed on the painters). Composed at the canonical 800×480 and NEAREST-downsampled (the `metro` convention). Saturation tier `0.7`.

    **Tried and rejected.** Letting the sky's teal run on below the ridge: the card sat in a green-blue field, so the earth was made black.
  - `expanse` (black glass / blue hairline panels with cut corners / white Barlow body / MCRN-orange matched phrase, Share Tech Mono readouts and system text — ***The Expanse* (2015–2022)**, the Rocinante's console). A custom-render frame (`render_expanse_frame`).

    **The object.** The show's screen graphics (HUDS+GUIS) are dense grids of small modules on dark glass: a plot of the system across the top with the stations named in column heads over vertical rules and the trajectories curving through them; rows of concentric arc gauges with partial sweeps; stacked status pills in green, red and amber; a camera feed in a thin frame with corner brackets; a boxed list of contacts with their silhouettes; waveform charts over dotted baselines; and under every module a few lines of tiny `//` system text, because the ship was built for her crew. The page is one of those displays.

    **Top:** the ship's tag (the service as an orange block, the name, the class) and the **orbital strip** — Luna, Mars, Ceres, Tycho, Ganymede and Saturn as column heads, a dashed blue track and a solid yellow one curving through, a banded amber gas giant with the Roci's marker beside it, a ringed planet, a moon.

    **Left:** the **tactical plot** — three range rings as arcs gapped at the cardinals, thirty-six bearing ticks with the cardinals in the mono, a gapped crosshair, a tick ruler down the edge, the Roci as a white chevron, two unknowns as hollow triangles — and a row of four **arc gauges** (`RCS` / `PWR` / `O2` / `TMP`) beneath, a cyan sweep on the outer ring and an orange one on the inner.

    **Middle:** the **comms feed** — the sender in Barlow Bold capitals in orange, the quote in a thin blue frame with white corner brackets and a tick scale along its foot, the book as the source, signal bars, and a command line with the transmission ID and an orange cursor.

    **Right:** the **contacts list** — boxed rows with a silhouette, a name and a dotted leader (the Roci, the contact as an orange diamond, two unknowns) — and a **drive scatter box** with a crosshair and a seeded scatter under `DRIVE 01`. **Foot:** a grid of twelve status pills in four labelled rows, a `DSR` waveform chart filled to its dotted baseline, three lines of system text with two boxed IDs and an `ARMED` flag.

    **The hour is a bearing:** the plot's one tracked contact sits on the outer ring at the hour's place on a clock face ("contact, two o'clock" is how a pilot has always called one), an orange diamond in brackets with a white core and an amber bloom (`paint_neon_mask`, yellow with a red minor share, grounded on black and blue). Its flyby track is dashed through it in blue and the intercept dashed in orange from the Roci; the contact's row in the list names the same hour in degrees (`BRG 060`). Hour-only, pinned byte-identical across the minutes of an hour by `TestExpanseFrame`; the frame never reads the wall clock.

    **Colour is the ship's.** The MCRN orange is the R+Y 50/50 amber (`gothic`'s recipe, for the same reason — on a black ground the red-biased tangerine goes dim) on the matched phrase, the sender, the header leads, the lit pills, the inner gauge sweeps, the gas giant and the cursor. The gauge sweeps, the chart area and the signal bars are the B+W sky-blue checker, which at panel distance is the console's cyan. Hairlines — rings, rules, frames, dotted leaders, the sparse dot grid on the glass (pitch 23, coprime with both Bayer tiles) — are solid blue, since a hairline cannot carry a stipple. Pills and flags are green for nominal and red for armed or empty; the running text is white.

    **Red is never a hairline and green and red are never text:** on the panel both are a shade off black, so a status is a coloured pill beside white text, never coloured text. All six inks, nothing synthesised the frame does not need.

    **The ship's state is the quote's:** `_row_digest` deals the four gauge sweeps, the twelve pills, the waveform's seeded walk, the signal bars, the transmission ID and the boxed IDs, so a different quote is a different moment aboard and the same quote is always the same one. None of those digits is derived from the time; the bearing is the only number that is.

    **Plumbing.** The chrome — ground, tag, orbital strip, the three panel frames, the plot, the list, the module labels and system text — is quote- and hour-independent and cached once per process (`_EXPANSE_SCENE`, keyed on the painters). Composed at the canonical 800×480 and NEAREST-downsampled (the `metro` convention). Saturation tier `0.7`.
  - `beksinski` (dust-coloured dithered haze / umber dithered plain / black bone cathedral with a rust rim / black Old Standard TT body / red matched phrase / bone-white byline — **Zdzisław Beksiński's fantastic period (c. 1964–1983)**, a procession across a dead plain toward a cathedral of bone). A custom-render frame (`render_beksinski_frame`).

    **The object.** Beksiński painted in oils on hardboard, never titled a work, and said he wanted to paint as if photographing dreams. The paintings of this period share one world: a plain under a hazy, dust-coloured sky with a dim sun behind it, a vast structure on the horizon grown rather than built, with pointed openings the light comes through and roots running into the ground, and emaciated hooded figures walking toward it in a line. The page is that painting: the quote is set in the haze, where his skies are the only light, and the time is read off the plain.

    **The procession is the hour**: one figure per hour, led from the cathedral's foot back along the road, so a single walker stands at one and a file of twelve at twelve, and the leader never moves (`_beksinski_paint_figures`; every figure's jitter is drawn whether or not it walks today, so the ones that do stand where they always stand). Hour-only, pinned byte-identical across the minutes of an hour by `TestBeksinskiFrame`; the minute stays with the matched phrase. Each figure is a hooded cloak with a ragged hem and a forward stoop, black, with a one-pixel bone-white line down its edge toward the cathedral — the glow catching it — which, with the ash-pale road it walks on, is what keeps a black figure legible on the dark plain.

    **The sky and the plain are painted in continuous tone and dithered** (the `expedition` / `hades` posture): a per-row gradient from a smoky umber zenith to a bone-ochre glow at the horizon, a horizontal streak and a broad smudge of seeded noise for the scraped-oil facture, the sun's glow pooled behind the left spires so the cathedral is seen against the light, a vignette that lets the edges fall into the dark, then Floyd–Steinberg against the calibrated inks and re-labelled nominal.

    **No green and no blue anywhere**: the period's palette is umber, rust, ochre and bone, and with green admitted the plain's umber came out of the quantiser as red-and-green confetti (the `hades` green-speck failure, on the ground instead of the sky). The umber is red and black with a yellow fleck, the haze white and yellow with a black grain, the rim light solid red; the plain is lighter at the horizon than at the foot, the one depth cue a flat dither keeps.

    **The cathedral** is an `L` mask (`_beksinski_tower_mask`): a mound, six tapering spires with ragged seeded edges and a bulge along their length so they read as bone rather than as cones, pinnacles clustered round each, buttresses that run from the body down into the plain as tendons, pointed-arch windows and an oculus knocked through so the haze shows through the bone. It goes into the continuous-tone scene before the dither as a near-black grained body with a **rim light** — the mask minus itself offset down-right, blurred — in rust on the faces that look toward the sun. Roots drip from the foundation into the plain, and a low ruin stands far off on the left horizon in the haze's own colour, which is what makes the plain wide.

    **After the dither** `paint_craquelure` crazes the plain and the bone with a sparse bone-white net, the cracked ground of every one of these paintings; the sky is left alone and the byline's footprint is kept out of the net.

    **Plumbing.** The mask is built by the assembly and handed to the painter rather than returned by it, so the decoration fence's neutered painter cannot take the mask down with it. The scene is quote- and hour-independent and cached once per process (`_BEKSINSKI_SCENE`, keyed on the painters). Composed at the canonical 800×480 and NEAREST-downsampled (the `metro` convention). Saturation tier `0.5`: the haze is two thirds of the canvas.
  - `goya` (dithered ochre void / black slope / black Libre Baskerville body / red matched phrase, Prado label — **Francisco de Goya's *Pinturas negras* (1819–1823)**, *El Perro*). A custom-render frame (`render_goya_frame`).

    **The object.** Goya painted the fourteen Black Paintings straight onto the plaster of the Quinta del Sordo, for nobody, untitled and unsigned; they were cut from the walls and transferred to canvas in 1874 and hang in the Prado's room 67. *The Dog* is the barest of them — a dog's head looking up out of a dark slope into a void that fills five-sixths of the picture — and nobody knows what it is looking at. Here it is looking at the time.

    **The palette is the paintings'**: bone black, umber, yellow ochre, a dirty lead white and one red (the blood on Saturn's son), so the quantiser gets black, yellow, red and white and never blue or green.

    **The void is painted in continuous tone and dithered** (the `expedition` posture, via `_dither_calibrated`): a per-row gradient from a dark umber top through the pale ochre passage where the painting's light sits to a deeper ochre over the slope, a cool stain dragged across the upper right, a noise streaked eight to one for the dragged strokes and a finer one for the grain, Floyd–Steinberg against the calibrated inks and re-labelled nominal. The slope is a warm black `(42, 34, 28)` painted into the same scene, so its edge is brushed rather than ruled and its body carries the sparse red-and-yellow fleck that makes it umber rather than ink.

    **The plaster is crazed**: `paint_craquelure` over the whole cached scene at a `(30, 19)` cell — coarser than `bosch`'s panel, as a mural's craze is — dark in the ochre and a sparse yellow in the black, the ground showing through. The quote is painted after the net, so it is never cracked through.

    **The dog looks at the time**: `_goya_gaze` turns the head toward the centroid of the matched phrase as laid out, clamped between a near-level look and a nose well in the air (14°–44°), so the pitch is the quote's; a phrase left of the dog gets the full lift, and a quote with no phrase the painting's own tilt. The head is shaded under an upper-left light (`_shade_silhouette` — the void is brightest at the upper left), the dropped ear darker, dithered in place against the same four inks and crazed in its own pass; the eye, its catchlight and the nostril go on crisp.

    **Black type, not white**: on the calibrated inks the void's luminance sits a third of the way from black to white, which gives white text a contrast ratio near two and black one near six. The matched phrase is Bold in red — the one red in the Black Paintings.

    **The label is the Prado's**: a white card on the slope with the author on the artist's line, the title in italic, the medium (*óleo sobre revoco trasladado a lienzo* — oil on plaster transferred to canvas), the series, and an inventory number in the museum's `P000767` form made from the Gutenberg id, so a different book is a different catalogue entry. It carries no date, because the book's would be wrong and Goya's a lie about the author.

    **Plumbing.** No hour carrier and no wall clock: a painting carries no clock, and `time_str` is deleted at entry. The void, slope and craze are quote-independent and cached once per process (`_GOYA_SCENE`, keyed on the painters). Composed at 800×480 and NEAREST-downsampled (the `metro` convention). Saturation tier `0.7`.
  - `hal` (black ground / solid blue main monitor / white Jost body / yellow matched phrase, Michroma chrome — ***2001: A Space Odyssey* (1968)**, the Discovery One's monitors and HAL 9000). A custom-render frame (`render_hal_frame`).

    **The object.** Kubrick's Discovery is run from banks of small CRTs, each a flat field of one saturated colour with a three-letter mnemonic in its corner naming the subsystem it watches — COM, NAV, VEH, ATM, HIB, GDE, LIF, MEM, DMG, FLX, CNT, NUC — and a few lines of white type or a schematic beneath. The graphics were animated by hand, which is why they are the one sci-fi interface made of solid flats, and why a six-ink panel renders them better than any raster HUD.

    **The page is the main monitor:** a blue field with the active mnemonic in Michroma across its header beside a row of readout bars seeded from the quote, a white rule, the quote in white Jost with the matched phrase Bold in yellow, and the author and title letterspaced along the foot in the monitors' tracked capitals (`draw_tracked`).

    **The hour is which subsystem is up:** the twelve mnemonics run along the foot as tiles in the film's four tile colours in rotation, the hour's tile is the white one, and its mnemonic is the one on the header — byte-identical across the minutes of an hour, with the matched phrase carrying the minute; nothing reads the wall clock.

    **HAL's eye** fills the right column: the blue nameplate (HAL over 9000), the lens — a red disc in a white bezel, a yellow bloom at its centre through `paint_neon_mask` with `ground` pinned to red, a white catchlight, and red light spilled into the black with `ground` pinned to black so the bloom cannot eat the bezel — and beneath it the hibernation monitor's two life traces in a white hairline frame.

    **The glass is what makes a flat read as a tube.** The screen sits in a hairlined housing with its phosphor leaking a blue bloom onto the black between (`paint_neon_mask`, `ground` pinned to black). A raster of one black row in four runs over the blue *ground only* (`_crt_paint_scanlines` — the type and the graphics stay solid, so the screen gains a CRT's texture without the body losing weight). The mnemonic sits in its own title box; the foot tiles are small rounded screens under the same raster; and the right column carries the vehicle monitor's Discovery in white wireframe (command sphere, spine, engine block) above the hibernation traces.

    **Inks.** Every colour is an ink painted solid; the stipples are the blooms, the raster and nothing else. Composed at 800×480 and NEAREST-downsampled otherwise.
  - `lumon` (vignetted blue CRT dithered to blue and black / black bezel / white Montserrat body / yellow matched phrase in a white hover box, Inter file name and byline, Michroma wordmark — ***Severance* (2022–)**, the Macrodata Refinement terminal). A custom-render frame (`render_lumon_frame`).

    **The object.** On the severed floor, refinement is a file named after a town — Siena, Dranesville, Tumwater, Cold Harbor — shown as a field of small white digits on a deep blue screen; some of the numbers feel scary, the refiner boxes the cluster and sweeps it into one of five bins, and the completion percentage in the header creeps up.

    **The screen** is a continuous-tone field, the panel's blue at the centre falling to black at the corners, painted in the calibrated ink space at a quarter of panel resolution, bicubic-upsampled and Floyd–Steinberg dithered to the two inks (`_dither_calibrated`, so the vignette is error-diffused rather than latticed) inside a black rounded bezel; painted once per process (`_LUMON_SCENE`).

    **The header is the file's:** its town, chosen by the quote's digest, in Inter Medium; the Lumon globe and the wordmark in Michroma at the right.

    **The hour is the completion:** the header reads *N% Complete* with N the hour over twelve — one o'clock is 8%, noon and midnight 100%, the day the file is finished. The scary cluster carries it again: in the grid of four rows by twenty-four columns the boxed two-by-two cluster, its digits a size larger and nudged off the grid, sits in the hour's column pair, so the box walks across the twelve hours. Both are byte-identical across the minutes of an hour.

    **The quote is the file's text:** Montserrat Regular in white — the digits' own face extended to a sentence —, the matched phrase Bold in yellow inside one white hairline box per line it occupies (`_lumon_hover_boxes`) — the refiner's hover — the author and title in Inter beneath. The five bins run along the foot, each boxed `00`–`04` with a progress bar whose fill is a blue-and-white stipple at a level seeded from the quote.

    **The housing and the glass:** the terminal's beige case is a white stipple with a yellow quarter around the whole page, the glass is recessed in a black edge the screen's blue bloom leaks onto, and the same one-in-four raster as `hal` crosses the blue ground under the type (`_crt_paint_scanlines`), so the dithered vignette reads as a lit tube rather than a gradient. Composed at 800×480 and NEAREST-downsampled otherwise.
  - `dsky` (dithered grey console / modelled black unit / cream flight-plan card / black Special Elite body / red matched phrase / segments white in a green bloom — **the Apollo Guidance Computer's DSKY (1966–1972)**). A custom-render frame (`render_dsky_frame`).

    **The object.** The display-and-keyboard unit was the crew's face of the AGC: fourteen warning lamps behind smoked glass at the left, an electroluminescent display behind dark glass at the right (PROG, VERB and NOUN in two digits over three signed five-digit registers), nineteen keys below; the crew spoke to it in verb-noun pairs and read the answer off green segments. Real hardware, so nothing is licensed and nothing approximated: the geometry is the Block II unit's.

    **The console is painted in continuous tone and dithered** (`_dither_calibrated`, white and black): the Apollo grey with a horizontal brushed grain, and on it the unit *modelled* rather than drawn — a raised rim shaded under the upper-left light (`_shade_silhouette`), recessed dark-glass windows with a diagonal reflection across the display, the keycaps as near-black domes with a lit edge on a recessed grey tray, screws at the corners, a soft shadow on the panel. Legends, segments and the lit COMP ACTY lamp, solid green, go on crisp after.

    **The flight plan is paper:** a cream card (white with a yellow quarter) clipped to the console with a bulldog clip, its own shadow beneath it in the scene, the quote typed on it in Special Elite — the typewriter the flight plans were cut on — in black with the matched phrase in red the way the pen-and-ink updates were.

    **The hour is the program:** the AGC's major modes were numbered programs and the PROG register shows the one running, here the hour 01–12; VERB 06 NOUN 62 stays up and the registers carry telemetry seeded from the quote.

    **The segments glow:** true seven-segment strokes drawn into a mask (`_dsky_draw_glyph`) and painted white-hot with a green bloom through `paint_neon_mask`, `ground` pinned to black. Composed at 800×480 and NEAREST-downsampled otherwise.

    **Its own sleep frame** (`render_dsky_sleep`, the theme's `spec.sleep`): the computer put to bed for the crew's rest period. The real AGC had a standby mode, entered through **P06** (AGC power down), and a **STBY** lamp in the warning bank that lit while it was in it, so the frame shows the hardware's own sleep rather than a metaphor. The same console, unit and clipped card as the quote frame (`_dsky_scene`). STBY is lit, a solid white face with its word in black (`_dsky_paint_legends(lit=...)`), and COMP ACTY is dark glass with its word in white, because the computer is no longer working. The display shows P06's request to the crew: VERB 50 NOUN 25 ("please perform"), with checklist code 00062 in R1 and R2 and R3 blank. **PROG is left blank on purpose.** P06 would show 06 there, but in the quote frame that register *is* the hour, so a 06 at night would read as six o'clock. The card carries the presleep checklist, typed on dot leaders (`_dsky_paint_checklist`: P06 AGC PWR DOWN / V37E 06E, V50 N25 R1 00062 / PRO, STBY LT / ON), and under it "Crew to their couches. The computer sleeps in standby until the wake-up call from Houston." with *sleeps in standby* in the pen-update red (`_DSKY_SLEEP_ROW`). **Two liberties, both deliberate:** on the real unit the display blanked once PRO put the computer into standby, so STBY lit beside the V50 N25 prompt is a composite of the moment before and the moment after; and the 00062 code and the V37E 06E keying come from memory of the Colossus / Luminary procedures, not from a document checked while this was written, so they are worth checking against the GSOP. A fully blank display with only STBY lit was considered and set aside, because a dark display reads as a dead panel (see `marquee`). `_dsky_paint_display` takes the PROG text, registers, VERB / NOUN and the COMP ACTY state as arguments; the quote frame calls it byte-identically to before. `time_str` is `del`'d, and `TestDskySleepFrame` spies on the segment painter to pin that only 50, 25 and 00062 light, with both PROG positions dark. Composed at 800×480 and NEAREST-downsampled.
  - `oblivion` (dithered grey glass with a white pool / contour map / shaded drone / Exo 2 Light body / red matched phrase — ***Oblivion* (2013)**, the Sky Tower's light table). A custom-render frame (`render_oblivion_frame`).

    **The object.** Kosinski's film is remembered for its screens, designed by GMUNK's team and set in Blender: the Sky Tower's desk is a light table whose whole glass surface is the interface — a white glow pooled under the hand, frosted panes overlapping at slightly different tones, hairline geometry, a topographic map of the sector with the hydro rigs on it, a drone's status as a ring, waveforms, columns of tiny numerals, and one warm accent for anything alive or wrong.

    **The glass is painted in continuous tone and dithered** (`_dither_calibrated`, white and black): pale grey at the edges, a white pool at the upper left where the quote sits (its saturated heart wiped clean after the dither, because error diffusion carries the grey's residue a little way in), the panes a shade darker with soft edges, the drone's shadow, a grain kept out of the pool.

The twelve rigs sit on the terrain at seeded positions as hairline glyphs with their numbers, and the dial's middle ring is kept clear of contours so it reads as an instrument over the terrain.

    **The drone** hovers at the pane's corner: a sphere whose height field is a blurred disc, shaded Blinn-Phong under the upper-left light (`shade_height_field`) and mapped so the lit hull is white and only the terminator greys, two pods modelled with `_shade_silhouette` and outlined after, and the red lens painted after the dither with a catchlight and a red bloom into the glass.

    **The hour is the rig, and the bearing:** the hour's rig is red on the map with a leader to its number (run toward the pane's centre so it stays inside), the dial's tick at the hour's place is a red wedge with a red dot on station, and the status row along the foot fills the hour's cell. The waveform strip and the numeral column are seeded from the quote. Composed at 800×480 and NEAREST-downsampled otherwise.

A custom-render frame (`render_yorha_frame`).

    **The object.** Automata's pause screen lays a parchment-cream sheet over the blurred world, hatched with fine diagonals and dotted on a grid, with a dark tab bar across the top, a left-hand menu of boxed rows with corner ticks and the selected row inverted, a content pane with a rule under its title, and Pod 042 — the android's floating support unit — hovering at the edge of it all.

    **The panels are laid crisp after the dither** as white with a yellow eighth on the 4×4 Bayer tile (`_yorha_fill_panel`) — error diffusion worms at a light density, and a page should not. Then come the hairlines, corner ticks, dot grid, and the tab bar with INTEL open under the YoRHa crest. Every label in a row, a tab or the dialog is set on a baseline that centres its cap height in the cell (`_yorha_cap_baseline`): EB Garamond's ascent carries accent room above the caps, so a top-anchored label sat visibly low.

    **Pod 042 is drawn crisp too** (`_yorha_paint_pod`): the game's box-bodied unit in side view, facing the pane and seen a little from above, with its arms folded under. A white keyline lifts it off the stippled sheet; the faces are Bayer-stippled by the upper-left light (`_yorha_stipple_polygon`: top white, side a cream 3/16 yellow, the front end shaded with black), with black edges, panel seams, a rear vent, a dark port in the front end, and its legend on a small tag. Only its shadow, well below the hull so it reads as hovering, goes through the dither. *Tried and rejected:* a capsule hull modelled in continuous tone with fins and an antenna — error diffusion left its surfaces mottled, the outline didn't register with the dithered tone, and the antenna poked into the twelfth menu row.

    **The hour is the open entry:** ARCHIVE 01–12 are the hours, the hour's row is the inverted one with a white pointer, and the pane's counter reads the same number over twelve. The quote is in EB Garamond with the matched phrase Bold knocked out of a black box per line (`_yorha_phrase_boxes`: `_lumon_hover_boxes` supplies the runs, and the box is trimmed to the Bold face's ascender top and descender foot with an even margin — the line-height box ran a third too tall, all of it above the ascenders, while descenders touched its foot), the book's title heads the pane, the author closes it, and one glitch sliver at the pane's foot — the game's own tic — is seeded from the quote. Composed at 800×480 and NEAREST-downsampled otherwise.

It is the quote frame's cached sheet, panels, Pod and crest (`_yorha_scene`), with the tab bar turned from INTEL to SYSTEM (`_yorha_paint_tab_bar`, which the quote frame's rules pass also calls). The twelve menu rows become the System menu (`_YORHA_SLEEP_MENU`), with SLEEP MODE inverted under the white pointer. The pane's head reads System › Sleep Mode with STANDBY at the right, and the pane holds the game's confirmation dialog: a crisp panel face with a hard Bayer-stippled black shadow, black bands at its top and foot, corner ticks, "Enter sleep mode?" in Regular 30 px, then **Yes** selected (knocked out white of a black bar behind a white ▶) over an outlined **No**. Under the dialog, Pod 042 makes a proposal in the game's register: "Proposal: Unit 2B enter sleep mode. The archive will be kept until morning." The quote frame's glitch sliver is left out on purpose, so the unit reads as resting, not damaged. The inks are the theme's white, yellow and black. Nothing on it tells the time (`time_str` is `del`-asserted), and like the quote frame it is composed at 800×480 and NEAREST-downsampled.
  - `hitchhiker` (black screen / yellow masthead and badge / hand-animated white Michroma entry with coloured line markers / yellow matched phrase / the Babel fish under a raster / the galaxy chart — **the BBC's *Hitchhiker's Guide to the Galaxy* (1981)**, the Guide's entries). A custom-render frame (`render_hitchhiker_frame`).

    **The object.** The series' Guide graphics were animated by hand at Pearce Studios under Rod Lord, cel by cel and backlit, to look like a computer display no computer of 1981 could draw: black screens, square-shouldered capitals laid down a line at a time with a coloured marker at the head of each, diagrams in flat colour with hairline leaders and numbered callouts, and DON'T PANIC in large friendly letters.

    **The page is an entry with its figures:** the Guide's name in tracked yellow Michroma over a rule, the DON'T PANIC badge, the entry's subject — the quoted author — in white capitals under a green ENTRY label with the stack of colour bars, the entry's text as the quote in white with the matched phrase in yellow and a marker in the inks in rotation at the head of each line, SEE ALSO: the book.

    **The lettering is hand-animated:** every glyph of the title and the text is set one at a time with a seeded one-pixel registration wobble (`_hitchhiker_draw`), which is what cel lettering looks like when it holds on screen.

    **Figure 1 is the Babel fish** in the series' own diagram: a spline-drawn yellow fish under a one-in-three CRT raster (`_crt_paint_scanlines`), its brainwave sensor in blue, telepathic matrix in green, gills in red, nerve in white, five numbered callouts on the organs with a two-column legend under a rule.

    **Figure 2 is the galaxy, and the clock:** a seeded two-armed spiral of white and yellow stars in a boxed chart with twelve blue sector lines. **The hour's sector**, counted clockwise from the top like a clock face, is outlined in yellow with the Earth as a ringed blue dot inside it and a leader to YOU ARE HERE (and, beneath, MOSTLY HARMLESS). Pinned across the minutes; the matched phrase carries the minute. Composed at 800×480 and NEAREST-downsampled otherwise.
  - `escritoire` (dark mahogany desk / cream sheet / black Dancing Script body / blue matched phrase / out-of-focus brass and a fountain pen — **a handwritten letter on a writing desk, seen from the writer's chair**). A custom-render frame (`render_escritoire_frame`).

    **The sheet in perspective.** The sheet is a trapezoid whose far edge is about three fifths the width of its near edge, rolled a few degrees so the right side sits higher, with its near corners running off the panel. The quad is the image of a flat sheet turned 2.5° on the desk under a level camera, not a freehand trapezoid: the vanishing point of its rows and that of its sides share one horizontal horizon (`test_sheet_is_a_real_perspective_with_a_level_horizon`). The first quad was drawn by eye, with a far edge rising to the right and side edges leaning as if the sheet were turned the other way; its implied horizon tilted about ten degrees, and the writing, which follows the homography faithfully, fanned between the two and read as climbing off the paper. Moving a corner means re-solving the camera, not nudging the point.

    **Only the near half of the page is legible, on purpose:** at the far edge a glyph is 60% of its near-edge size, which at the panel's resolution is noise. So the foreshortened band carries the letter's *earlier* lines as faint asemic script (`_codex_script`, stippled at ~40% black and seeded from `_row_digest`, so a different quote is a different letter) and the quote is centred in the near band (`_ESCRITOIRE_BAND`) where the perspective costs it least. The faint band sells the depth; the quote never pays for it.

    **The page is laid out flat, then warped:** `_escritoire_layout` fits the quote with `fit_quote_balanced` on an upright 900×560 sheet at 2× (`_ESCRITOIRE_SIZES` raises the caps per layout so a hero quote fills the band instead of sitting on one line). `_escritoire_masks` writes prose, matched phrase and faint script into three `"L"` masks, and `_escritoire_warp` carries each through one `Image.Transform.PERSPECTIVE` onto a double-size canvas with bicubic sampling, box-reduces 2:1 and hard-thresholds, so every ink pixel lands solid.

    **Geometry is proven, not hoped.** The perspective coefficients are solved from the quad by a small Gaussian elimination (`_escritoire_coeffs`, memoised; the runtime has no numpy), and `TestEscritoireFrame` carries the closed-form inverse it uses to prove every layout length stays on the panel, including a 470-character raw row, which refits down to `_ESCRITOIRE_FLOOR` rather than running off the page. The room reserved for the signature follows the lines actually present, so a quote without an author sets larger.

    **Inks:** the prose is black and the matched phrase blue — fountain-pen ink, the one accent that reads as ink rather than decoration on cream. The paper is white with a yellow stipple thickening toward the near, shadowed corner. A second page lies under the letter (`_ESCRITOIRE_UNDER_QUAD`), turned a few degrees further so a wedge of it shows along the far edge and past the top-right corner, carrying more yellow and the letter's own soft shadow so the two separate without an outline.

    **The desk is mahogany, not aubergine.** A fixed 30% of every lit run is yellow, the catalogue's print-sepia direction, riding the same tile read as the red so the hue holds down the lamp pool's falloff, and the grain drifts with x instead of running as ruled lines.

    **The brass are turned pieces, whole on the panel.** `_ESCRITOIRE_BRASS` gives each piece as a lathe profile (an inkwell with a ball finial on a domed lid, a pen cup with a rolled rim and two pens standing in it, a sander with a pierced dome and a bell foot), shaded by `_escritoire_lathe` as a cylinder falloff with a narrow glint and darkened rows where the profile steps, with lamp glints (`_ESCRITOIRE_GLINTS`) that the blur turns into bokeh and a mirrored, fading reflection below each foot. The two pens lying further back (`_ESCRITOIRE_BACK_PENS`) are drawn by what the lamp catches — a gold nib, band and finial and a bright line along the barrel — because a dark barrel on a dark desk vanishes. All of it is a luminance field and an alpha field, blurred together and stippled on a black, red, yellow and white ramp, so it reads as out of focus.

    **The pen** (`_escritoire_paint_pen`) lies across the faint band with its nib resting where the writing stopped: black lacquer tapering toward the cap, a gold section band, cap ring, clip and finial in R+Y 5/8 : 3/8, a slit nib, a dotted highlight and a stippled shadow.

    **Caching and cold-start cost.** The desk, brass and empty sheet are quote-independent and cached per process (`_ESCRITOIRE_SCENE`), keyed on the painters themselves so the decoration fence's neutered painters rebuild it (the `expanse` convention). The cache does nothing on the appliance, which starts a fresh renderer per repaint, so the scene is built to be cheap from cold: the lamp pool and the paper's warmth are smooth fields computed at quarter resolution and scaled up (`_escritoire_field`), and the per-pixel brass and gold passes run over their own bounding boxes, ~0.13 s for the scene where per-pixel fields took ~0.6 s.

    **The sheet's shadow is shifted with `_shift_no_wrap`, not `ImageChops.offset`:** the sheet runs off the bottom of the panel, and a wrapping shift carries its shadow round to the top rows.

    **Plumbing.** No clock surface beyond the phrase: `time_str` is `del`-asserted, pinned byte-identical across every time. Composed at the canonical 800×480 and NEAREST-downsampled (the `metro` convention). Saturation tier `0.5`: the cream sheet covers most of the frame and its W+Y stipple goes lemon at the harder push, the `yorha` argument.

    **Tried and rejected.**
    - *Warping a finished RGB frame* — the obvious first idea: the resampled glyph edges come out as a grey fringe that the palette snap turns into a ragged stipple, and a script face has nothing to spare.
    - *Red stippled over black for the desk* — read as plum on the panel.
    - *Three cylinders cut off by the top edge for the brass* — read as lit columns.
    - *Pills for the back pens* — read as lights.
  - `bladerunner` (black glass / white hairlines and Barlow Condensed / yellow matched phrase / a blue-and-white X-ray, the twins' DNA and the baseline test — ***Blade Runner 2049* (2017)**, the LAPD's systems). A custom-render frame (`render_bladerunner_frame`). Its sibling `lasvegas` is the place; this one is the screens K works at.

    **The object.** Territory Studio's interfaces for the film are monochrome and hairline: black glass, white rules and tracked condensed capitals, data in a plain mono, colour only where the system has found something. The frame takes that grammar literally — white for structure, yellow for a finding (the matched phrase, IDENTICAL, the hour's prompt), red for what the operator is being shown (the serial's zoom, the trace marker, the live dot).

    **Four panes under an LAPD header.** The record holds the quote, bracketed, with a file line from the row's source id and line number and a yellow gutter bar down the text. The bone scan is the box's remains on the light table. The DNA comparison is the twins born 06.10.21, one male and one female, two lines each of base pairs seeded from the quote and identical base for base, a yellow tick for every match between them. The baseline strip is the post-trauma test.

    **The X-ray is the one painted passage.** The pelvis (two ilia as splined fans, the sacrum, the pubic ring with both foramina cut out, the femoral heads, necks, trochanters and shafts) is a single mask turned into luminance: a white cortical rim, a marrow whose density rises away from the edges so thick bone reads as volume (a blurred copy of the mask, gated by it), trabecular value noise, a faint scatter and film grain. That is mapped onto a black → blue → white ramp and Floyd–Steinberg dithered against those three measured inks (`_dither_calibrated`); black holds to the bottom tenth so the grain stays sparse on the glass. The serial on the left iliac crest is boxed in red, with a leader to an inset that magnifies N7FAA52318.

    **The hour is the baseline's prompt.** Twelve words of the call-and-response (from the *Pale Fire* passage the test is built on) sit in cells along the foot; the hour's cell is filled yellow, a red marker crosses the trace above it and the otherwise flat trace spikes there. Pinned across the minutes; the matched phrase carries the minute. Composed at 800×480 and NEAREST-downsampled otherwise.
  - `lasvegas` (the orange haze of the dead Las Vegas / a skyline of broken casino towers / K among the beehives / a black LAPD archive pane with white Barlow prose and the matched phrase in yellow — ***Blade Runner 2049* (2017)**). A custom-render frame (`render_lasvegas_frame`). Its sibling `bladerunner` is the film's systems UI; this one is the place.

    **The vista is painted in continuous tone and dithered to four inks** (`_dither_calibrated`: red, yellow, black, white). Every tone is written as a blend of the *measured* inks (`_lasvegas_mix`), so the field is specified in the colours the panel will actually show and Floyd–Steinberg only has to find the ratio. The haze runs from a dark rust overhead through a red-heavy orange to its palest at the horizon, where the dust is lit edge-on; a red share of about 5/8 is what reads as orange rather than mustard once the panel's greenish yellow is in the mix. The sun is a smear rather than a disc: a wide warm bloom, a paler inner bloom and a barely edged core, all clipped to the sky. Long soft dust bands (value noise stretched horizontally) drift through it; at a tighter grid they read as scan lines, which was the first attempt.

    **Depth is the haze eating the city.** Two ranks of broken casino towers (and the Luxor pyramid, which the Wallace tower echoes) sit along the horizon barely darker than the air, the farther rank fainter and taller. A first version stood a colossal pair of legs in stiletto pumps over the vista; it was cut, and the frame is the emptier for it on purpose.

    **Laid crisp after the dither:** K in his long coat with one hand held out, the three-box hive stack, a seeded cloud of bees, and a yellow rim down K's sun side.     **The pane is K's LAPD archive:** black with a yellow hairline, a chamfered corner and a yellow header bar (LAPD · REPLICANT DETECTION, KD6-3.7), the quote in white Barlow Medium with the matched phrase in Barlow Bold yellow, the byline in yellow tracked Barlow Condensed, two lines of base pairs in Share Tech Mono seeded from the quote with a few matched in yellow. **The hour is the archive drawer:** twelve cells along the pane's foot, the hour's filled yellow, pinned across the minutes. Composed at 800×480 and NEAREST-downsampled otherwise.
  - `traumateam` (black screen / drawn white wordmark and six-armed mark / red dispatch band with cyan end tabs / white Oxanium body / matched phrase Bold white on a red block / vitals trace — ***Cyberpunk*: a Trauma Team International dispatch screen**). The letters and the mark are inspired by the logo, not traced from it. The mark (`_traumateam_mark_polygons`) is a vertical bar crossed by two broad diagonal bands with square-cut ends, and a thin black cut from the bar's upper left to the centre. The lockup is centred on the frame as a whole, so the mark sits right of centre, as it does in the logo.

The band is solid red under white SemiBold chrome, and the matched phrase is Bold white on a red block, one block per line it occupies (`_lumon_hover_boxes`, shared with `lumon`), which ties the phrase to the band. The logo's third colourway puts the band on cyan; here cyan is only the band's two end tabs, a G+B 50/50 stipple. A cyan field cannot hold text on six inks.

The call's status beside it (`EN ROUTE`, `ON SCENE` …) and the vitals trace's rate and phase are seeded from the quote (`_row_digest`), so a new quote changes the call and an unchanged one never redraws differently.

  - `redacted` (white page / the Bureau's letterhead and black seal / red DECLASSIFIED stamp / typed Special Elite body / matched phrase in red / seeded black marker bars over other words — ***Control*: a declassified Federal Bureau of Control document**). A custom-render frame (`render_redacted_frame`); `control` is the game's title card, this is its paperwork.

    **The censor never touches the time.** Bars land only on words outside the matched phrase with four letters or more that are not function words or time words (`_REDACTED_SPARED`: "would", "morning", "seven", "clock" …; a compound is spared when any part is, so a second time such as `FORTY-SEVEN MINUTES PAST TEN` stays whole), and a word glued to the phrase with no space (`seven)`'s `)` side) is skipped, so the phrase and anything that reads as part of a time stay legible. Edge punctuation is trimmed off a bar, so a trailing comma survives beside it, as on a real document. About a third of the eligible words go (`_REDACTED_RATE`), capped at `_REDACTED_CAP` of them so a short quote is never blacked out whole, and at least one goes whenever any qualify.

    **Bars are a felt marker, not a rectangle tool.** Each row of a bar's ends wanders a pixel either way, and two redactions side by side on a line join into one stroke across the gap, unless a spared word sat between them. The AUTHORIZED signature in the foot is a bar too.

    **Seeded, never clocked.** Which words go, the bars' edges, the document type, file number, clearance level and the stamp's tilt all come from the quote (`_row_digest`); nothing reads the time, so a frame is byte-identical across every minute and an unchanged quote never redraws differently.

    **Plumbing.** Solid black and red on white, no dither; the stamp's wear is a positional-hash scatter of pinholes (`position_noise`), clipped to the panel. Composed at the canonical 800×480 and NEAREST-downsampled (the `metro` convention). Saturation tier `0.5` (light ground).

    **Its own sleep frame** (`render_redacted_sleep`, the theme's `spec.sleep`): a Standby Order from the Office of the Director, stamped SUSPENDED, with every word under a bar except **lights**, early in the first line, and **out**, partway along the last, both in red.
