The paragraph under the bars (`_REDACTED_SLEEP_ROW`) is never read, but its wording decides where the two words fall, and `TestRedactedSleepFrame` pins that: `lights` on line 1, `out` on the last line and not at either end of it, so the eye has to look for it. It is set from a larger ceiling than a quote (40 against 34) with more leading, because only two words are read. Bars sit a pixel inside each word so the gaps survive, and a seeded share of gaps (`_REDACTED_SLEEP_JOIN`) is carried straight across, as a censor's stroke would. Nothing on it tells the time.

  - `gantry` (night sky and dark asphalt / a steel truss gantry over a motorway / a full-matrix LED message sign with the quote in amber and the matched phrase lit white and bold / tail-light streaks running off to the vanishing point / the source on a green guide sign whose exit number is the hour — **an overhead highway message sign at night, shot as a long exposure**). A custom-render frame (`render_gantry_frame`).

    **The font is read as dots, not outlines.** Lumen is a 5×7 matrix with its dots on a strict grid a tenth of an em apart, so `_gantry_glyph` (in `themes/_shared.py`, which `platform` also reads) renders each character once at eight pixels a dot and reads the grid centres back as a set of lit cells, trimmed to the glyph's ink columns. Every LED on the face is then drawn by the theme on one lattice that covers the whole sign, lit or not, the way a real sign is built. Drawing the outlines at a fitted size instead would put each line's dots on a grid of its own, and the unlit matrix between the letters, which is most of what makes this read as a sign rather than a pixel font, could not be drawn at all.

    **The dot pitch is chosen per quote.** `_gantry_fit` tries pitches from 9 px down to 3 px and keeps the first the quote fits at, so a short quote gets fat round LEDs and the longest corpus quote still runs at 5 px. At each pitch it tries a roomy 10-row line first, then a tight 9-row one, where a descender (rows 9-10) sits directly over the caps of the line below: a bigger dot on tight lines reads better than a smaller one on loose lines. Tight is refused when a line below the first carries a glyph that reaches the accent rows (in the corpus only `É`), which the descender above would overprint. The wrap is then balanced to the narrowest measure that keeps the line count, and every line is centred, as a sign sets a message. Letters are packed proportionally, ink columns plus one blank column, with three blank columns between words; a monospace 7-column cell left the words looking spaced out. The face's arrow ligatures (`->`, `<-`, `=>`, `_GANTRY_LIGATURES`) are read as one wide glyph each, shaped by the raqm layout engine Pillow's wheels carry; the face's other ligatures (a heart, a smiley, the maths operators) are deliberately left unshaped, since they would turn a believable sign into a font demo.

    **The matched phrase is bold the matrix way**: each glyph ORed with itself one column over, the way sign controllers embolden, and lit white instead of amber. Its bloom is mostly white with a third of yellow; an all-yellow bloom fell in the 1 px gaps between the doubled columns and speckled the letters from inside. The amber body blooms red, which on the panel's dark red is barely more than a warmth around the dots.

    **The face.** Unlit LEDs carry a single blue pixel at pitch 5 and up (the lens catching the skyglow); module seams show as a sparse dotted blue line every sixteen LEDs (every second pixel was too strong at big pitches); two lit LEDs per quote are dead, chosen from `_row_digest`, because every sign in service has some.

    **The scene.** Sky and asphalt are a continuous-tone field dithered against the calibrated black, blue and red (the sodium night sky recipe), with grain added before the dither, since Floyd–Steinberg over a smooth ramp settles into a regular lattice. The road is kept dark on purpose: an earlier draft pooled the sign's amber light on the wet asphalt, and the dither turned the whole road yellow and drowned the trails. Tail lights run in pairs down three lanes, their cores red with a quarter white; head lights come the other way beyond the median. The steel is a silhouette, black members picked out by a blue hairline and a white glint on the top chord, and the beacons on the cabinet's top corners are mid-flash.

    **The hour is the exit number.** The guide sign posts the title as the destination and the author under it, in Barlow Condensed, with `EXIT <hour>` on its tab (`_clock_hour12`), so a frame is byte-identical across the minutes of an hour; nothing else reads the time.

    **Plumbing.** Composed at the canonical 800×480 and NEAREST-downsampled (the `metro` convention). Saturation tier `0.7` (dark ground).

    **Its own sleep frame** (`render_gantry_sleep`, the theme's `spec.sleep`): the small hours on the same motorway. The traffic has gone, the beacons are dark, and the sign has been set to the message highway authorities run at three in the morning: **TIRED?** in white over REST AREA / NEXT EXIT → in amber, the arrow the face's own ligature, with the guide sign reading *Rest Area, Next Right* under a bare EXIT tab. Nothing on it tells the time.

  - `platform` (a station canopy at night / a railway departure board hung from it / amber Lumen Round Medium dots on black, the matched phrase in Round Bold / the book as the 1st train's destination, "via" its author / the quote as the calling points / the station clock — **a railway departure board after dark**). A custom-render frame (`render_platform_frame`); the second Lumen theme, and deliberately the opposite of `gantry` in how it uses the face.

    **Weight is dot size.** Lumen's Round instances differ only in how big each dot is: Round Medium's dot spans 66 of its 100-unit cell, Round Bold's 86 (`_PLATFORM_MEDIUM_DOT` / `_PLATFORM_BOLD_DOT`, measured from the face). The body is Medium, airy, the way a real board's text is; the matched phrase is Bold, and stands out the way a heavier weight does, with no change of colour. Only the Bold dots carry a red bloom: round dots as small as the Medium body's only muddy the black between them.

    **Each element picks its own pitch, and an unlit dot is not drawn.** `gantry` is one lattice over the whole sign with its dark LEDs showing; here the destination runs at 4 px, the "via", status and label at 3 px, the clock at 6 px with its seconds at 4 px on the same baseline, and the message takes the largest of 7 to 3 px that it fits at (the same roomy-then-tight line pitches as `gantry`). A board in a dark station is black wherever it is off. The message is set ragged-right, as a board sets it; the destination is truncated with three dots when it runs long, as a board does.

    **The dots are placed, not rasterised.** Drawing the outlines straight onto the panel put each dot's centre between pixels, so the same dot came out two different sizes across a line. The frame places every dot from the face's grid (the Lumen reader shared with `gantry`, in `themes/_shared.py`) and draws it at the instance's diameter rounded to the pixel, Medium rounding down and Bold to nearest so the two always differ (`_platform_diameter`). At a 4 px pitch the Medium dot is a 2 px square; a 3 px dot with its corners dropped was tried there and read as cross-stitch up close.

    **A departure board is a clock.** This is one of the themes that prints HH:MM digits: the 1st train's departure time and the station clock under the message, both the render time, with the clock's seconds reading `:00`. "Calling at:" is a fixed small label; set at the message's pitch, a short quote turned it into a headline.

    **Plumbing.** Solid amber dots and a red bloom on black, no dither. Composed at the canonical 800×480 and NEAREST-downsampled (the `metro` convention). Saturation tier `0.7` (dark ground).

    **Its own sleep frame** (`render_platform_sleep`, the theme's `spec.sleep`): the board after the last train, **No further departures** / *from this platform*, then "**Good night.** The last train has gone. Services resume in the morning.", with "Good night." in the Round Bold the matched phrase has by day. The clock is left dark, so nothing on the frame tells the time.

  - `diags` (white / black / red, the system sans — **a diagnostic status panel**, not a literary frame). A custom-render frame (`render_diags_frame`) that shows no quote, so it is in `RANDOM_EXCLUDED_THEMES` and reachable by button B, the web dropdown or `--theme`.

    **The panel.** A header bar; a big clock beside a status table of the picker's choice (bucket, layout, quality, ID, matched phrase); a strip of host, IP and uptime (`_diags_system_info`, each lookup failing soft to `—`); the six native inks as labelled swatches; then the synthesised colours in two bands — the two-ink stipple recipes (`_DIAGS_SYNTH_SWATCHES`) and the three-ink ones (`_DIAGS_TRIPLE_SWATCHES`) — each swatch a bold name over its recipe. It is for on-panel calibration and for checking that the picker chose what you expect. The clock is shown verbatim, malformed or not, because this is the diagnostic panel.

    **The swatch bands mirror the recipe catalogue**, every recipe in `docs/spectra6_color_recipes.md` and nothing else (`test_swatch_list_has_every_documented_recipe`), so a swatch means a recipe is documented, not that a theme paints it.

    **Type and palette.** The system sans chain (DejaVu, Liberation, Noto Sans): a grotesque reads better than Playfair at label sizes, and it keeps the palette-only paths (the source card, a `--message` headline) visibly distinct from `default`. White / black / red keeps those paths readable, and the frame reads the same palette for its labels.

  - `splitflap` (a pale wall / a black-framed split-flap message board on it, in the manner of a Vestaboard / white Bebas Neue capitals on black flap tiles / the matched phrase on yellow colour tiles / the byline on the bottom row behind a run of colour tiles / two tiles caught mid-flip — **a split-flap message board**). A custom-render frame (`render_splitflap_frame`).

    **The hinge does the work.** Every position on the grid is a tile, blank or not, and every tile is split across its middle by the 2 px gap between its two flaps, which runs straight through the letter; each tile also carries a dotted blue sheen along its top edge and a white axle pin at either end of the hinge. Without the hinge the board reads as letters in boxes. The tiles are plain black: a sparse blue scatter over each face, meant to lift the tiles off the black gaps, lined up into diagonal hatching across the whole board.

    **The grid never changes size.** A split-flap board cannot change its tiles, so the grid is fixed at 32 columns by 12 rows (tiles 21×33 px on a 23×36 pitch) and a short quote sits small on a mostly blank board. Every corpus quote wraps into nine rows at 32 columns; under the quote is a blank row, then the byline (`-AUTHOR, TITLE`, the title dropped when the row is too short) right-aligned behind a run of red, orange, yellow, green, blue and violet colour tiles. Lines are centred, as people set a message on a real board.

    **Capitals, in the board's character set.** The flaps carry capitals, digits and a short run of punctuation (`_SPLITFLAP_CHARSET`, a Vestaboard's). Curly quotes and dashes fall back to their straight forms, accented letters to their base letter, and a character with no stand-in is dropped, since a flap board can show only what is printed on its flaps. Letters are thresholded masks: antialiased edges would snap to stray inks.

    **The matched phrase is on yellow tiles,** black capitals on the panel's brightest ink after white, and the spaces between its words are yellow too, so the phrase reads as one bar.

    **Two tiles are caught mid-flip**, chosen from `_row_digest` among the quote's letters outside the phrase: the top half already shows the new letter, the bottom half still the letter before it (flaps turn in order), and the falling flap is a dark sliver at the hinge with a blue glint, a pixel wider than the tile either side. Two earlier versions read wrong: a foreshortened half-letter on the falling flap read as an underline under a crossbar, and a white edge on the flap read as a strikethrough.

    **The wall** is the panel's white with a 1-in-16 yellow scatter (`position_noise`): a 1-in-4 lattice read as a mustard wall against the panel's greenish yellow.

    **Plumbing.** Nothing reads the time; the matched phrase carries it. Composed at the canonical 800×480 and NEAREST-downsampled (the `metro` convention). Saturation tier `0.7`: the board is a dark ground with a light wall round it.

    **Its own sleep frame** (`render_splitflap_sleep`, the theme's `spec.sleep`): tile art, the way people decorate a real board. A crescent moon in yellow colour tiles and a scatter of white star tiles over GOOD NIGHT / SEE YOU IN THE MORNING.

  - `saros` (black sky / diffusion-dithered red-and-gold corona / white Saira body / ember matched phrase, Orbitron wordmark and Michroma chrome — **Housemarque's *Saros* (2026)**, the eclipse that hangs over the colony of Carcosa). A custom-render frame (`render_saros_frame`).

    **The corona is painted in continuous tone and dithered, not stippled.** `_saros_corona_field` evaluates a radial profile per pixel at a quarter of panel resolution (`_SAROS_SKY_STEP`; a glow has no finer detail) and bicubic-upsamples it: a chromospheric rim decaying over 0.06 radii, a mid corona over 0.36, an outer haze over 1.1, and eight **streamers** (`_SAROS_STREAMERS` — angular Gaussian lobes that brighten the mid and outer terms along their axes, asymmetric on purpose, a few long plumes and short brushes rather than a halo). A **sunset band** rings the horizon (`_saros_dusk`, an exponential up from the ground line, strongest under the sun and a third as bright at the edges — what a total eclipse's horizon actually does, and what cuts every silhouette out of the sky), and a cold **blue haze** pools low on the left, away from the sun (`_saros_haze`). The photosphere disc and the moon are then painted sharp at full resolution, because the exposed sliver is the one hard edge in the sky, and the field is Floyd–Steinberg-dithered to K/R/Y/W + blue (`_SAROS_SKY_PALETTE`) — the `biomech` dusk recipe.

    **The fire carries no blue channel**: a blue value in the corona is an error that diffusion eventually pays out as a blue speck in the fire, so blue exists only in the haze, and `TestSarosFrame::test_the_corona_holds_no_blue` pins it. Green is never used.

    **The time is the eclipse's phase.** At twelve the moon (`_SAROS_MOON_SCALE`, a shade larger than the sun so totality is total) sits dead centre: a black disc, the rim, a symmetric corona. At every other hour it is offset `_SAROS_HOUR_OFFSET` radii *away* from the hour's place on a clock face, so a sliver of photosphere shows through on the hour's side and the **diamond ring** — a white bead in a yellow bloom with a horizontal lens spike (`_saros_paint_bead`) — sits where the hour hand would point. The bead *is* the hand; the wordmark's O, a small eclipse, carries the same bead; the status line's occlusion figure (`_saros_occlusion`, the two-disc lens formula) is derived from the same geometry. Hour only, the `cardcatalog` / `observation` posture, pinned byte-identical across every minute of an hour; the matched phrase carries the readable time.

    **The ground is silhouette plus rim light.** Spires, broken colony towers and a bone arch go into one mask. The rim is the mask minus itself shifted two pixels *away* from the sun (left of the sun the light comes from the upper right, right of it from the upper left, composited on a half-plane), weighted by the sky's own falloff and stippled yellow where strong, red where weak — the `biomech` wall's rim-light trick on flat cut-outs. The sky is quote-independent and cached per hour (`_SAROS_SKY_CACHE`, keyed on the painter so the decoration fence measures a painter, not a cache).

    **The quote** (`_SAROS_QUOTE_RECT`, ragged-left — it reads as a transmission) is white Saira pasted over a black halo grown from its own mask (`_halo_paste`, shared with `biomech`, `atropos` and `expedition`; saros grows it to 7 px), so a dithered speck never lands between two strokes; the matched phrase is an ember, yellow core in the `bakelite` split-band tangerine bloom, `ground` pinned to black so it only spills into the halo. Spores (`_saros_paint_motes`) are seeded from `_row_digest`, so a different quote is a different drift, and never land on the moon. Composed at the canonical 800×480 and NEAREST-downsampled (the `metro` convention). Saturation tier `0.7`.

    **Tried and rejected.**
    - *A blue channel in the corona* — diffusion paid it out as blue specks in the fire.
    - *A reflected pool on the ground* through a bare `BAYER_8x8` compare at ~5% density — it rendered as a dot lattice on an 8 px pitch (the "jitter an ordered field over large areas" rule); the sunset band does the job.
    - *The game's Enforcer under the eclipse*, back to us, shield on one arm, with two blue suit lights on his pack — at panel scale he read as a blob with two dots, and the composition is stronger as an empty place. The only blue left on the page is the haze.
- **Fonts.** Typography is per-theme, and each non-default theme deliberately picks a face from a different type *family* so the rendered frame's silhouette changes with the theme — not just its palette.

    **Chains.** `THEME_FONTS` maps each `THEMES` entry to a dict with `quote_regular` / `quote_bold` / `ornament` candidate chains, each a list of path strings OR `(path, variation_name)` tuples. The tuple form targets variable fonts — `load_font` calls `set_variation_by_name(name)` after the truetype load, so a theme can pick Regular/Bold instances from a single variable font file (whose default axis instance may be Thin — a missing variation call would render near-invisible hairlines on the panel).

    **Fallbacks.** Every per-theme chain ends at the Playfair / DejaVu defaults so a missing-font install degrades to the default face instead of dropping to the PIL bitmap fallback. System metadata / debug strips always use DejaVu / Noto / Liberation Sans regardless of theme. Install `apt install fonts-noto-core fonts-dejavu-core` if the bundled fonts aren't found. When every TTF candidate is missing, `load_font` logs a one-shot warning to stderr before returning `ImageFont.load_default()` so a misconfigured install surfaces instead of silently producing an 8-pixel bitmap render.
  - `default` and `dark` share the Playfair Display chain (transitional / high-contrast serif; repo-local `idle_hours/fonts/`, then common Pi/Linux paths, with DejaVu Serif / Liberation Serif / Noto Serif as system fallbacks).
  - `newsprint` uses **Old Standard TT** (vintage broadsheet / scientific-journal Didone revival — Regular body + Bold accent).
  - `nightvision` uses **Space Mono** (retro-terminal monospace — Regular + Bold; DejaVu Sans Mono is the system-font fallback).
  - `gothic` reuses the same EB Garamond body but promotes **UnifrakturMaguntia** into *both* the `quote_bold` and `ornament` slots, so the matched time phrase joins the oversized quote marks in dramatic red blackletter — the font defines the theme rather than appearing as a guest accessory, while the body stays in legible Renaissance serif so a 200-character dense layout still reads cleanly.
  - `bauhaus` uses **Jost** (Futura-adjacent geometric-constructed sans; variable font, Regular / Bold pinned via `set_variation_by_name`).
  - `comic` uses **Bangers** (all-caps comic-book display hand — the only display / hand-lettered face in the rotation; only one weight ships so the matched-phrase role re-uses the same file and gains differentiation purely through the accent colour).
  - `dispatch` uses **Special Elite** — a slab-mono typewriter face whose deliberately uneven inking is the whole point.

    Like Bangers it ships only Regular, so the matched-phrase role re-uses the same file and gains differentiation through the accent colour alone — period-authentic, since a real bichrome typewriter ribbon shifted between black and red without changing weight.

    **Fallback.** The chain falls through Space Mono (the closest in-rotation typewriter-adjacent face) and DejaVu Sans Mono before degrading to the Playfair serif chain, so a missing-Special-Elite install lands on a typewriter-flavoured mono rather than dropping a slab-typewriter theme onto a transitional serif silhouette.
  - `atomic` uses **Atomic Age** — a chunky 1950s display face from Sorkin Type (OFL) with pointed angular terminals on slab bodies, very mid-century signage / Sputnik-poster register. Like Bangers / Special Elite / UnifrakturMaguntia it ships only Regular, so the matched-phrase role re-uses the same file and gains differentiation through the accent colour alone. Body text in a display face is loud by design — that's the point. Falls back through DejaVu Sans Bold (and other heavy sans) before degrading to the Playfair serif chain, so a missing-Atomic-Age install lands on a heavy display silhouette rather than an elegant serif.
  - `firmament` uses **Cardo** (David Perry, OFL) — a Renaissance humanist serif designed for classical scholarship with full Polytonic Greek and Latin epigraphic coverage, evoking the typography of 17th-century celestial atlases.

    Ships Regular / Bold / Italic; the italic fills the ornament slot for the oversized opening quote mark, carrying the classical / mythological register of constellation names. Distinct from the EB Garamond used by gothic so firmament reads as a different humanist-serif branch rather than another Garamond variant.

    **Fallback.** Through EB Garamond → DejaVu Serif → Liberation Serif → the Playfair chain, so a missing-Cardo install still lands on a humanist serif silhouette rather than the Playfair display silhouette.
  - `lcars` uses **Antonio** (Vernon Adams, OFL) — a tall narrow condensed sans whose silhouette echoes Helvetica Compressed / Swiss 911, the original LCARS typeface family.

    Variable font with a Weight axis (100..700) whose default instance is Regular (400) — both Regular and Bold variation instances are exposed by name, so the Regular / Bold slots are pinned explicitly the same way the Bitter / Jost / Rubik variable fonts are. The condensed proportions are what make the theme read as console / instrumentation UI rather than editorial typography — every other sans in the rotation (Archivo, Jost, Rubik, Bangers) is normal-width or wider.

    **Fallback.** Through heavy DejaVu / Liberation / Noto Sans Bold before degrading to the Playfair serif chain so a missing-Antonio install lands on a heavy sans silhouette rather than dropping the LCARS theme onto an elegant transitional serif.
  - `marker` uses **Permanent Marker** (Font Diner / Google, Apache 2.0) — single-weight hand-drawn marker face. Same bichrome-ribbon trick comic / dispatch / atomic / saloon use: matched phrase re-uses Regular and gains differentiation through the blue accent alone. Heavy DejaVu / Liberation / Noto Sans Bold fallback before degrading to Playfair.
  - `saloon` uses **Rye** (Sorkin Type, OFL) — a 19th-century wood-engraved display slab serif evoking Wild West saloon signs and wanted posters. Single-weight; matched phrase re-uses Regular and gains differentiation from the red accent alone — exactly the way two-colour letterpress broadsides shifted between black and red ink on the same type plate. Heavy serif fallback (DejaVu / Liberation / Noto Serif Bold) before Playfair.
  - `roman` uses **Cinzel Decorative** (Natanael Gama, OFL) — a digital revival of the Roman capitalis monumentalis cut on Trajan's Column, the same flared serifs and even stroke contrast a chisel produces in marble. Body in Regular, matched phrase steps up to Bold (switching face mid-line would shatter the inscription illusion), and the Black weight carries the SPQR cartouche and oversized quote marks.
  - `alchemy` pairs **IM Fell English** (Igino Marini, OFL — 17th-century Oxford-press types of the actual alchemical-treatise era) for the body with **MedievalSharp** (skosch, OFL — calligraphic display face whose sharply-pointed strokes read as a ritual scribe's hand) for the matched-phrase and ornament slots. UnifrakturMaguntia is wired in behind MedievalSharp as the obvious next-nearest "ritual hand" silhouette before the chain degrades to a generic bold serif.
  - `deco` uses **Righteous** (Astigmatic, OFL) — 1930s geometric art-deco display sans, single-weight bichrome-ribbon discipline.
  - `chalkboard` uses **Playwrite GB J Guides** (TypeTogether, OFL) — UK primary-school joined cursive *with the dotted-outline practice letters schoolchildren trace over*. Single-weight; the dotted/hollow letterforms are the point of the theme. Fallback chain prefers italic faces (DejaVu Sans Italic, Liberation Sans Italic) before degrading to Playfair so a missing install lands on at least a slanted silhouette rather than dropping a cursive theme onto an upright serif.
  - `placard` uses **Patrick Hand SC** (Patrick Wagesreiter, OFL) — friendly hand-printed face whose small caps for lowercase do all the visual work, giving the text the silhouette of hand-lettered shop signage.
  - `chanbara` uses **Shojumaru** (Astigmatic / Brian J. Bonislawsky, OFL — by the same designer as Righteous and Atomic Age) — a dramatic brush-painted display face evoking samurai cinema posters.
  - `fillmore` uses **Bungee Shade** (David Jonathan Ross, OFL) — single-weight 3D-blocked display face, for the whole body on purpose: at body size under the maroon stipple it is borderline illegible, and that is the register (Wes Wilson's posters made you work for the band's name).

    **Fallback.** Through Bangers and Atomic Age (the closest in-rotation display-face siblings) before heavy DejaVu / Liberation / Noto Sans Bold, so a missing-Bungee install still lands on a chunky display silhouette rather than the Playfair serif.

    **Tried and rejected.** A Rubik Black body with the Shade kept for the phrase — reverted as too polite.
  - `astrarium` uses the **Cormorant Garamond** chain for the body / matched-phrase / ornament — the editorial / mid-century-modern register the astronomical-clock mockup wants, with the serif body sitting next to a hairline-ruled dashboard layout. The dashboard chrome (header strip, datum-panel keys, dial scale numerals) loads directly from `META_FONT_BOLD_CANDIDATES` inside `render_astrarium_frame` — chrome, not literary, sits in the same DejaVu/Liberation/Noto sans chain `diags` uses for its status labels.
  - `kanagawa` uses **Yuji Boku** — a sumi-brush Japanese face whose uneven hand-painted strokes pair naturally with the seigaiha tile pattern. Single-weight; bichrome-ribbon discipline. Fallback chain favours humanist serifs (Cormorant Garamond → EB Garamond → Old Standard TT) over sans because the sumi-brush register is closest to a high-contrast humanist serif silhouette, and a missing-Yuji install should land on a serif rather than a grotesque sans that would clash with the brush-calligraphy seascape mood.
  - `marquee` pairs **Cardo Italic** (David Perry, OFL — same humanist serif family `firmament` uses as its ornament chain, here promoted to the body slot) for the literary quote with **Bungee Shade** (David Jonathan Ross, OFL — 3D-blocked display face shared with `fillmore`) for the chunky chrome book-title display.

    Cardo Italic at 18–30pt carries the warm "feature copy" register on the black ground; Bungee Shade at up to 72pt reads as physical relief letters mounted on the marquee canopy. Cardo Bold fills the matched-phrase slot so weight differentiation is independent of the red accent.

    **Small chrome labels** (NOW SHOWING / ONE NIGHT ONLY / WRITTEN BY) deliberately switch to **Antonio Bold** at 12–14pt rather than Bungee Shade — Bungee Shade's 3D silhouette muddies into noise at small sizes, and reserving the chunky display face exclusively for the dominant title chrome is the same scale-discipline `astrarium` uses with Cormorant Bold for the dial digits versus sans labels in the datum strip.
  - `tarot` pairs **EB Garamond** (humanist old-style serif, same body face `gothic` uses) for the quote body with **Cinzel Decorative** Black + Bold (Natanael Gama, OFL — the chiselled capitalis monumentalis revival `roman` uses for its body) for the chrome stack.

    **Three sizes.** Black 36pt for the Roman numeral hour, Bold for the trump's name in Tyrian purple along the foot (fitted from 22pt down to a 12pt floor against the card's inner width — see the theme paragraph), and Regular 12pt for the centred attribution. There is no fourth size. The two-family pairing follows the "humanist body + period display chrome" convention — the body stays in dense-text-legible Renaissance serif while the chrome carries the period register.

    **Tried and rejected.** 18pt Roman numerals in the corners — replaced by rubricated lozenges because the numerals did not survive the playing-card index convention.
  - `cartograph` reuses the bundled **IM Fell English** chain (the Oxford-press silhouette `alchemy` already pulls from), but promotes the *Italic* to the primary body face — the period-accurate cartographic-italic register 17th-century mapmakers used for chart legends and place names — and switches the matched-phrase slot to upright Roman so the body-vs-accent split is roman/italic + colour rather than weight. Falls back through DejaVu Serif Italic before the Playfair chain so a missing install lands on a slanted serif rather than an upright one.
  - `vitrail` pairs **Liberation Serif** for the quote body, the cartouche-foot attribution, and the matched phrase with **Uncial Antiqua** (Astigmatic / Brian J. Bonislawsky, OFL — a single-weight ecclesiastical uncial display face modelled on early-medieval Insular majuscule hands) in the ornament slot for the rose-window Roman numeral only.

    **Liberation Serif** is a clean even-weight serif that stays crisp at the 15-30px cartouche sizes after `snap_image_to_palette`; the chain leads with the `liberation2/` then `liberation/` system paths and falls back through the default Playfair / DejaVu / Noto serif chains so a missing install still lands on a serif silhouette. The matched-phrase role reuses Liberation Serif Bold and earns differentiation from the violet-glass R+B accent colour alone.

    **Uncial Antiqua** shatters at byline sizes after palette snapping, so it is kept off the small-text chains. The ornament chain falls back through the bundled **MedievalSharp → UnifrakturMaguntia** "ritual hand" faces so a missing Uncial Antiqua install still lands on a medieval display silhouette rather than the Playfair default.
  - `questline` uses **Press Start 2P** (CodeMan38, OFL — the chunkiest of the bundle's three pixel faces, beside Pixelify Sans and Silkscreen, modelled on the 1980s Namco arcade typeface: a fixed 8×8 grid scaled up, every glyph a chunky monospace cell with no descenders, which PIL reports as ascent=size / descent=0).

    It carries the entire frame — body, matched-phrase accent, and the nameplate / footer chrome — since the pixel letterform *is* the theme. Single weight, so the matched-phrase ("bold") role reuses Regular and earns differentiation from the yellow accent colour alone (comic / dispatch / atomic discipline). Falls back through DejaVu / Liberation / Noto Sans before the Playfair chain — there is no other pixel face to fall to, so a missing install lands on a legible sans rather than the bitmap default.
  - `chrono` uses **Pixelify Sans** (eifetx / The Pixelify Sans Project Authors, OFL) — a *clean proportional* pixel sans (not a fixed grid like Press Start 2P) with lowercase descenders and a real weight axis, the refined 16-bit-era counterpart to questline's chunky 8-bit Press Start 2P. Variable font; Regular / Bold variation instances are pinned by name (same `set_variation_by_name` mechanism as Inter / Jost / Rubik / Antonio), so the matched phrase gets a genuine **Bold** weight on top of the yellow accent — unlike the single-weight pixel/display faces that lean on colour alone. Falls back through DejaVu / Liberation / Noto Sans before the Playfair chain.
  - `outrun` uses **Oxanium** (Sev Meyer, OFL) — a techno / cyberpunk display sans whose squared geometric terminals read as 1980s retro-future, the register a synthwave album sleeve or Outrun arcade marquee uses.

    Variable Weight axis; Regular / Bold variation instances are pinned by name (same `set_variation_by_name` mechanism as Inter / Jost / Rubik / Antonio / Pixelify Sans), so the matched phrase gets a real **Bold** weight step on top of the synthesised magenta accent. Distinct family from every other sans in the rotation (Inter / Archivo / Jost / Rubik / Antonio). The small Antonio-Bold author/title credit chrome is loaded inline (same condensed poster-credit face the other custom frames reuse). Falls back through DejaVu / Liberation / Noto Sans before the Playfair chain.

    **Tried and rejected.** **Orbitron**, the more famous canonical synthwave face — its very wide proportions shrink a dense 200-char literary quote to an unreadable size on a clock read across a room, while Oxanium's narrower geometry keeps long lines legible without losing the neon-future silhouette.
  - `letter` pairs **Dancing Script** (The Dancing Script Project Authors, OFL — a fluid pen-script with a real Weight axis, named Regular / Bold instances pinned via `set_variation_by_name`) for the body and matched phrase with **Pinyon Script** (The Pinyon Project Authors / Sorkin Type, OFL — a formal English-roundhand copperplate) confined to the ornament slot for the oversized opening / closing quote marks.

    **The body is the legible hand, not the copperplate.** Pinyon's hairlines shatter at body sizes on the panel after `snap_image_to_palette` (the same legibility caveat the blackletter / uncial themes carry), so the legible medium-weight script carries the dense body while the copperplate carries only the decorative flourish. Dancing Script's genuine Bold instance gives the matched time phrase a real weight step on top of the sealing-wax red accent.

    **Fallback.** The body falls back through DejaVu / Liberation Sans *Italic* (a slanted stand-in for a hand, same fallback discipline `chalkboard` uses for its Playwrite cursive) before the Playfair chain; the Pinyon ornament falls back to Dancing Script Bold first so the marks stay a pen hand even if the copperplate is missing.
  - `sampler` uses **Silkscreen** (Jason Kottke, OFL) — a crisp low-res fixed-grid bitmap pixel face (5px cap height at size 8) for the body, matched phrase, and ornament alike.

    **Glyphs become stitches.** The third pixel face in the bundle, but used differently from the other two: rather than rendering Silkscreen glyphs directly, the sampler frame draws text to a small mask in this face and stamps each set mask pixel as a scaled-up cross-stitch X, so the clean pixel grid *is* the counted-stitch chart. Ships Regular + Bold as two static TTFs, so the matched-phrase floss gets a true second weight on top of the red accent colour. Distinct from Press Start 2P (questline — chunky 8×8) and Pixelify Sans (chrono — proportional 16-bit).

    **Fallback.** The THEME_FONTS chains end at DejaVu / Liberation Sans then the Playfair chain as a safety net, but a missing Silkscreen install renders non-grid glyphs that read wrong as stitches, so the TTFs are shipped as package-data.
  - `anna_atkins` uses **Libre Caslon Text** (Pablo Impallari / Rodrigo Fuenzalida, OFL — a screen-legible revival of Caslon, the canonical English book face of the 18th–19th centuries and thus the printed-letterpress register of Atkins's own era and nation) for the white body and matched phrase.

    **Pairing.** The bundled **Pinyon Script** copperplate (reused from `letter`) sets the white handwritten Latin margin labels and the oversized quote-mark ornament — the "legible printed body + period copperplate caption" split that mirrors a real cyanotype plate (printed text + handwritten species name).

    **Instances.** Variable font with named instances Regular / Medium / SemiBold / Bold; the Regular / Bold slots are pinned via `set_variation_by_name` the same way Inter / Jost / Rubik / Antonio / Oxanium are (the matched phrase's Bold weight stacks on top of the sky-blue stipple reroute). Distinct from every other serif in the rotation (Playfair display-transitional, Bitter slab, Old Standard Didone, the Garamond-family EB Garamond / Cormorant / IM Fell, Cardo humanist) so the cyanotype plate reads as its own typographic register. Falls back through DejaVu / Liberation / Noto Serif before the Playfair chain.
  - `lieder` pairs **Alegreya** (Huerta Tipográfica, OFL — a humanist serif designed specifically for long-form literary setting, with a markedly calligraphic italic) for the sung lyrics with **Noto Music** (Google, OFL) for the clef and noteheads.

    **The roles follow an engraved score.** Alegreya carries all three text roles and the split follows a real engraved score's own convention rather than the usual regular/bold one: Regular for the lyric, Bold for the matched phrase (a genuine weight step stacked on top of the red voice ink), and *Italic* for every editorial mark — tempo, expression, composer credit, plate line. Roman-vs-italic is how a score separates plate from apparatus, which is why the italic fills the `ornament` slot here even though this theme has no oversized quote mark to ornament.

    **Distinct and safe.** Distinct from every other serif in the rotation (Playfair display-transitional, Bitter slab, Old Standard Didone, the Garamond-family EB Garamond / Cormorant / IM Fell, Cardo humanist, Libre Caslon) — the calligraphic angularity is what keeps it from reading as another Garamond. Falls back through the bundled EB Garamond and the system serifs before the Playfair chain so a missing install still lands on a book serif rather than a display face.

    **Noto Music is a symbol face, not a text face** — it covers the Unicode Musical Symbols block (U+1D100..U+1D1FF) plus the U+2669.. note characters and carries no Latin text — so it is loaded directly by the frame's painters (`load_font([NOTOMUSIC_REGULAR], size=gap * 4)`) rather than being wired into a `THEME_FONTS` chain, where a fallthrough to it would tofu every word on the page.
  - `izakaya` pairs **Quicksand** (Andrew Paglinawan, OFL — a rounded geometric sans whose monoline strokes and circular terminals are the closest thing in open type to a bent glass tube) for the neon lettering with the bundled **Yuji Boku** for the Japanese shop signs and the lantern's hour numeral.

    **The body face is deliberately *quiet*:** the glow is the effect, so the letterform underneath has to be a uniform-width tube and nothing more — a high-contrast serif blooms into something that reads as anything but neon. Distinct from every other sans in the rotation (Inter grotesque, Archivo grotesque, Jost geometric-constructed, Rubik rounded-but-squarer, Antonio condensed, Oxanium techno).

    **Shared Yuji Boku.** Shared with `kanagawa`, the rotation's only other Japanese register, and the two do not collide: kanagawa is a daylight woodblock seascape, izakaya a night street. Falls back through the system sans chain before the Playfair serifs.
  - `abyssal` uses **Lato** (Łukasz Dziedzic, OFL) — a humanist sans, a branch of the sans family nothing else in the rotation occupies: Inter and Archivo are grotesques, Jost / Rubik / Quicksand geometrics, Antonio condensed, Oxanium techno.

    **Chosen on legibility rather than period:** the body is white text over a dark gradient, which needs sturdy letterforms with open counters to hold at panel distance, and a high-contrast serif visibly thins out against the water. Regular body, Bold matched phrase — a real weight step underneath the mint bloom — and the Italic fills the ornament slot for the attribution, the one piece of text down here that is *not* a light source. Falls back through the system sans chain before the Playfair serifs.
  - `pulp` uses **Alfa Slab One** (Jose Manuel Sole, OFL) — a heavy slab in the mid-century advertising and paperback idiom.

    It is more condensed than the alternatives — a cover title is the dominant element and the corpus has long book titles, so the narrower face fits more of them on one line at a larger size. The frame's misregistration also specifically needs a *fat* face. **Space Mono** carries the small serial chrome (issue line, byline, corner banner) where a slab at 13-16 px would clog.

    **Tried and rejected.**
    - *The bundled display faces*, ruled out on register: **Rye** is Western, **Bangers** belongs to the theme pulp is meant to be distinct from, **Atomic Age** is the right era but the wrong object.
    - **Ultra**, the other candidate — less condensed, so it fits fewer long titles on one line.
  - `bakelite` reuses the bundled **Jost** chain (`bauhaus` / `pride` / `metro`), and the reason is mechanical rather than stylistic.

    **Why a monoline face.** `paint_neon_mask` reads a blurred glyph mask back as a halo density, so a high-contrast face haloes unevenly — thick stems flare while hairlines vanish — and the letterform stops reading as a lit tube. Jost's geometric, near-monoline strokes bloom evenly, and its flat terminals match the geometric numerals of the period's instrument faces. Sharing a family is safe here for the usual reason: a white-ground modernist grid, a flag, a route map and a CRT in a plastic slab are in no danger of being confused.

    **Space Mono** carries the letterspaced silkscreen legend — a readout's labels came off a stencil, not off its display face — the same chrome split `metro` uses.
  - `synoptic` uses **Space Mono** rather than the Archivo the issue suggested: a mono reads as instrument printout, which is the meteorological idiom. Shared with `nightvision` (black terminal) and `circuit` (green board); neither collides with a white-ground chart.
  - `pride` reuses the bundled **Jost** chain that `bauhaus` pulls from. Jost is Futura-adjacent, and geometric sans is the register of the 1970s poster and protest printing Gilbert Baker's flag came out of — a period serif would read as a book jacket laid over a flag. Sharing a family with `bauhaus` is safe: a white-ground minimal grid and a full-bleed flag are in no danger of being confused, and family sharing is well established here (EB Garamond, IM Fell, Cormorant, Cardo, Space Mono, Yuji Boku). Regular for the body, Bold for the matched phrase, so the time phrase gets a real weight step underneath the violet accent.
  - `vhs` uses **Antonio** — its first appearance as a *body* face rather than as poster chrome (`lcars`, `marquee`, `outrun` all use it for small condensed labels).

    **Why condensed.** A camcorder character generator draws in a tall narrow cell, and the condensed proportions are also what lets the chroma ghosts read: on a wide face a ±2 px separation is a fraction of the stem width and disappears into it, where on a narrow one it clears the stem and the letterform visibly comes apart. Variable Weight axis, so the matched phrase gets a real Bold step underneath the wider chroma offset. The OSD chrome loads **Pixelify Sans** inline — a pixel face, which is literally what an OSD character generator was. Falls back through DejaVu Sans Condensed before the Playfair chain.
  - `cardcatalog` reuses `dispatch`'s **Special Elite** for the card body: real catalogue cards were typed, and this is the bundle's typewriter. #210 asked whether the two themes stay distinct once they share a face — they do, because the *objects* differ completely (see the theme paragraph), and the shared face is the correct one for both. **Space Mono** carries the call number and the date-stamp faces, which on real cards came off a metal type wheel rather than a typewriter, so the register shift within the card is period-correct rather than arbitrary.
  - `nocturne` reuses **Cormorant Garamond** but pins the **Medium** instance for the body, not Regular: the theme blooms its text, and the Jost lesson says a high-contrast face haloes unevenly — thick stems flare while hairlines vanish. Medium thickens the hairlines enough to survive the faint cold halo (radius 3, cap 0.35) that Regular's strokes would shred; Bold carries the gold matched phrase. Sharing the family with `astrarium` is safe — a dial and a night river are in no danger of being confused.
  - `plaque` uses **Cinzel Decorative** one weight up across the chain — Bold for the body, Black for the matched phrase and ornament — because the relief pass needs stroke mass for a gold face to live in: Regular's hairlines left nothing after the rim light claimed the edges. Shared with `roman` / `tarot` on the usual object-difference grounds; the Trajan capitalis is THE letterform of cast bronze.
  - `daguerreotype` reuses **Libre Caslon Text** (`anna_atkins`'s face) for the quote stamped on the lid — the Caslon revival is the printed register of the 1850s studio, and the two photographic themes sharing a face makes the cyanotype/daguerreotype kinship literal. Variable instances Regular / Bold pinned as usual.
  - `photo` makes it four, and for the same reason: its caption card is the cream card `daguerreotype` once carried, and a neutral book serif is the right face for a caption that has to sit over a picture nobody has seen. The letterspaced mount chrome `autochrome` carries is deliberately absent — there is no process to name.
  - `autochrome` makes that three. Sharing the face across every photographic theme is the point rather than a shortcut: Caslon is the printed register all three plates would have been captioned in, and the objects are far enough apart (a blue photogram, a silvered case, a colour lantern slide) that no reader will confuse them. Variable instances Regular / Bold pinned; the letterspaced mount chrome loads from `META_FONT_BOLD_CANDIDATES` directly, the "metadata in chrome face, body in serif" convention `astrarium` established.
  - `betweenus` / `betweenus_dark` use **Fraunces** (Undercase Type, OFL) — the Between Us web app's typeface (its iOS build substitutes the system New York), a variable "soft" old-style serif with optical-size, softness, weight and wonk axes, paired with the bundled **Inter** for the pill and legend chrome (the app's UI sans).

    **Instances.** The named instances all sit at opsz 9, the sturdy text cut, which is what a stippled matched phrase on eInk wants. **The file's default axis instance is Black (wght 900)**, so every candidate pins an instance by name — the `bitter` hazard in the other direction: a missed variation call renders the body as a display black, not as hairlines (`TestBetweenUs::test_fraunces_instances_are_pinned_off_the_black_default` measures the pinned Regular against the raw default).

    **The matched phrase is the italic cut, not a bold.** Regular for the body; "say *what you want.*" is the app's own gesture, and the accent colour plus the roman/italic split carry the differentiation (the `cartograph` move) — Italic 400 in light, where it paints solid, and SemiBold Italic in dark so the amber stipple has stroke mass to live in. The italic chain falls back through the system serif italics before the Playfair bold chain so a missing install still lands on a slanted serif rather than an upright one.
  - `carcosa` uses **Almendra** (Ana Sanfelippo, OFL) — a calligraphic book face whose pen-cut wedges and faintly unsettled rhythm read as fin-de-siècle Decadent printing, the register of Chambers's 1895 first edition; it also sets the historical long-s `ſt` ligature, which suits a book that should not be read.

    Regular body, a real Bold for the matched phrase under the yellow, and **Almendra Display** (same designer and licence — a hollowed-stroke display cut) confined to the oversized quote marks. Distinct from every other serif in the bundle, none of which is calligraphic in this way. Each chain falls back through the bundled IM Fell English (the nearest period book face) before the system serifs and the Playfair chain.
  - `control` uses **Jost** (Owen Earl / indestructible type*, OFL) pinned to **Bold** for the body as well as the matched phrase, and **Archivo** (Omnibus-Type, OFL) for the sign chrome.

    **The game's faces.** The Game Font Library entry for the series (and Fonts In Use for the 2019 game) puts the title cards and logo in **ITC Avant Garde Gothic Bold** in tightly spaced caps and the interface in **Altered Grotesk**, a custom cut of Akzidenz-Grotesk (Basic Commercial in the 2019 game); neither is open.

    **Jost for the title card.** Jost is the bundle's geometric in the Futura / Avant Garde line and the nearest it gets to the title card, and the Astral Plane page *is* a title card, so it carries the quote. Bold for the body too, because a title card is heavy by definition, and the phrase earns its step from the Hiss red and its bloom rather than from weight (the `comic` / `dispatch` discipline).

    **Archivo for the interface.** Archivo is the bundle's Akzidenz-descended grotesque, so the Bureau sign — which is interface, not a title card — is set in it: Bold at 16 px for the name and Bold at 10 px for the sub-line and the Board's credits, loaded inline through the shared `draw_tracked` / `tracked_width` / `fit_text_to_width` helpers.

    **Tried and rejected.**
    - *Condensed grotesques the game never uses* — Antonio, then Oswald, on the belief that the title cards were Univers Condensed. Oswald stays as the fallback so a stripped install still lands on a heavy sans, ahead of the Playfair chain.
    - *Regular at 10 px on the sign* — shredded on the black sign after the snap.
  - `observation` uses **IBM Plex Mono** (IBM, OFL) — the station's own terminal face: a mono cut from an engineered grotesque, carrying the 1970s-IBM-console register the game's analogue retro-future interiors are built from.

    **Weights.** **Medium** for the body rather than Regular, because white strokes on black need the extra stem to survive the palette snap; **Bold** for the matched phrase under its tangerine halo; SemiBold for the HUD chrome.

    **Files.** Ships as three static TTFs (Medium / SemiBold / Bold) converted losslessly from IBM's published WOFF (`@ibm/plex-mono` on npm) back to sfnt — unmodified font data, so the Reserved Font Name is untouched. Distinct from Space Mono (`nightvision` / `synoptic` / `circuit`), which is its first fallback so a stripped install stays monospaced. Plex Mono has no `●` / `▸` glyphs, so the REC tally is drawn as an ellipse rather than typed.
  - `trisolaris` uses **Titillium Web** (Accademia di Belle Arti di Urbino, OFL) — a technical humanist sans drawn at a design school: cold and legible, the register of an observatory printout rather than a game HUD, and a branch nothing else in the bundle occupies (Inter / Archivo are grotesques, Jost / Rubik / Quicksand geometrics, Antonio / Oswald condensed, Oxanium techno, Lato the warmer humanist).

    Static Regular for the body; **SemiBold** rather than Bold for the matched phrase, which already carries a bloom — a full Bold clogs its counters once the halo closes in. Italic carries the attribution. The `三体` masthead reuses the bundled **Yuji Boku** (`kanagawa`'s / `izakaya`'s brush face, which covers both characters) and the header chrome loads **Space Mono** Bold inline. Falls back through the system sans chain before the Playfair serifs.
  - `biomech` uses **Spectral** (Production Type, OFL) — a cold, sharp-bracketed book serif with sturdy, hairline-free stems, which is what bone-white on black needs to survive the palette snap and a 2 px halo; the name's ghostliness suits Beksiński, the edge suits Giger.

    Static Medium for the body (the extra stem weight, the `observation` reasoning), SemiBold for the ember phrase, Medium Italic for the byline. **Grenze Gotisch** (Renata Polastri / Omnibus-Type, OFL) — a blackletter/roman hybrid with thorned, spurred terminals, variable Weight axis with named instances pinned — carries the sill plate's title and the ornament slot. Distinct from every serif in the bundle; the two faces are the first in it from either foundry. Falls back through the system serifs and the Unifraktur blackletter.
  - `codex` uses **Fondamento** (Brian J. Bonislawsky / Astigmatic, OFL) — a calligraphic book hand with the broad-nib modulation of an Italian chancery script.

    **Why a pen hand.** The *Codex Seraphinianus* is a *handwritten* encyclopedia, so its one deciphered passage is set in a pen hand rather than a typeset serif; Serafini is Italian, and a chancery hand is the Italian register of that. Regular for the body, **Italic** for the matched phrase — Fondamento ships no bold, so the step is roman/italic plus the red, the `cartograph` / `betweenus` move — and the Italic carries the blue byline.

    Distinct from every other face in the bundle: the other calligraphic book face, `carcosa`'s Almendra, is Decadent and angular where Fondamento is round and open. Falls back through the bundled IM Fell English before the system serifs. The asemic script and the numerals are not a font at all — see the theme paragraph.
  - `culture` and `orbital` use **Jura** (Daniel Johnson / The Jura Project Authors, OFL) — a humanist technical sans with calligraphic stroke endings: futurist without being a spaceship font, which suits a civilisation whose Minds are urbane rather than martial.

    **Weights.** `culture` pins **Medium** for the body (white strokes on black need the extra stem to survive the palette snap), `orbital` **SemiBold** (dark type on its white day card wants the heavier stem to hold Jura's hairline terminals); both take **Bold** for the matched phrase. **Share Tech Mono** (Carrois Type Design, OFL) carries the signal header, captions and card header — a narrow squared technical mono, loaded directly by the frames.

    **Files.** Jura ships as static Medium / SemiBold / Bold TTFs and Share Tech Mono as one Regular, both taken from the `@expo-google-fonts` npm packages (full Google Fonts cuts, unmodified); each chain falls back through DejaVu Sans before the Playfair chain.
  - `furies` uses **Libre Franklin** (Impallari Type / The Libre Franklin Project Authors, OFL) — a revival of Morris Fuller Benton's Franklin Gothic, the grotesque of mid-century museum wall text, which is what the quote is on this page. Variable Weight axis plus a separate italic file, instances pinned by name: **Medium** body (white strokes on black want the extra stem to survive the palette snap), **ExtraBold** matched phrase (the orange stipple and its dragged smear need stroke mass), **Medium Italic** attribution. Distinct from every other sans in the bundle (Inter, Archivo, Jost, Rubik, Antonio, Oxanium, Oswald, Quicksand, Lato); falls back through Inter before the Playfair chain.
  - `bosch` reuses **Grenze Gotisch** (Omnibus-Type, OFL), which `biomech` bundled for its plate label — here promoted to the whole banderole.

    **Why it holds as running text.** Its capitals are true textura while the lowercase keeps a roman's open counters, so it holds as running text at 16 px where the bundled UnifrakturMaguntia shreds: the nearest open face to the gothic book hand Bosch lettered his phylacteries in. Pinned by name: **Medium** body (a scroll's ink must survive the palette snap on cream), **Bold** for the rubricated phrase — a real weight step under the red — and **Black** for the ornament slot and the letter M on Hell's knife. Sharing with `biomech` is safe: a Giger wall and a Netherlandish altarpiece are in no danger of being confused. Falls back through the bundled IM Fell English before the system serifs.

    **Tried and rejected.** **Texturina** (same foundry): at every optical size its lowercase reads as a humanist serif, so the banderole stopped looking lettered.
  - `semiotic` uses **Barlow Condensed** (Jeremy Tribby, OFL) — a DIN-descended condensed grotesque, the register of industrial wayfinding and of the stencilled legends on Cobb's Nostromo signage; the first face in the bundle from that branch (Antonio and Oswald are display condensed cuts, not DIN). Four static TTFs: **Medium** body (black on the white placard), **Bold** for the red matched phrase and the featured sign's name, **SemiBold** for the header and attribution. Falls back through Oswald before the system sans.
  - `atropos` uses **Saira** (Omnibus-Type, OFL) for the translation and **Michroma** (Vernon Adams / The Michroma Project Authors, OFL) for the HUD.

    **The game's faces.** The Game Font Library lists *Returnal*'s faces as **Erbaum** (Fonderia Inhouse Type) for running text, **Decima Pro** (TipografiaRamis) as the secondary face and **Kellion** (Harvester Type) for the title and logo; all three are commercial, so the theme sets the nearest open face in each register.

    **Saira** is a squared technical grotesque with a text-sized lowercase, the closest open type gets to Erbaum: a variable font on weight and width whose default instance is **Thin**, so every candidate pins a name — Regular for the white body, SemiBold for the matched phrase (distinguished mainly by its tangerine bloom), Medium at 13 px for the cipher's source line.

    **Michroma** — wide, squared, Eurostile-descended — is the nearest open face to Kellion and keeps the biome title, the cycle counter, the translation-frame labels and the readouts; it loses its counters below 10 px, so no chrome is set smaller.

    **Tried and rejected.** Michroma for the body as well — a display face the game never uses for prose. It is now the body's fallback, ahead of the system sans.
  - `expedition` uses the game's own faces, all three from Google Fonts under the OFL.

    **IM Fell Double Pica** (Igino Marini's digitisation of the seventeenth-century Fell types at the Double Pica size) is what *Clair Obscur: Expedition 33* sets its UI text in: the roman carries the white body on the dusk, and since the face has no bold the matched phrase takes the italic and is told apart by its paint. Its sibling IM Fell English (`alchemy` / `cartograph`) is the fallback, so a wheel missing the new file still renders a Fell.

    **Bebas Neue** (Dharma Type) is what the game sets its UI numbers in: the painted hour is set in it before it is brushed rough — a tall narrow stroke is what the Paintress's number looks like on the slab — and it carries every label (`CLAIR OBSCUR`, the byline, the credo), where solid-yellow condensed caps at 15–17 px survive the panel that a stippled serif at that size would not. Antonio, the bundle's other condensed caps, is its fallback.

    **Cinzel Decorative Bold** (`roman` / `tarot` / `plaque`), the poster's lettering, sets the `EXPEDITION 33` wordmark.
  - `witcher` uses **Barlow Condensed** (Jeremy Tribby, OFL) for the entry and **Archivo Narrow** (Omnibus-Type, OFL) for the labels.

    **The game's faces.** The Game Font Library lists *The Witcher 3*'s faces as **PF DIN Text Condensed** (Parachute) for the interface and **Bell Gothic Bold** (Bitstream) for the `WILD HUNT` logotype; neither is open.

    **Barlow Condensed** is the bundle's DIN-descended condensed grotesque (shared with `semiotic`) and the nearest open face to PF DIN Text Condensed: Bold for the entry title, Medium for the black body on cream, Bold for the matched phrase, which is told apart by its tangerine as much as its weight.

    **Archivo Narrow** — the narrow cut of Archivo, variable on weight and pinned by instance — is the nearest open face to Bell Gothic and sets `BESTIARY`, the entry number, `MEDITATION`, `SUSCEPTIBLE TO`, the attribution and the `THE WITCHER · WILD HUNT` mark, all solid ink at 12–15 px. Falls back through Archivo before the system faces.
  - `hades` uses the game's faces where they are open, from the Game Font Library's *Hades 2* sheet: **Caesar Dressing** (Open Window, OFL), **Spectral** / **Spectral SC** (Production Type, OFL) and **Lato** (Łukasz Dziedzic, OFL); its main interface face, **P22 Underground SC** (P22 Type Foundry — Johnston's London Underground letter), and **Mrs. Eaves** (Emigre) are commercial.

    **Caesar Dressing** is the brushed Greek-inscription display face the game sets its title cards and god names in: here the author's name, the boon's title, in yellow with a one-pixel red stroke so the letters read as beaten gold on the panel rather than flat, and the `HADES II` wordmark. Capitals only, so the name is uppercased; Cinzel Decorative is the fallback.

    **Spectral** is the serif the game sets its codex and boon text in: **Medium** for the white body on the card's midnight, **SemiBold** for the matched phrase, which is told apart by its gold as much as its weight; Alegreya, the bundle's other literary serif, is the fallback. **Spectral SC** — new to the bundle, Medium only — sets the book's title at the foot in the small caps the game's codex headings use. Lato Bold sets the rarity label.

    **Hammersmith One** (Sorkin Type, OFL) — new to the bundle, one static Regular — is the open Johnston and the nearest face to P22 Underground; it sets the `THE CROSSROADS` label and `CHRONOS`, tracked, in solid ink (small text in a stipple shreds). Mrs. Eaves has no open sibling and no role on the card. Three new font directories ship with their OFL licence files.
  - `expanse` uses **Barlow** (Jeremy Tribby, OFL) for the transmission and the sender, the bundle's **Barlow Condensed** for the tracked labels and **Share Tech Mono** (Carrois Apostrophe, OFL) for the readouts and the command line.

    **Barlow for DIN Pro.** The show's screen graphics are set in a modified **DIN Pro** (HUDS+GUIS), which is commercial; Barlow is the Google Fonts DIN descendant — a low-contrast grotesque drawn from Californian signage with DIN's squared bowls and open apertures. The body takes Barlow Regular in white at up to 30 px, the matched phrase Barlow SemiBold, told apart by its orange rather than by a weight the stipple would blur, and the sender Barlow Bold capitals tracked two pixels.

    **Labels and readouts.** The labels are Barlow Condensed Medium at 11–14 px, solid white or yellow (small text in a stipple shreds), and the readouts are Share Tech Mono because the console's values and its prompt line read as a terminal, not as signage. Barlow is a new font directory (four static weights) and ships with its OFL licence file.
  - `beksinski` uses **Old Standard TT** (Alexey Kryukov, OFL), already bundled for `newsprint`. Beksiński set no type, so the face is the period's rather than the painter's: Old Standard is the Didone of Central and Eastern European book printing through the twentieth century, the letter a Polish novel of 1970 was set in.

    Regular sets the black body in the haze at up to 32 px — a Didone's hairlines hold on the light W+Y ground where they would shred on a dark one — Bold the matched phrase in solid red, the panel's dried-blood ink and the only saturated colour on the page, and Regular again the bone-white byline on the plain, which the craquelure keeps out of. No new font directory.
  - `goya` uses **Libre Baskerville** (Impallari Type, OFL), new to the bundle as two variable files — roman and italic, default instances Regular and Italic, every candidate still pinned by name.

    **The register.** The Black Paintings carry no lettering, so the register is the printing of their own decade in Madrid: the Imprenta Real's transitional romans (Pradell, Gil), cut in the Baskerville / Didot school that Bodoni — whose patron was the Spanish diplomat José Nicolás de Azara — brought to its finish.

    **Why it holds on ochre.** Libre Baskerville is Benton's 1941 ATF Baskerville redrawn for screen body text, with a taller x-height, wider counters and *less* contrast than the original, which is what lets a serif hold on a dithered ochre ground — Playfair's and Old Standard's hairlines shred in the stipple. Regular for the body, Bold for the matched phrase, SemiBold and Italic for the label's artist and title lines, Regular at 11 px for its medium and inventory lines, in solid black on solid white. Libre Caslon, the bundle's other low-contrast old-style, is the fallback.
  - `hal` uses **Jost** (Owen Earl, OFL) for the body and the byline and **Michroma** (Vernon Adams, OFL) for every label, both already bundled.

    The film's screens and labels set their capitals in Microgramma / Eurostile, the wide squared face of 1960s futurism, and its signage in Futura; Michroma is the open Microgramma and Jost the open Futura, so each register gets the face the film used rather than a stand-in for it. Jost is a variable font whose default instance is Regular; the body pins Regular, the matched phrase Bold, the byline Medium. Michroma ships as one static Regular — it sets the header mnemonic at 38 px, the nameplate and the eleven-pixel tile labels, which hold on the panel because every one is a solid ink on a solid ink.
  - `lumon` uses **Montserrat** (Julieta Ulanovsky, OFL), new to the bundle as one variable roman file (default instance Regular, every candidate pinned by name), with the bundle's **Inter** for the file name, the completion and the byline and **Michroma** for the wordmark.

    **The show's faces.** Fonts In Use records the show's faces as Manifold Extended CF (the Lumon wordmark, drawn after Microgramma), Forma DJR (the house neo-grotesque), Input Sans and Optima, and the fan consensus puts the MDR number grid in a Gotham-like geometric sans — all commercial.

    **Open equivalents.** Each register takes the open face in the same family: Montserrat, drawn in the Buenos Aires-signage register Gotham came from, for the digits (Medium at 17 px, Bold at 23 px for the scary cluster) and the body (Regular at up to 30 px, Bold for the matched phrase in yellow); Inter, the open neo-grotesque, for Forma DJR; Michroma for Manifold Extended.
  - `dsky` uses **Special Elite** (Astigmatic, Apache 2.0), already bundled for `dispatch` and `cardcatalog`, for the quote and the byline — the quote is typed on a flight-plan card, and the Apollo flight plans were cut on a typewriter; one weight, so the matched phrase is told apart by its red ink — and **Jost** (Owen Earl, OFL) Medium for every legend on the unit: NASA silkscreened the Apollo panels in Futura Demi, so the open Futura is the right face rather than a stand-in, at 9–10 px for the lamp words and the key caps, which hold because every one is solid white on near-black. The digits are not type at all but seven-segment strokes.
  - `oblivion` uses **Exo 2** (Natanael Gama, OFL), already bundled as `saros`'s fallback.

    The film's screen graphics are set in **Blender** (Nik Thoenen, Gestalten 2003; commercial), an angular geometric sans with squared bowls, which UI team member Navarro Parker named as the Oblivion UI face; Exo 2 is the bundle's open face in that family, a geometric techno sans with the same squared rounds. Variable on weight with a Thin default, so every candidate pins a name: **Light** for the body — the film's typographic identity is that lightness, and a Light stem survives here only because it is solid black on the panel's white, where a stipple would shred it — Medium for the matched phrase in red, Regular at 10–22 px tracked for the header, the bearing labels and the rig legends.
  - `yorha` uses **EB Garamond** (Georg Duffner / Octavio Pardo, OFL), already bundled for `gothic`. Automata's interface is set in a refined classical serif — the game's most imitated typographic feature — that has never been identified under a commercial name (custom or unreleased); the free faces usually offered as its nearest matches are EB Garamond and Cormorant, and of the two EB Garamond's larger x-height and sturdier hairlines are what let a 16 px menu row hold on the panel. Regular for the body at up to 34 px, the menu rows, the counter and the author; Bold for the matched phrase (white on its black box), the header and the book's title.
  - `hitchhiker` uses **Michroma** (Vernon Adams, OFL), already bundled, for every register: the series' lettering was drawn by hand in the square-shouldered monoline manner of Microgramma, which Michroma is the open descendant of. One static weight, so the matched phrase is yellow rather than bold; the body sets at up to 26 px with a generous 1.5 leading, because a wide face at a narrow leading reads as a block.
  - `escritoire` uses **Dancing Script** (Pablo Impallari, OFL), already bundled for `letter`, pinned Regular for the body and Bold for the matched phrase and the signature. Pinyon Script's copperplate is closer to a real letter, but its hairlines shred once the warp has shrunk the far lines; Dancing Script's even, rounded stroke survives the threshold.
  - `bladerunner` uses **Barlow Condensed** (Jeremy Tribby, OFL), already bundled, Medium for the white body, Bold for the matched phrase, SemiBold for every tracked label and the baseline's prompts (stepped down to 10 px where a word would touch its cell), and **Share Tech Mono** for the base pairs and the magnified serial. The film's interface type is custom; a narrow, low-contrast grotesque in tracked capitals is the register it reads in.
  - `lasvegas` uses **Barlow** (Jeremy Tribby, OFL), already bundled for `expanse`, Medium for the white body on the black pane (a Regular stem thins once the panel's white bleeds into the black) and Bold for the matched phrase; **Barlow Condensed** for the tracked chrome, tags and byline; and **Share Tech Mono** for the base pairs. Territory Studio set the film's interfaces in plain, low-contrast grotesques, and Barlow's slightly squared curves sit between that and a highway sign.
  - `traumateam` uses **Oxanium** (Sev Meyer, OFL), already bundled for `outrun`, pinned Regular for the body, Bold for the matched phrase and SemiBold / Medium for the band, the byline and the vitals label. The game's interface faces are commercial; Oxanium's squared bowls sit in the same register and stay sturdy in white on black at the byline's size.
  - `redacted` uses **Special Elite** (Astigmatic, Apache 2.0), already bundled for `dispatch`, for the typed body, the form values and the source line. It ships one weight, so the matched phrase differs by colour alone, the bichrome-ribbon shift `dispatch` uses. The letterhead, the form labels and the stamp are **Archivo** Bold, the Akzidenz-Grotesk stand-in `control`'s sign already uses, so the two *Control* themes share a house face.
  - `gantry` reads **Lumen** (Font Studio, OFL) as dot bitmaps rather than setting it as type: a variable LED dot-matrix face whose glyphs are a 5×7 matrix on a grid a tenth of an em apart, so its dots can be sampled onto the sign's own lattice at any pitch (see the theme paragraph). It is pinned to the **Bold** instance, whose larger dots sit squarely over every sampled grid centre; the Weight and Roundness axes change only the shape of each dot, which the theme draws itself. The face lacks the curly quotes, the em dash, `£` and `œ`; the first two go through `apply_theme_glyph_fallbacks`, the rest through `_GANTRY_STANDINS`. The guide sign is **Barlow Condensed** SemiBold and Bold, the nearest bundled face to Highway Gothic, already loaded by `lasvegas`.
  - `platform` uses **Lumen**'s Round instances (Font Studio, OFL), the face `gantry` bundled: **Round Medium** for the body and the furniture, **Round Bold** for the matched phrase, the destination and the clock. As in `gantry` the glyphs are read from the face's dot grid rather than rasterised, but here each instance's own dot size is kept (see the theme paragraph), so the two weights are what the panel shows. No second face: the whole board is one dot-matrix family, as a real one is.
  - `splitflap` uses **Bebas Neue** (Dharma Type, OFL), already bundled, at a 21 px cap height: an all-caps condensed grotesque, the register of the printed flaps on a split-flap board. One weight, so the matched phrase is set apart by its yellow tile, not a heavier face.
  - `saros` uses **Saira** (Omnibus-Type, OFL) for the body, **Orbitron** (Matt McInerney, OFL) for the wordmark and **Michroma** (Vernon Adams, OFL) for the status chrome.

    **The game's faces.** The Game Font Library lists *Saros*'s faces as **Arame** (dmtr.org) as the main display face, **Tamba Sans** (Dharma Type) for text and **Korataki** (Typodermic) as the tertiary face; none is open, so each register gets the nearest open face.

    **Saira** — a squarish grotesque, the closest open type gets to Tamba Sans — is a variable font whose default instance is **Thin**, so every candidate pins a name: Regular for the white body on black, **SemiBold** rather than Bold for the matched phrase, which carries a bloom that would close a Bold's counters (the `trisolaris` lesson), the Italic file for the byline.

    **Chrome.** Orbitron, a squared geometric display sans pinned Bold, stands in for Arame on the `SAROS` wordmark at 22 px; Michroma, wide and squared, stands in for Korataki on the 10 px tracked status chrome, checked glyph-by-glyph by `TestSarosFrame::test_chrome_face_carries_every_glyph_it_sets`. Exo 2, the first revision's body face, is the fallback before the system sans.
- **Layouts.** Three named layouts (`hero` ≤90 chars, `standard` ≤170, `dense` otherwise) each define their own `max_width`, `quote_height`, font size range, line-height multiplier, and quote-mark sizing. See the `LAYOUTS` dict.
- **Bold time phrase.** `resolve_display_match` tries to grow a multi-word time phrase ("five minutes past", etc.) inside the display text, then `tokenize_quote`/`wrap_styled_text` render it in bold + accent color while keeping word wrap correct across the bold boundary.

    **Breaks only at whitespace.** `wrap_styled_text` regroups the regular / bold / regular segments into words first, gluing pieces across a segment seam that has no space at it, so `door (at a quarter to seven) with` can never end a line on `seven` and open the next with a bare `)` (the dangling parenthetical seen on the panel for the *Portrait of a Lady* row), nor strand an opening `(` / `"` at a line end. Each glued piece keeps its own style, so `seven)` still paints `seven` bold and `)` regular; `tests/test_render_quote.py::TestWrapStyledTextNoBreakInsideWord` sweeps the wrap width to pin it.
- **Text cleanup.** `strip_underscore_emphasis` drops Gutenberg's `_emphasis_` markers and `normalize_dashes` converts bare `--` to em-dashes before layout.
- **Missing-glyph fallback.** PIL falls back per font file, not per glyph, so a character the theme's face lacks renders as a `.notdef` box.

    **The swap.** `render` therefore first calls `apply_theme_glyph_fallbacks`, which swaps characters the theme's `quote_regular` / `quote_bold` fonts lack for ASCII stand-ins from `GLYPH_FALLBACKS` (… → `...`, ′ → `'`, ″ → `''`, em dash → spaced hyphen, …), applied identically to quote, matched phrase, author and title so the matched phrase still resolves.

    **Detection.** A glyph counts as present unless `font_has_glyph` finds it renders identically to U+10FFFF (a codepoint no font maps, so PIL draws the face's `.notdef`), memoised per font file and face. A theme whose fonts carry every glyph gets its row back as the same object, which is why the golden fixtures are unaffected; today it catches the primes some forty faces lack. Chrome text that a custom frame draws in its own inline-loaded fonts is covered only where that text also passes through the theme's body or bold face.
- **Fit loop.** `fit_quote` shrinks the quote font in 2pt steps from the layout's `font_max` down to `font_min` until all lines fit within `quote_height`.
- **Justification.** Non-last lines are fully justified by distributing leftover slack across inter-word spaces — but only when slack is ≤25% of the measure (a looser line stays ragged on its own).

    **Decided per block.** The rest is decided **per block** by `justify_flags`: if any eligible line has fewer than three gaps or would stretch each gap past 0.45 em of the body size, every line is set ragged-right. Bounding the slack alone is not enough: a three-word hero line would take 75 px per gap, and a block could come out half justified and half ragged, which reads as a mistake.

    **Always ragged.** `_THEMES_RAGGED_RIGHT` (`nightvision`, `circuit`, `dispatch`, `marker`, `chalkboard`, `placard`, `kanagawa`) is always ragged: a terminal, a typewriter and a hand never justified a line.
- **Widow control.** `fit_quote_balanced` wraps the quote and, when the last line would be one word or under 30% of the measure, re-wraps at the same size on measures down to 70% of the layout's, then at 2 px smaller sizes down to 80% of the fitted size, taking the first wrap with no widow that neither adds a line nor leaves a body line under 55% full (or shorter than the unbalanced wrap's shortest). The hour is the point of the quote; it should not sit alone on the last line. The block justifies against the measure it was wrapped to (`wrap_width`), so a balanced block hangs a little short of the right margin.
- **Hanging marks and byline.** The oversized opening mark hangs at a fixed x (`SIDE_MARGIN + 18`) with its lower two thirds level with the first line, so on the standard and dense measures it runs under the first word. That is a deliberate style choice. Author and title sizes still scale with the body but floor at 18 / 16 px instead of 13, so the byline reads from across the room.

    **Tried and rejected.** Right-aligning the mark to the text edge and shrinking it to fit the gutter — reverted, because the small, timid marks lost the gesture.
- **Shared mount furniture.** `paint_mount_card`, `draw_centred_styled_lines` and `draw_truncated_centred_byline` were extracted when `daguerreotype` and `autochrome` both put the quote on a cream card beside the plate and had the card stock, the centred styled-line loop and the truncating byline verbatim identical between them (the extraction was behaviour-preserving). Neither carries a card any more, since the three photographic themes were given distinct compositions, so `photo` is the card's one user; the styled-line loop and the byline are still shared by all three.

    **Not folded in.** The line loop is worth naming because three *other* frames — `pulp`, `vhs`, plus `wrap_quote_into_masks` — carry their own near copies, differing in where the block is anchored, whether it draws to a mask or a canvas, and how the matched phrase is coloured. Those are deliberately **not** folded in: unpicking them touches golden fixtures for no behaviour change. Another copy was not worth adding either, which is the line the extraction draws.
- **Modes.** `--mode debug` (default) draws a top-right `DEBUG MODE` banner (rendered in sans-bold to match the footer strip, in the theme's accent color) plus a centered bottom strip (`HH:MM · bucket[ → resolved] · layout X · quality N · id source:Lline`) separated from the quote block by a dotted horizontal rule. `--mode production` hides all of that for a clean appliance look.
- **Outputs.** `--output` defaults to `output/current.png` (the same stable filename `run_clock.py` passes explicitly), so repeated ad-hoc CLI invocations overwrite one file instead of leaking up to 1440 `render-HHMM.png` siblings across a day. Pass an explicit path when you want a persistent per-time artifact.
